# Anvil session log

Newest entry last. Each entry: what was done, decisions, and "next up" for the following session.

## Session 1 — 2026-09-21

**Context.** Scheduled task `daily-code-limit`. The user rejected two earlier directions this session (a scripting language, then a roguelike) and asked for: a CAD program that is best for Claude's own use to draw and create 3D models, with a UI whose features sit in the same places as SolidWorks. A parallel Claude session joined mid-way and owns `tests/`, `examples/` and `README.md`.

**Done.**
- Wrote the whole application from scratch as static files (no build step; three.js r128 from cdnjs):
  `csg.js` (BSP CSG), `kernel.js` (sketches, features, rebuild, meshing, mass props, STL/DXF, script builder), `icons.js`, `style.css`, `index.html`, `ui-core.js` (scene, camera, picking, highlights, edges), `ui-sketch.js` (mouse, sketch tools), `ui-commands.js` (PropertyManager + commands), `ui-panels.js` (ribbon, menus, tree, HUD, modal), `ui-app.js` (task pane, script console, files, exports, shortcuts, `window.anvil`, boot).
- `serve.ps1` + `.claude/launch.json` serve the folder on http://localhost:8765 (PowerShell HttpListener, since there is no node/python).
- Verified in the browser pane: loads with no console errors, sample bracket (12 features) rebuilds in ~17–30 ms, face selection highlights, layout correct at 1024 px after fixing the ribbon overflow and the initial fit timing.

**Decisions.**
- Model = feature list JSON; the script API is a builder over that list and `generateScript` regenerates code from the tree, so both representations stay interchangeable.
- Sketch frames follow SolidWorks: Front (x=X, y=Y, n=Z), Top (x=X, y=-Z, n=Y), Right (x=-Z, y=Y, n=X).
- Holes in profiles are subtracted as slightly over-long prisms to avoid coplanar-cap artifacts.
- Edges are drawn from kernel face outlines (T-junction aware) with tangent edges (< 22°) hidden, not from `THREE.EdgesGeometry`.
- Polygon tags (`shared`) carry the owning feature id through CSG so selecting a feature highlights its faces and double-clicking a face opens its feature.
- No fillet/chamfer/shell yet: they need edge selection and offset geometry (Phase 2).

**Known gaps / bugs to check first next session.**
- Sketch tools were only exercised by reading the code, not by clicking through in the browser; test Line/Rect/Circle drawing, entity PM editing, exit sketch → extrude.
- Revolve: holes inside revolved profiles are revolved at exactly the same radius as the outer loop caps; coplanar end caps may leave slivers for partial revolves.
- Circular pattern preview uses the source's cached tool; for patterns-of-patterns the tool exists but `isCut` follows only one level.
- `featureFamily` in ui-core returns only the feature itself; pattern/mirror copies keep the source's tag, so selecting a pattern highlights nothing (selecting the source highlights all copies).
- Downloads are inert inside the claude.ai artifact sandbox; the export dialogs offer Copy as the fallback.
- **Published artifact:** https://claude.ai/artifact/Dhex98h8xugn5w2RXraAPS (multi-file; republish with `url` from any session, `root: anvil`, files map of style.css, csg.js, kernel.js, icons.js, examples/examples.js, ui-*.js).
- Click-tested in the browser pane: Sketch → Top plane → Circle tool → Exit Sketch → Extruded Boss/Base PM (depth edit, yellow preview, ✓ commit) all work; the parallel session's kernel test page passes 101/101 after two kernel fixes it found (generateScript round-trip vars, slot tangent vertices).

**Later in session 1 (same day).**
- Edge selection (kernel `bodyEdges` with interior directions from outline winding, tangent chains; viewport `pickEdge` with occlusion test) and **Fillet / Chamfer** features with mitred caps at shared vertices (`p.fillet(r, [[x,y,z]])`, `p.chamfer(d, [...])`). Verified in the browser: hole-rim chamfer as one 31-edge chain, four top edges filleted in one feature, clean corners.
- **Sheet metal**: `p.sheet(T,R,K)`, `baseFlange`, **Edge Flange** feature (bend + wall swept along picked edges, reverse side), **Flatten** viewer (SVG blank with bend centre lines, UP/DOWN labels, BA = angle·(R+K·T)) and **Flat to DXF** (OUTLINE/HOLES/BEND/NOTES). Verified: 100×60 base with two flanges → 120.37 × 85.37 blank.
- Convexity bug found by testing (centroid test failed on faces with holes) fixed by using outline winding.

- Parallel session click-tested the UI (all core flows pass) and reported 8 issues; all fixed: cut defaults (Through All + Reverse for face sketches, "cut removed no material" warning), modal confirm/prompt replacing blocked native dialogs, face-first picking with the Sketch PM, empty new sketches discarded, preview through-all clamped, status text on exit, narrow-window layout, export textarea/hole rounding cosmetics.
- Sketch tools added: Convert Entities, Offset Entities (PM), Sketch Fillet (PM + click a corner). Kernel `offsetLoop`, `filletPolyline`.
- Testing note: in the browser pane, navigating to the same localhost URL can serve stale scripts; use `location.reload()` via javascript_tool. Never name a REPL variable `top` (it is window.top).

**Next up.**
1. Sweep and loft features (kernel + PMs), then wire the greyed Swept/Lofted ribbon buttons.
2. Phase 2: Convert Entities / Offset Entities in sketches on faces, sketch fillet, Shell, sweep/loft, driven dimensions display.
3. Sheet metal: Material Inside placement, flanges on flanges in the flat pattern, holes on flange walls.
4. Performance: incremental rebuild (cache tools for unchanged prefix), Web Worker.

## Session 2 — 2026-09-22

**Context.** Scheduled task `daily-code-limit`, unattended. Five interactive peer sessions were active on the folder at the same time (one had just added `tests/ui-smoke.html`, three pattern tests and README text); I messaged all of them with the file split (this session: `kernel.js`, `ui-*.js`; helpers: `tests/`, `examples/`, `README.md`) and only touched their files additively.

**Found on arrival.** Sweep and loft were already implemented in the kernel and UI at the end of session 1 but never verified, tested, documented or logged (the log's "next up" was stale). Verified them headlessly against analytic volumes (straight tube, mitred L, 3D-point path, line+arc path, swept cut, square frustum 7000 exact, lofted cut) and by clicking through the Swept/Lofted PMs.

**Done.**
- Sweep polish: path sketches no longer raise the "open contour" warning (they are open by design); path entities that do not connect produce a warning on the sweep instead of vanishing silently; the Sweep PM pairs the closed sketch as profile and the open one as path automatically.
- **Shell** feature (`p.shell(t, [[x,y,z]…])`, feature `{type:'shell', thickness, faces:[{at}]}`): `Kernel.resolveFaces` (point → mesh face by plane distance + outline containment), `Kernel.buildShell` builds the inner solid as body − ⋃(slab behind each kept face) ∪ extensions through removed faces, then body − inner. Slab outlines are grown per edge by T·tan(φ/2) (φ = dihedral angle to the neighbour, via `offsetLoopVar`), which is exactly where the two offset planes meet: sharp offset corners at concave edges, no over-cut at tangent facets (the uniform-T version left a star pattern around polygonal holes). Exact results: closed box 15744, open box 11712, two open faces 11280, L block 19264; cup and holed box within 0.02 %. Shell PM: click faces to remove (highlighted), thickness, red cavity preview; editing an existing shell previews on the body rebuilt up to that feature (`bodyBefore`). Ribbon Shell button, Insert menu, tree icon and description.
- `unionTree` (balanced pairwise union) for shell slabs and sweep segments: bushing shell 4.1 s → 0.39 s, cup 110 → 16 ms. NOT used for patterns: a tree union of coincident copies loses material (three pattern tests fail with it).
- Kernel bug fixed (found by the helper session's new tests): a pattern's tool holds only its copies, so a pattern/mirror of a pattern missed the seed instance (3 holes instead of 4). `familyTool` now unions the seed recursively for `lpattern`/`cpattern`/feature `mirror` sources.
- Tests added to `tests/kernel-tests.html` (groups `sweep`, `loft`, `shell`: 19 tests) — 169/169 pass; `tests/ui-smoke.html` 16/16. Examples added: Mug (shell + swept handle), Funnel (loft + shell open both ends), Handlebar (3D-point sweep with a swept-cut bore). README: Part methods, feature JSON, kernel API, ribbon/menu lists, known limitations.
- Status bar says "updated" instead of "created" when a feature edit is committed.

**Notes.**
- The browser-pane `key: Return` does not reach the page's keydown handler; click the ✓ button (`find "OK (Enter)"`) to accept a PM when driving the UI from tools. Enter works for real users (ui-app keydown + number inputs).
- Shell on a revolved part (~300 faces) takes ~0.4 s; on a loft-with-spout funnel ~0.55 s. Fine interactively, but the PM recomputes the preview on every keystroke in the thickness field.
- Editing an existing Shell cannot re-pick removed faces (they no longer exist on the displayed body); delete and recreate instead.

**Next up.**
1. Draft on extrudes (tapered prism; the greyed Draft button), then Rib.
2. Sketch tools: Trim, Mirror Entities, Linear Sketch Pattern; driven dimensions display.
3. Sheet metal: Material Inside placement, flanges on flanges in the flat pattern, holes on flange walls.
4. Performance: cache tools for an unchanged feature prefix; Web Worker rebuilds.

**Later in session 2 (continued on request).**
- **Draft on extrudes**: `extrude(d, { draft: deg, draftOutward })` / feature fields `draft`, `draftOut`. `taperedPrism` offsets the section by depth·tan(angle) at each end (mitred `offsetLoop`, so side quads stay planar); holes offset the other way (they widen as material narrows); mid-plane and direction-2 ranges are split at the sketch plane so the taper starts there on both sides. Collapse is detected by edge reversal (a square offset past its centre keeps a positive area) and reported as "Draft angle is too large for this depth". Draft group (on/angle/outward) in the Boss/Cut-Extrude PM, tree description shows the angle. 4 tests (frustum volumes exact to Float32 precision, outward, mid-plane, drafted cut, boss with widening hole, error, round-trip) — 173/173 pass; committed from the PM with the exact expected volume.
- Note for tests: mesh positions are Float32, so volume checks on non-integer geometry need `nearRel(…, 1e-6)`, not an absolute 1e-6.
- The ribbon "Draft" button stays greyed: SolidWorks' Draft feature drafts existing faces about a neutral plane, which is a different (harder) operation than the extrude option.

**Later in session 2 (sketch tools).**
- **Trim Entities** (`Kernel.trimEntity`, `SketchBuilder.trim(i, u, v)`): entities are decomposed into exact primitives (segments and circular arcs; slots explode into 2 lines + 2 arcs, rects/polygons become polylines), intersections are analytic (seg–seg, seg–arc, arc–arc), and the span of the clicked primitive between its neighbouring intersections is removed: lines split, arcs split, a full circle becomes the remaining arc, a closed polyline opens, an entity nothing crosses is deleted. PM "Trim Entities" (click to trim, repeat, ✓ to finish); `onSketchPoint` now also receives the screen coordinates so the command can use `UI.pickEntity`.
- **Mirror Entities** (`Kernel.mirrorAxis/mirrorEntity`, `SketchBuilder.mirrorEntities(axis, {entities, copy})`): axes = sketch X/Y or a line entity; arcs reflect as 2φ − a with the sweep re-ordered, regular polygons keep their type (rot' = 2φ − rot), rects/crects keep their type for axis-aligned mirrors and become polylines otherwise. PM previews the copies as temp entities; mirrors the selected entity or all.
- **Linear Sketch Pattern** (`Kernel.translateEntity`, `SketchBuilder.patternEntities({dx, dy, count, dx2, dy2, count2})`): PM with angle/spacing/instances for two directions, temp preview.
- Kernel: `meshFromBody` now keeps `positions64` (Float64) and `massProps` uses it, so volumes are exact in double precision instead of carrying the Float32 render error (about 4e-8 relative) — this made the helper session's absolute-tolerance draft tests pass.
- Coordination: the helper session wrote its own `draft` test group with a `frustum` helper that shadowed mine (function declarations hoist), so I removed my draft group and kept theirs. Tests: 185/185; sketch tools have 7 tests in the `sketch tools` group.

**Later in session 2 (sheet metal).**
- **Flat pattern rewritten as a recursive unfold.** The base face (largest face on the +n side of the base sketch plane) is mapped into the sketch frame; each flange attached to an unfolded face adds a bend strip of length BA beyond its edge and then unfolds its wall face on the tracked side of the sheet (inner radius when the fold comes toward that side, outer radius otherwise) with a rigid map built from the arc-end line, the wall direction and the edge direction, recursively. Regions (real face outlines, holes included) are merged by extruding them 1 unit in the Front frame and unioning with the CSG kernel; the merged top face outline is the blank, so collinear points vanish (the two-flange test now expects 6 outline points, not 8). Verified: hole on a wall lands at BA + 8 in the flat, Z bracket flat = 100 + 2·BA + 30 + 25, Flatten dialog draws both.
- **Flange placement** (`position`: `bendOutside` default, `outside` = Material Outside, `inside` = Material Inside): the bend section shifts inward by R or R + T and a strip of the base of that width is cut first (`ctx.flangeCut`), so the envelope keeps the base size. Flange info now stores the shifted edge, `mid`, `origin` and `T` for the flat pattern. PM has a Flange Position select; script generation emits `position` when not default.
- Tests: three new sheet-metal tests (wall hole, Z bracket, placements + round trip); 188 expected.

**Later in session 2 (smart dimensions).**
- **Smart Dimension** (driven, editable): in sketch mode each entity gets HTML labels positioned by `worldToScreen` every frame (`UI.frameDims` from the render loop; `App.dimsStale` set by `updateSketches` rebuilds them): line length, circle Ø, arc R, rect/crect w and h, polygon R, slot length and width. Clicking a label swaps in a number input; Enter applies (line keeps its start and direction, slot keeps centre 1), Esc/blur cancels or commits; one undo step per edit. `App.view.showDims` (default on) is toggled by the ribbon Smart Dimension button, which now shows an active state (generic `active()` hook for ribbon items). Overlay is `#dims` in index.html with styles at the end of style.css.
- The local server had gone away mid-session (no listener on 8765); `preview_start` still refuses unattended sessions, so serve.ps1 was started in the background from PowerShell as CLAUDE.md documents.

**Later in session 2 (hem, jog, `toward`).**
- `p.hem(L, edges, {gap})` is a flange feature with `hem: true`, angle 180 and R = gap/2 (default gap T/2); script generation emits `p.hem`. `p.jog(height, length, edges, {angle, reverse, radius, position})` is a new `jog` feature: `buildJog` builds the first flange, applies it to a temporary body, computes the wall's end-edge mid-thickness point and builds the second flange there with `toward` = the outward direction, so it folds back parallel to the base; both flange infos are recorded so the recursive flat pattern unfolds them (UP then DOWN). Wall length = (H − 2R(1−cosA) + T·cosA)/sinA so the jogged flat sits `height` from the base's fold-side face.
- `buildFlange` gained `toward: [x,y,z]` (a world direction that decides the fold side, overriding `reverse`), and the bend origin is now derived from the actual fold side (`dot(u, nF) > 0`) rather than from `reverse` — this was wrong whenever `toward` flipped the side.
- A peer session is mid-way through its own SVG dimension layer (`#dim-layer`, `#dim-edit`, `UI.drawDims`, `App.dimsStale`) in index.html, style.css, ui-core.js. My HTML dimension labels now use `App.dimLabelsStale` so the two do not fight; the next session should keep one of the two implementations. Peer sessions were not reachable by message when this was written.

## Run 2026-09-22T12 — first routine run under the head-and-subagents protocol

**Context.** Scheduled task `daily-code-limit`, unattended, head + three parallel subagents (kernel, commands, tests). No open board requests; no peer sessions (`ListAgents` is not available in the scheduled session, so the head worked solo). Two subagents were killed mid-read by the account's session rate limit at 12:4x and resumed with `SendMessage` after the 13:00 reset; the resumed agents kept their context and finished. Baseline before the run: kernel tests 199/199, UI smoke 20/20.

**Queue item resolved without code.** "Decide between the two sketch-dimension overlays": the baseline commit only contains the SVG `#dim-layer` implementation (`drawDims` in ui-sketch.js, `#dim-layer`/`#dim-edit` in index.html and style.css); the HTML-label version (`#dims`, `frameDims`, `dimLabelsStale`) no longer exists anywhere. The `dimensions` test group passes, so the decision is: SVG layer stays, nothing to delete.

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (kernel.js, csg.js): **Miter flange** — `flange` feature fields `miter` (bool), `miterGap` (default 0). `buildFlange` now builds a per-edge record list; ends of two selected edges within `max(1e-4, 1e-3·len)` are a shared corner. With `miter`, each tool is swept past the corner by `wmax·dot(o, n_b)/dot(dIn, n_b)` (capped at 4·wmax, +1) and clipped by the bisector plane through the shift-corrected corner point, offset by `miterGap/2` toward its own side (`CSG.prototype.clipByPlane(normal, w)`: intersection with an enclosing box whose one face is the plane, so the cap is watertight). Collinear neighbours, opposite fold directions and corners sharper than ~18° are left unmitred. **Auto relief** — `relief: 'none' | 'rect' | 'obround'`, `reliefWidth` (null = T), `reliefDepth` (null = T/2). At every unshared flange end two probe points half a thickness past the end (one inside the base plate, one on the outward side of the edge) are tested with `CSG.prototype.pointTester()` (BSP descent, boundary counts inside); if either is inside the body a slot `[tEnd, tEnd + w] × [−(shift + d), R + T + d]` through the full thickness is added to `ctx.flangeCut`, which `rebuild` already subtracts before the union; obround rounds the inner end with 12 segments. `Kernel.flangeProfile(A, R, T, L, ov)` exports the section loop. Flange info records gain `mitred: [bool, bool]`. Builder `p.flange(L, edges, { miter, miterGap, relief, reliefWidth, reliefDepth })`; `generateScript` emits only non-defaults (fixed point verified). **Degenerate geometry guards** — `sketchProfile` drops zero-area loops with the warning "N zero-area contour(s) was/were ignored." (through `profile.warnings`, same path as the open-contour warning) and tags self-crossing loops with `cross` (exact segment–segment test, O(n²)); `checkProfile` throws "Sketch profile crosses itself near (x, y). Trim or move the entities so the contour does not self-intersect." from extrude, revolve, sweep and loft. Non-finite depth/angle/draft/path coordinates error with "… must be a number." (`Builder.extrude` no longer coerces NaN to 0).
- `commands` (ui-commands.js, ui-panels.js, ui-app.js, icons.js): Edge-Flange PM gains a **Miter** group (Miter corners check, Gap when on) and an **Auto Relief** group (type select; "Use default width (T)"/value and "Use default depth (T/2)"/value pairs, folded back into `reliefWidth`/`reliefDepth` null-or-number by `toFeature`, UI-only keys deleted so they never reach the model or the cache key). `UI.cmdMiterFlange()` opens the same PM titled Miter-Flange with `miter: true`; ribbon Miter Flange button wired (new `miter` icon), Insert ▸ Sheet Metal ▸ Miter Flange, unnamed mitred flanges become `Miter-FlangeN`, tree icon and title ("Miter flange 20 long, 90°, 2 edges, mitred", ", relief rectangle|obround"). **Ribbon tooltips**: `UI.shortcutFor(id)` in ui-app.js mirrors the keydown handler (S, E, L, R, C, A, P, T, D, F, Ctrl+Z/Y/B/S/N/O, Ctrl+8, Space, Del, F2); `ribbonTitle` in ui-panels.js renders `Label (Key) — description` from a label-keyed help map, keeps the "not available in this build yet" suffix and sets `aria-label`. No CSS or DOM changes.
- `tests` (tests/, examples/, README.md): kernel-tests group `miter & relief` (6 tests: corner volume = 2·first moment of the o ≥ 0 profile, gap opens the corner and leaves 45° faces, three mitred edges, relief slot 9600 − 24 + F with obround bounds and a notched flat, no relief at a convex corner, script round-trip) plus 5 error/warning tests (bow-tie, NaN depth/string depth/NaN revolve, zero-area rect, nothing-left → no closed profile). UI smoke: two new steps (mitred flange through `anvil.run` with the tree title checked; the Miter-Flange PM flow picking two edges → `Miter-Flange1`). Example "Tray" (120×80 base, five-edge mitred flange with obround relief, hanging hole). README: ribbon list, Part methods, feature JSON, sheet-metal section, kernel API, diagnostics, limitations.

**Verified by the head.** kernel-tests 210/210, ui-smoke 22/22, app loads with no console errors; click flow Sheet Metal ▸ Miter Flange ▸ two edge picks ▸ ✓ commits `Miter-Flange1` (volume 20175.27 on the 100×60 sheet), the generated script line is `p.flange(20, [[0,2,30],[50,2,0]], { angle: 90, name: "Miter-Flange1", miter: true })`.

**Decisions.**
- Web Worker rebuilds were deferred (queue item 3): making `UI.rebuild` asynchronous ripples through every PM commit, `window.anvil.run` and the smoke tests, which is a three-lane change that needs a design first (see QUEUE.md note).
- Relief is opt-in (`relief: 'none'` default) so every existing sheet-metal volume test stays exact.
- The miter clip plane is offset along its normal by `gap/2`, so the material removed by a gap is √2·A·gap on a square corner, not A·gap; the test allows that range.

**Known gaps.**
- Flat pattern of a mitred flange: the wall unfolds with its real 45° extension but the bend strip stays edge-length, leaving a small step in the blank at each mitred corner (flat area = base + Σ len·(BA + L) + 2·R·L per corner). Cosmetic; queued.
- Relief slots that lie fully inside the plate appear in the flat as an enclosed slot loop rather than an outline notch; the test accepts either.

**Next up.**
1. Web Worker rebuilds (design first; see QUEUE.md).
2. Flat pattern: bend strips to the mitre line; thickness-aware normal cut.
3. Phase 4: faster CSG (bounding-box early-out per polygon).
4. Phase 6: icons closer to the SolidWorks set; dark theme.

## Run 2026-09-22T13 — Web Worker rebuilds, mitre flat pattern

**Context.** Scheduled task, head + three parallel subagents (kernel; sketch+commands as one subagent; tests). No open board requests, no peer sessions (`ListAgents` is not available in the scheduled session). The previous run's commit landed at 13:31 while this run was taking stock, so the tree was clean at planning time. Baseline: kernel tests 210/210, UI smoke 22/22. Mid-run another session committed a protocol change (per-lane board documents and run phases); the lane documents for this run were written by the head after the subagents returned. Queue item 1 (Web Worker rebuilds) was taken with the design fixed in the briefs: the worker owns `Kernel.rebuild` and its cache and posts back a packed build; `UI.rebuild` keeps a synchronous fallback.

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (csg.js, kernel.js, new worker.js): `CSG.prototype.pack()` → `{ verts: Float64Array(3·V), counts: Int32Array(P), planes: Float64Array(4·P), shared }`, `CSG.unpack(obj)` (planes copied, so `volume()` is bit-identical), `CSG.packTransferables`. `Kernel.rebuild` now also returns `profiles: {sketchId: profile}`; `Kernel.packBuild(build)`, `packTransferables`, `unpackBuild` (`cache: null`), `applyProfiles(model, profiles)`. Planes and flange records were already plain JSON, so no conversions. `worker.js`: `importScripts('csg.js', 'kernel.js')`, keeps the feature cache between messages, `{type:'rebuild', seq, model}` → `{type:'built', seq, build}` or `{type:'error', seq, message}`. **Mitre flat pattern:** flange info records gain `miterPlanes: [{n, w}|null, {n, w}|null]`; in `flatPattern` each mitred strip end is derived from that plane (wall corner at `ext = t0 + κ·Rt·sinA`, `κ = (o·n)/(dIn·n)`), so the bend strip is a trapezoid ending on the mitre line and the bend line reaches it too. Reference model (100×60 sheet, two mitred 20 mm flanges sharing a corner): e = R = 2, the step vertices at (−BA, 0)/(0, BA) are gone, blank area = old + R·BA = 10150.284, 8 outline points.
- `sketch` + `commands` (ui-core.js, ui-sketch.js, ui-commands.js, ui-panels.js, ui-app.js, style.css): `UI.rebuild(opts)` returns a Promise. Sync path (`App.workerOn` false, `opts.sync`, or no `Worker`) runs `rebuildSync` and returns a resolved promise flagged `applied: true`; async path posts the model to a lazily created `worker.js`, one message in flight, later calls coalesce into one pending request (`noSave` AND-ed) that is posted when the reply lands, stale replies (seq mismatch) dropped, reply → `unpackBuild` → `applyProfiles` → `applyBuild` (shared tail; `lastRebuildMs` = kernel time + apply time). Worker `error`/`messageerror`/`{type:'error'}`/`postMessage` throw → `disableWorker` (one toast) and a synchronous finish, so the promise always resolves. A sync rebuild while a worker request is in flight detaches it so the stale reply is ignored. New: `UI.afterRebuild(p, fn)`, `UI.whenIdle()`, `UI.setWorkerOn(on)` (localStorage `anvil.worker`, `'1'`/`'0'`), `App.workerOn` (default `window.Worker && http(s)`), `App.rebuilding`, status "Rebuilding…" with a pulsing `busy` dot. Boot uses `{ noSave: true, sync: true }` so the first frame has a body. Converted call sites: `commitFeature` (returns a promise resolving to the feature; all 13 `onOK` handlers return it; `pmOK` returns it or `whenIdle()`), `newSketchOnPlane/OnFace` (`enterSketch` in the tail), Hole Wizard, `deleteFeature`, `exitSketch` (pending callback and selection in the tail), `runScript` (resolves `{ok}`/`{error}`), macro pane, `fileOpen`, Ctrl+B and Edit ▸ Rebuild toast, `undo`/`redo`/`afterModelSwap`/`loadModel` (return the promise; `zoomToFit` in the tail). `window.anvil.run/append/load/rebuild` return promises (run/append reject on a script error, as they threw before); `anvil.busy()`, `anvil.idle()`, `anvil.workerOn(bool)`. Menu: Edit ▸ "Rebuild in background (Web Worker)" (checked). PM live previews stay synchronous.
- `tests` (tests/, README.md): `test(name, fn)` accepts a thenable (row shown as WAIT, settled on resolve/reject, 10 s timeout; summary and `__anvilTests` written only when nothing is pending). New group `pack & worker` (5 tests: exact pack/unpack round trip, packBuild/unpackBuild of the Sheet Metal Bracket incl. flatPattern equality, MessageChannel clone, transferables, worker end-to-end with cache reuse and a broken feature) and the mitre flat-pattern test in `miter & relief` → 216. Smoke: all steps converted to `await anvil.run` / `pmAccept()` / `await idle()`, 30 s per-step timeout, `localStorage['anvil.worker']` cleared before the frame loads; four new steps (worker on by default with busy and "Rebuilding…" observed, worker off is synchronous and persisted, three back-to-back runs coalesce to the last model, the Edit menu item toggles `workerOn`) → 26. README: `worker.js` row, "Background rebuilds" section, automation table, kernel API lines, mitre note.

**Verified by the head (over HTTP, port 8765).** kernel-tests 216/216, ui-smoke 26/26 (busy and "Rebuilding…" observed, coalescing volume 3000, menu check mark toggles), app loads with no console errors. Funnel + closed shell through the worker: 8.9 s build during which a 10 ms interval fired 873 times on the main thread (the UI stays live). Ribbon flow Boss-Extrude ▸ ✓ click with the worker on commits `Boss-Extrude1` (volume 8000, selection set, status line written from the promise tail).

**Decisions.**
- The worker keeps the feature cache; the main thread's `App.buildCache` is only refreshed on synchronous rebuilds, so the first sync rebuild after a worker phase is a full build. Acceptable; noted in QUEUE.
- `anvil.run`/`append` reject on script errors (unchanged behaviour, now asynchronous); `runScript` in the UI resolves `{error}` instead.
- The mitre strip end follows the wall's clipped end line (a pentagon with the epsilon overlap) so non-90° mitres give no slivers.

**Known gaps.**
- Pre-existing (found by the kernel lane): two `position: "outside"` flanges sharing a corner do not attach in the flat pattern; the `attached` check uses the untrimmed edge ends. Queued.
- Kernel tests run from `file://` in headless Chrome with a virtual-time budget time out on the worker test; over HTTP with real time it passes.

**Next up.**
1. Thickness-aware "normal cut" for sheet metal (kernel + PM).
2. Flat pattern: attach Material-Outside flanges that share a corner.
3. Phase 4: faster CSG (bounding-box early-out per polygon).
4. Phase 6: icons closer to the SolidWorks set; dark theme.

## Run 2026-09-22T14 — Normal Cut, shared-corner flat pattern, worker cache, dark theme

**Context.** Scheduled task, head + four parallel subagents (kernel, sketch, commands, tests). No open board requests, no peer sessions. Clean tree at 46c8549; baseline kernel tests 216/216, UI smoke 26/26. Tasks taken from QUEUE "Do next" (all three) plus the dark theme from "Ideas"/Phase 6. Feature JSON and kernel API were fixed in the briefs (`normalCut` flag, `Kernel.normalCutTool`, `Kernel.cacheFromBuild`, `UI.setViewportTheme`, `UI.setTheme`) so the lanes could work at the same time.

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (kernel.js): **Normal Cut.** `extrude` features with `cut: true` may carry `normalCut: true`; `p.cut(d, { normalCut: true })` / `p.extrude(d, { cut: true, normalCut: true })`; `generateScript` emits it. `Kernel.normalCutTool(tool, body, model, dir)`: R = body ∩ tool is meshed; a face of R is a sheet-surface cap when its area-weighted triangle centroids see air just in front, metal at T/2 behind and air at T+ε behind (`body.pointTester()`), so flat regions and bend facets qualify and tool faces and thickness edges do not; with `dir` (extrusion direction; null for mid-plane / two-direction) only entry caps (n·dir < 0) are used, else all caps; each cap outline (outer loops, holes subtracted) is pushed through the thickness along −n and the balanced union replaces the tool. A cut of any depth becomes a through-thickness cut. If nothing crosses a sheet surface the plain tool is used with the warning "Normal cut: the cut does not cross a sheet face; a plain cut was made." (warnings now append instead of overwrite). Reference: 10×10 square on a 45° plane through a 100×60×2 sheet removes 282.8427 with body centroid z −29.806886 (oblique −29.782747) and no tilted faces. **Flat pattern:** `onSegment` → `overlapOnSegment`; a flange is attached when its edge line is collinear with an outline segment and overlaps it by more than max(1e-3, T/2), so two Material-Outside flanges sharing a corner both unfold (2 bends, blank 9692.7955, bbox [0,−23.372]–[123.372,60]). **`Kernel.cacheFromBuild(model, build)`**: one step per feature (key, tool, plane, flange, profile, error, warning), body only on the last step, `partial: true` on the rest; `rebuild` walks the matching prefix back over partial steps (unchanged model → reused n, appended feature → n, middle change → 0).
- `sketch` (ui-core.js): after every worker build `App.buildCache = Kernel.cacheFromBuild(App.model, build)` (guarded), so the next sync rebuild reuses the applied body and the old cache's bodies are released; the cache is kept across the worker-error fallback (never wrong, only less useful). `UI.buildCacheInfo()` → `{steps, partial}`. `UI.setViewportTheme('dark'|'light')` + `UI.viewportTheme`: recolours edge/grid/gridMajor/sketchDim/planeLine and the HLR occluder body (dark 0x1e232b so hidden-line mode reads as background); the stored theme is applied before boot. ui-sketch.js untouched (the dimension overlay is styled from style.css).
- `commands` (ui-commands.js, ui-panels.js, ui-app.js, icons.js, style.css, index.html): Cut-Extrude PM gains a `Sheet Metal` group with a `Normal cut` checkbox (key `normalCut`), shown only for cuts on a part with sheet parameters (`renderPM` groups now accept `show`; the Feature group's Cut checkbox rerenders); a new cut on a sheet part defaults to on; `toFeature` drops the key when off. Tree title appends ", normal cut". **Dark theme:** every colour in style.css is a `:root` variable with a `:root[data-theme="dark"]` block (panels #2b2f36/#23272d, viewport #1e2126, `--dim`, `--sketch`, `--icon-ink`, `color-scheme: dark`); icons' slate ink is `currentColor`; `UI.setTheme(name)`, `UI.theme()`, `anvil.theme(name)`; persisted as `localStorage['anvil.theme']`, applied by an inline script in index.html before first paint and again at boot; View ▸ Theme ▸ Light / Dark with check marks.
- `tests` (tests/, examples/, README.md): kernel tests 216 → 227 (`normal cut` ×5, shared-corner Material-Outside blank, `cacheFromBuild` ×3, "Normal Cut Bracket" example + round-trip); UI smoke 26 → 29 (Normal cut checkbox default/edit/absent on a plain part, View ▸ Theme with storage and viewport and menu marks, cache after a worker build); README sections for Normal cut, `cacheFromBuild`, theme.

**Verified by the head (over HTTP, port 8765).** kernel-tests 227/227, ui-smoke 29/29, app loads with no console errors. Live app: the oblique normal cut through the worker gives volume 11717.157 and centroid z −29.806886; Cut-Extrude PM on a sheet part shows Sheet Metal ▸ Normal cut checked, ✓ commits `normalCut: true`, and the generated script carries the flag; `anvil.theme('dark')` switches the chrome, viewport (rgb 30,33,38) and edge colour (d8dde3), light restores exactly.

**Decisions.**
- Normal cut replaces the tool rather than adding to it, so a partial-depth cut becomes a through cut (SolidWorks behaviour). Entry-side caps only when a direction exists, so a blind cut is not doubled from the far side.
- A cache made by `cacheFromBuild` cannot serve a change in the middle of the tree (only the final body is known); the worker keeps the full cache, so this only affects the sync fallback. A real cache produced from a partial one still carries the partial steps.
- The pre-existing duplicate `stroke` attribute in icons.js (accent strokes render in the slate ink) was left alone so the light theme stays pixel-identical.
- A cut sketched on the top face of a sheet from the PM still defaults to the +normal direction (away from the sheet) unless the sketch plane is a picked face; pre-existing, noted below.

**Known gaps.**
- Cut-Extrude from the PM on a `Top`-offset sketch defaults to going away from the sheet; the user needs "Reverse direction" (the smoke test sketches on the bottom face to avoid it). Consider defaulting a cut's direction toward the body.
- Normal cut ignores SolidWorks' "Optimize geometry" option and a closed hem (zero gap) is not recognised as a sheet surface because the metal continues 2T behind it.

**Next up.**
1. Cut direction default: point a new cut into the body when the sketch plane is offset from a face.
2. Phase 4: faster CSG (bounding-box early-out per polygon).
3. Phase 6: icons closer to the SolidWorks set; equations in PropertyManager fields.
4. Phase 5: multi-body parts (Solid Bodies folder without auto-merge).

## Run 2026-09-22T20 — CSG bounding-box early-out, cut direction default, SolidWorks-style icons

**Context.** Scheduled task, head + three parallel subagents (kernel, commands, tests). No open board requests, no peer sessions. Clean tree at 3b5f6d6; baseline kernel tests 227/227, UI smoke 29/29, bench sum of medians 2994 ms (26 parts). Tasks: both QUEUE "Do next" items (cut direction default, faster CSG) and ROADMAP Phase 6 icons. The kernel API was fixed in the briefs (`Kernel.cutDirectionHint(model, sketchId, body)` → true/false/null, `CSG.prototype.getBounds()`), so the commands and tests lanes could write against it while the kernel lane built it. Before the kernel lane touched csg.js the head recorded every example's volume and the bench with the old code, so the "unchanged volume" tests have real reference numbers.

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (csg.js, kernel.js): **Bounding-box early-out.** `CSG.prototype.getBounds()` (cached on the instance; every geometry-changing operation returns a new CSG so it never goes stale), `Node.prototype.extractFar(bounds, eps, out)`, helpers `boundsOf`/`boundsOverlap`/`polygonFar`/`farEps` (eps = EPSILON + 1e-9·extent, touching counts as near). Both BSP trees are still built from all polygons (a tree built from the near subset misclassifies: a cube floating in a plate's hole would lose its bottom); far polygons are lifted out of the node lists before clipping and appended back unchanged (union: both sides; subtract: A's kept, B's dropped; intersect: both dropped). Disjoint bounding boxes short-circuit (concatenate / copy of A / empty). **`Kernel.cutDirectionHint`**: resolves the sketch plane (including `plane` features), takes an inside point of the first outer loop (`insidePoint2D`, avoids holes), probes ±eps with `pointTester` (eps = max(1e-3, 1e-3·diag)); if neither probe is inside, `rayHits(body, p, n, eps)` intersects the line with every polygon (exact per-plane point-in-polygon on the dominant-axis projection) and the nearer side wins; both probes inside (plane in the material) → null. Never throws. The first version marched with doubling steps and stepped over a 2 mm sheet from 10 mm away; the head caught it in the tests and the ray test replaced it.
- `commands` (ui-commands.js, ui-panels.js, icons.js): `defaultCutReverse(sketchId, fallback)` applies the old `plane.custom → reverse` rule, then the kernel hint (true/false override, null keeps the rule); `cmdExtrude` uses it for every new cut, so the *Reverse direction* checkbox and the preview open pointing into the body from either face. Hole Wizard unchanged (`reverse: true`, always on a picked face); Cut-Revolve skipped (its `reverse` flips the sweep, not the plane normal). **Icons**: the whole set redrawn on a 16×16 viewBox (~1.25 stroke, `currentColor` ink, one `stroke`/`fill` per element, the duplicate-`stroke` quirk is gone): blue-grey three-face blocks, green boss/add, red-orange cut/remove, yellow sketch, grey-blue reference geometry. All 86 names kept; `hem` and `jog` added and wired (Sheet Metal ribbon, `featureIcon`: flange → miter/hem/bend by flag, jog → jog, base flange → flange). Layout and order untouched.
- `tests` (tests/, README.md): kernel tests 227 → 264: group `csg early-out` (getBounds, disjoint / touching / overlapping / nested cubes with volumes and the unsplit polygon count of a disjoint union, far boss + hole on a plate with analytic volume and centroid, pack/unpack of an early-out result, a missed cut leaves the body's polygons unsplit, and `same volume as before the early-out: <example>` for all 19 Design Library parts against `EXPECTED_VOLUMES`), group `cut direction hint` (10: top/bottom face, above/below the body, a plane whose normal misses, inside the material, `planeFromFace` frames, null cases). Smoke 29 → 32: Cut-Extrude PM on `p.sketch("Front", 2)` opens with *Reverse direction* checked and removes 10×10×2; on `p.sketch("Front", 0)` unchecked and still removes 10×10×2, edit PM keeps it; 84 icon ids × 2 sizes parse with `DOMParser` and every rendered ribbon SVG re-parses. bench.html exposes `window.__anvilBenchTotal` and a total row in the Markdown copy. README: `cutDirectionHint`, `getBounds`, the early-out paragraph, PM default, test descriptions.

**Head fixes during integration.** Pinned the 16 example volumes the tests lane left at null from the pre-change measurement; replaced the Enclosure lid analytic pin (its formula was off by 1.8 %, the measured 31747.799 is right); the "6 quads" assertion became "same polygon count as the box alone" (`p.box` is 8 polygons in both old and new code); Funnel gets a 5e-6 tolerance (see below). BENCHMARKS.md entry.

**Verified by the head (over HTTP, port 8765).** kernel-tests 264/264, ui-smoke 32/32, app loads with no console errors; ribbon Features and Sheet Metal tabs eyeballed at 800 px in light and dark themes (green boss, red cut, bent-sheet glyphs read correctly). Bench after: sum of medians **2375 ms vs 2994 before (−21 %)**; Mug 298 → 149, 24-hole bolt circle 81 → 46, revolve with 6 grooves 114 → 57, Enclosure lid 12 → 5, sheet box + flat 40 → 20, Funnel 1550 → 1325. Every example's volume unchanged to 1e-6 (17 of 19 to 1e-9 or better).

**Decisions.**
- Funnel volume moved by 1.0e-6 relative (12268.3952 → 12268.3829). Old-vs-new per feature: loft and spout identical to 1e-15 (510 polygons instead of 564, the old code split far facets needlessly), only the shell differs. The shell's slab union over 14k polygons now sees unsplit input facets; the old path interpolated split vertices and dropped sub-3-vertex pieces in `Polygon.tryCreate`, so the new number is likely the more accurate one. Pinned at the old value with a 5e-6 tolerance (`EXPECTED_TOL`) rather than re-pinned, so a future regression toward either value is still caught.
- Trees keep every polygon's plane; only the clipping is skipped. Building a tree from the near subset alone is wrong (kernel lane found the floating-cube case).
- Icons are original artwork in the SolidWorks colour language, not copies.

**Known gaps.**
- The Funnel and Rounded handle are now dominated by the shell and fillet slab unions, where every slab overlaps its neighbours, so the bbox test rarely fires; the rest of Phase 4 ("split only inside overlap regions") is what would speed those up.
- `cutDirectionHint` costs one pass over every body polygon (15k for the Funnel) when the ±eps probes miss; fine for opening a PM, not for per-frame use.

**Next up.**
1. Phase 5: multi-body parts (Solid Bodies folder, no auto-merge); agree feature JSON first.
2. Phase 4: polygon splitting only inside overlap regions (shell/fillet slab unions).
3. Phase 6: equations in PropertyManager fields; configuration manager.

## Run 2026-09-22T20b — multi-body parts (Solid Bodies folder, Merge result, Feature Scope)

**Context.** Scheduled task, head + four parallel subagents (kernel, commands, sketch, tests). No open board requests, no peer sessions. Clean tree at a5a945e; baseline kernel tests 264/264, UI smoke 32/32, bench 2375 ms. The run id carries a `b` because the previous run already used `2026-09-22T20`. Task: the top QUEUE item, ROADMAP Phase 5 "Multi-body parts: keep bodies separate (no auto-merge) with a Solid Bodies folder". The feature JSON and kernel API were fixed in the briefs (`merge: false`, `bodies: [i…]`, `build.bodies` / `build.bodyFeatures`, `Kernel.combineBodies` tagging every polygon with its body so `mesh.faces[i].body` tells the UI which body a face belongs to, `Kernel.bodyInfo`, `Kernel.bodyNearPoint`) so all four lanes could work at once.

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (kernel.js; csg.js untouched): `rebuild` keeps `ctx.bodies` / `ctx.bodyFeatures` and re-syncs `ctx.body = combineBodies(bodies)` after every feature so the single-solid readers (normal cut, cut direction hint, flat pattern, meshing) keep working. Merge rule: a boss unions with every body whose bounding box meets the tool's (touching counts; merged bodies collapse into the lowest-indexed slot), otherwise starts a new body; `merge: false` always starts one. Cuts subtract from every body in scope (`bodies`, or all), drop a body they empty, and warn "removed no material" only when no scoped body changed. Shell, fillet, chamfer, flange and jog act on the body nearest their first pick point and write the result back into that slot; `mirror` with `body: true` mirrors the combined solid then merges. `meshFromBody` groups faces by plane AND body (`face.body`). Cache steps, `cacheFromBuild`, `packBuild` (bodies travel; `body` only for a build without a list), `packTransferables`, `unpackBuild` (recombines, accepts old body-only messages). Builder `bodyOpts` on extrude/cut/revolve/sweep/loft/patterns/mirror/hole/box/cylinder; `generateScript` emits `merge: false` / `bodies: [..]` only when set.
- `commands` (ui-commands.js, ui-panels.js, ui-app.js, style.css): FeatureManager `Solid Bodies(n)` folder (`.ti.bodies-folder`, expandable, open by default on a multi-body part) with one `.ti.body-row[data-body]` per body named after its creating feature; click selects, double-click edits the feature, context menu Hide/Show, Mass Properties, Zoom to Selection, Edit Feature. `Merge result` checkbox at the end of Direction 1 in Boss-Extrude, Revolve, Sweep, Loft, Linear/Circular Pattern and Mirror (hidden on the cut variant). `Feature Scope` group on Cut-Extrude, Cut-Revolve, cut Sweep/Loft and patterns/mirrors of cuts, only with two or more bodies: select `Bodies` (All bodies / Selected bodies) plus one check per body, committed as `bodies: [indices]`. Edit round trip restores both. Mass Properties lists each body when there are two or more. `anvil.bodies()`, `anvil.selectBody(i)`, `summary().bodies`. Shared helpers `UI.bodyList`, `UI.bodyInfos` (cached per build), `UI.bodyTitle`.
- `sketch` (ui-core.js, ui-sketch.js): `App.sel = { kind: 'body', index }` (also sel2 and hover) highlights every face tagged with that body; `UI.selectBody(i)` sets it and reports `<name>: volume <v> mm³`. `App.hiddenBodies` + `UI.setBodyHidden(i, hidden)` / `UI.isBodyHidden(i)`: the three.js geometry keeps the full arrays and gets an index attribute of the visible triangles only, `visTris` maps the rendered triangle back to the global one in `pickFace`, so `App.mesh`, face indices, mass properties and exports are untouched; edge lines and `pickEdge` skip edges whose faces are all hidden (`App.edges` computed once per mesh). Hover status names the body (`Body: Boss-Extrude2`) on multi-body parts. `applyBuild` prunes stale hidden entries and body selections.
- `tests` (tests/, examples/, README.md): kernel tests 264 → 296: group `multi-body` (29: separate bodies and tags, scope, touching boss merges / disjoint boss is a new body / bridging boss merges three into one, a cut that empties a body removes it, fillet and shell on one body only, "removed no material" only when nothing changed, mirror-body with merge:false, script round trip, pack/unpack round trip incl. an old body-only message, cache reuse, single body unchanged, `bodyNearPoint`) + 3 rows for the new example; the `packBuild` shape assertion now expects `packed.bodies.length === 1`. Smoke 32 → 39 (Solid Bodies folder rows, selectBody, hide/show keeps mass, Merge result → second body, Feature Scope → `bodies: [1]` cuts body 1 only, no Feature Scope on a single body, per-body Mass Properties). Design Library: **Two-body clamp** (base + floating cap with `merge: false`, two holes scoped to the cap, fillet on the base; measured 28348.648 pinned in `EXPECTED_VOLUMES`). README: `merge`/`bodies` rows, Kernel API, a `## Multi-body parts` section, `anvil.bodies()`.

**Head fixes during integration.** `Kernel.bodyNearPoint` picked by nearest *vertex*, so a fillet picked mid-way along the clamp base edge resolved to the cap (a hole vertex was closer than the edge ends) and failed with "No edges found"; it now measures the distance to every polygon edge segment. The clamp example fillet pick moved back to the mid-edge point `[30, 0, 0]` to exercise it.

**Verified by the head (over HTTP, port 8765).** kernel-tests 296/296, ui-smoke 39/39, app loads with no console errors; two-box part: scoped cut and fillet touch only body 1 (1000 / 591.058), Solid Bodies(2) rows, `selectBody(1)` highlights the second cube with its volume in the status bar, `setBodyHidden(1, true)` draws 12 triangles with mass unchanged at 1591.058, Cut-Extrude PM shows Feature Scope. Bench sum of medians 2315 ms (2375 before; the per-feature body sync is noise on single-body parts).

**Decisions.**
- A boss that touches nothing starts a new body even with `Merge result` on (SolidWorks behaviour). The same polygons used to live in one solid, so total volumes are unchanged, but coplanar faces of disjoint pieces are now separate mesh faces.
- Merge is decided by bounding boxes meeting, not by actual contact: two pieces whose boxes overlap without touching still become one CSG with two shells, exactly as before this run. `merge: false` is the way to keep them apart.
- `build.body` stays the combined solid so every single-solid consumer keeps working; a lone body is tagged in place (always index 0), several bodies are cloned before tagging so a body that changes index never carries a stale tag.
- Hidden bodies are viewport state only (not saved, not undoable).

**Known gaps.**
- Feature Scope picks bodies from a checkbox list, not by clicking faces in the viewport.
- Patterns of a `merge: false` boss are one tool, so their instances merge with each other and with whatever their combined box meets.
- `Zoom to Selection` on a body zooms to the whole model (no per-body bounds helper yet).

**Next up.**
1. Phase 4: polygon splitting only inside overlap regions (shell/fillet slab unions dominate the Funnel and Rounded handle).
2. Phase 6: equations in PropertyManager fields; configuration manager.
3. Phase 5: simple assembly document (insert parts, mate by coincident planes / concentric axes) now that bodies are separate.

## Run 2026-09-25T15 — Equations (global variables) and faster CSG (faces restored whole)

**Context.** Scheduled task, head + four parallel subagents (kernel, commands, sketch, tests). The lock from run 2026-09-22T21 was three days stale; that run had left an uncommitted equations kernel in kernel.js, committed first as `WIP recovered from interrupted run` (4eefeb8). No open board requests, no peer sessions. Tasks: QUEUE "Do next" (CSG splitting only inside overlaps) and ROADMAP Phase 6 "Equations", finishing the recovered kernel work.

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (kernel.js, csg.js): Equations finished: `Kernel.isExpression / evalExpression / resolveEquations / resolvedModel / resolveFeature / hasExpressions / EXPR_FUNCTIONS` exported; `generateScript` writes `p.equations([{name, expr}])` and quoted `"=..."` fields (sheet, planes, depth, angle, fillet r, chamfer d, shell, flange options); Builder numeric args go through `num()` (pattern counts, cpattern centre, flange `toward`); a bad expression is a feature error (`depth: Unknown variable "Q"`), never a throw; cache keys follow resolved values. CSG: every operand polygon carries a source record; a boolean that removed nothing from a face returns that face whole instead of its BSP fragments (`CSG.options.restoreWhole`, default on). Funnel 1844 → 1292 ms (14071 → 4576 faces), Rounded handle 738 → 619 ms, Mug 197 → 154 ms, Handlebar 1775 → 646 faces. The flat-pattern region merge runs with it off: whole faces leave T-junctions wider than `faceOutline`'s weld and split the corner blank into two loops.
- `commands` (ui-commands.js, ui-panels.js, ui-app.js, icons.js, style.css): Tools ▸ Equations… modal "Equations, Global Variables, and Dimensions" (`#eq-dialog`: add, delete, reorder, live values, red rows refuse OK, one undo step); Equations folder in the FeatureManager (`.eq-folder`, `.eq-row[data-eq]`, double-click opens the dialog); every PM number field accepts `=...` with a `.pm-expr-hint` value/error and a disabled OK while broken, expression text kept on edit; previews use the resolved model; `anvil.equations()` get/set, `summary().equations/equationErrors`; Zoom to Selection on a body row calls `UI.zoomToBody`.
- `sketch` (ui-core.js, ui-sketch.js): Modify box evaluates `=T*3` and plain arithmetic through `UI.evalInput`, keeps the box open with the message on error; equation errors in the status bar ("Equation W: …"); `UI.equationVars()`; `UI.bodyBounds(i)`, `UI.zoomToBody(i)`, Zoom to Fit shares `UI.fitBox`.
- `tests` (tests/, examples/, README.md): kernel tests 296 → 334 (`equations` ×21, `csg overlap split` ×15, Parametric plate rows); smoke 39 → 46 (equations API/tree/dialog, PM `=` field, status line, Modify box, body zoom); Design Library **Parametric plate** (L, W, T, E, D; volume 74938.628 pinned analytically); README `## Equations`.

**Head fixes during integration.** `index.html` `#dim-edit input` was `type="number"`, so the browser discarded `=T*3` before the sketch code saw it; now `type="text" inputmode="decimal"`. Sent the mitre corner-blank regression back to the kernel lane (fixed as above).

**Verified by the head (port 8765).** kernel-tests 334/334, ui-smoke 46/46, app loads with no console errors; Parametric plate via the Equations dialog T 8 → 12 gives exactly 1.5× volume, UI.undo restores T=8 and 74938.628.

**Decisions.**
- Box/cylinder/hole sizes and sketch entity values given as `=...` are evaluated when the script runs; only feature fields stay live expressions (sketch geometry is never an expression).
- `Builder.equations([...])` keeps each expr exactly; the object form drops a leading `=`.
- Building BSP trees from near faces only was not done: a partial tree misclassifies inside/outside. Restoring whole faces gets most of the win safely.

**Known gaps.** Circular pattern axis point, hem gap and sketch-mode PM fields (offset distance, polygon sides, entity properties) keep numbers only. `window.anvil` has no `undo()`. Configuration manager (named parameter sets) not started.

**Next up.**
1. Phase 6: configuration manager driving the equations (named sets of variable values).
2. Phase 4: near-face-only BSP trees with a per-piece ray-cast inside test.
3. Phase 5: simple assembly document (insert parts, mates).

## Run cloud-2026-09-25a — Configuration manager (named sets of equation values)

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. Head wrote the kernel part, then two parallel subagents (commands, tests). The CSG near-face BSP task was left alone; a local run owns it. Tests ran in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56 + /opt/pw-browsers/chromium) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`; cdnjs is blocked by the cloud network policy, so the runner routed the three.js r128 CDN URL to the same file from npm `three@0.128.0` (harness only, no repo change).

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (head, kernel.js): `model.configurations = [{ name, values: { T: '6' } }]`, `model.activeConfiguration`. `resolveEquations` evaluates `effectiveEquations(model)` (the active configuration's values replace the named variables' exprs; unknown names ignored; an unknown active name means the base equations) and returns `configuration`; rebuild / pack / unpack carry `build.configuration`. Exported `configurationList / findConfiguration / activeConfiguration / effectiveEquations / captureConfiguration / applyEquations`. Builder `p.configurations(list, active?)`, `p.activeConfiguration(name)` (throws for an unknown name); `generateScript` writes both right after `p.equations`. Cache keys already follow resolved values, so a switch rebuilds only the features whose values change.
- `commands` (ui-panels.js, ui-app.js, index.html, style.css): ConfigurationManager tab in `#fm-tabs` shows `#config-panel` (`.cfg-row[data-name]`, `.active`, `.selected`; `#cfg-add / #cfg-rename / #cfg-delete / #cfg-activate` "Show"; double-click shows; context menu). `UI.configurations = { list, active, add, rename, remove, activate, set }`, each one undo step. First Add stores the current values as "Default" plus the new configuration and makes the new one active. Equations dialog is configuration-aware (`#eq-config`, `Kernel.applyEquations`). Part row reads "Part1 (Thick)". `anvil.configurations()` get/set, `.activate/.add/.rename/.remove`, `anvil.activateConfiguration`; `summary().configuration`, `summary().configurations`.
- `tests` (tests/, examples/, README.md): kernel `configurations` group ×17 + 3 via the example loops (354); smoke ×7 (53); Design Library **Configured spacer** (80×40×T plate − N 10×20×D pockets at pitch S; Small 18000, Medium 29000 (active), Large 32000, pinned analytically, every configuration checked); optional `configurations` map on example entries; README `## Configurations`.

**Verified by the head (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 354/354, ui-smoke 53/53, app page loads with no console errors.

**Decisions.**
- A configuration stores expressions for the variables it names, not a full snapshot requirement; unnamed variables fall back to the base equations.
- With a configuration active, the Equations dialog writes a variable the configuration names into that configuration only (SolidWorks "This configuration"); new variables go to the base list.
- Box/hole/sketch sizes given as `=...` are still evaluated at script time, so configurations vary only live feature fields (depth, pattern count/spacing, fillet, chamfer, shell, plane offset).

**Known gaps.** After a PropertyManager closes the left pane returns to the tree even if the ConfigurationManager tab was open. No design-table view (edit all configurations' values in one grid). Features cannot be suppressed per configuration yet.

**Next up.**
1. Phase 4: near-face-only BSP trees with a per-piece ray-cast inside test (owned by the local run).
2. Configurations: per-configuration feature suppression and a design-table grid.
3. Phase 5: simple assembly document (insert parts, mates).

## Run cloud-2026-09-25b — CSG trees built from the near region only

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents; `csg.js`, `tests/` and `BENCHMARKS.md` only (another cloud session was on the UI files; `kernel.js` untouched). Task: QUEUE "Do next" / ROADMAP Phase 4 "Faster CSG", last part. Tests in headless Chromium 141 (Playwright 1.56) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` because cdnjs is blocked (harness only). Baseline on 3377590: kernel 354/354, smoke 53/53, bench sum of medians 1510 ms.

**Done (csg.js).**
- `nearOperands` / `nearTrees` / `nearTree`: when at most `nearRatio` (0.5) of an operand's polygons lie near the polygons it will clip, its BSP tree is built from X ∩ K instead of X, where K is the bounding box of the other operand's near polygons grown by a margin (97·EPSILON + 1.37e-6·extent). X ∩ K is X's polygons clipped to K plus the parts of K's six faces inside X ("caps"). Inside K it is the same solid with the same faces as X, so it clips everything in K exactly like the full tree, coplanar faces included; clipped polygons stay ≥ margin away from the caps.
- Caps: K's faces are split down the tree of the clipped polygons; each leaf cell meets K in a convex region X's surface does not cross, so one point per leaf (the centroid of its largest piece) decides for all its pieces. That point is classified by a ray cast against all of X (`rayCount` over flat typed arrays with a bounding-box reject; signed crossing count, +1 per exit), two directions that must agree. A cap coplanar with a node plane, a ray within tol of an edge or face, disagreeing directions, over 256 leaves or an empty X ∩ K all return null, and the boolean falls back to the classic full trees (`CSG.stats.fallback`).
- The clipping runs as lists in exactly the classic order (union: A by T(B), B by T(A) twice with flips; subtract and intersect with the inverted trees), far polygons are passed through (union, A side of subtract) or dropped, and `restoreWhole` still returns uncut faces whole. The near path runs only with `restoreWhole` on, so the flat-pattern region merge (which turns it off) keeps the classic algorithm, fragments and all.
- `CSG.options.nearTrees` (default **on**), `CSG.options.nearRatio`, `CSG.stats { near, classic, fallback }`.
- Fixed a classic bug found by the fuzz: a boolean with an empty operand ran the trees and ∅ − A returned A's faces inverted (negative volume); now ∅ − A = ∅, A − ∅ = A, ∅ ∪ A = A, ∅ ∩ A = ∅.

**Tests.** kernel-tests 354 → 365, new group `csg near-region trees`: options/counters; a box sunk into a 96-gon disc (near path, +64/−32/32, the 96 side quads stay whole); coplanar faces (a square through the disc with shared top and bottom, one across the rim: union + ∩ = disc + box); touching solids (on top, underneath, against the rim vertex, no polygon left on the glued face); nested solids (a cavity whose near box holds no disc face, the disc inside a big box); a 36-hole plate cut one hole at a time (analytic volume, 36 near cuts, 3498 vs 5156 polygons); a tilted disc and box; empty operands; restoreWhole off never takes the near path; every example's volume with near trees on vs off (1e-9); 40 seeded random chains of boxes and prisms on a half grid against exact point membership. Each near result is also compared with the classic one on 400 sample points and meshed watertight. A mutation (keeping the outside caps) fails 136 tests. Outside the suite, on the committed code: 3400 random chains in Node (1400 of them rotated off-axis, 1400 with `nearRatio` 1) — 0 volume mismatches vs the classic, 0 sample points off the exact answer.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 365/365, ui-smoke 53/53, app page loads with no console errors. Bench (two runs each, tests/BENCHMARKS.md): sum of medians 1510 / 1484 ms before, 1050 / 1050 after. Rounded handle 208 → 39 ms, Mug 104 → 39, Funnel 816 → 766, grooves 51 → 14, 40 stacked unions 77 → 10, Pulley 19 → 4; polygons Rounded handle 5294 → 756, Mug 3525 → 1118.

**Decisions.**
- Default on: it is a clear win on every part where it applies and falls back to the classic trees on any doubt.
- `nearRatio` 0.5, not 0.7: 0.7 makes the Funnel ~10 % faster but moves its volume by 1.1e-6 (an EPSILON-level sliver between near-coplanar shell slabs, within the pinned tolerance but not "unchanged"); at 0.5 every example matches the classic to 1e-9.
- The Funnel's big shell booleans stay classic: their operands are 70–85 % near each other by bounding box, so there is nothing to leave out.

**Known gaps.** README's "Boolean early-out" paragraph does not mention near trees yet (README was outside this run's files). The near test is one bounding box per operand; a finer one (per-cell grid) could help the Funnel's shell.

**Next up.**
1. Configurations: per-configuration feature suppression and a design-table grid.
2. Phase 5: simple assembly document (insert parts, mates).
3. Funnel/shell speed: a grid-based near test or fewer shell slabs.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25c — Assembly documents (Insert Components, Coincident / Concentric mates)

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. Task given by the user: ROADMAP Phase 5 "Simple assembly document: insert parts, mate by coincident planes / concentric axes". Another cloud session was editing `csg.js`, so neither `csg.js` nor `kernel.js` was touched; the new kernel code lives in the new file `anvil/assembly.js`. Head wrote an API contract (model JSON, refs, mate rules, `Assembly.*`, UI plumbing names) and ran four parallel subagents (kernel, sketch, commands, tests) against it. Baseline 354/354 kernel, 53/53 smoke. Tests ran in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, with the three.js r128 CDN URL routed to npm `three@0.128.0` in the runner only.

**Done per lane.**
- `kernel` (new `assembly.js`, global `Assembly`): assembly model `{ type: 'assembly', name, units, features: [], components, mates, nextId }`; components `{ id, name 'Plate<1>', source { example | script | model }, fixed, transform { position, rotation (row-major 3×3) } }`; mates `{ type 'coincident' | 'concentric', a, b, alignment?, suppressed? }` with refs `{ component, face | plane | cylinder | axis }` (component-local points) or origin `{ plane } / { axis }`. `rebuild(model, cache)` builds each part once per source (cache), solves, writes the solved transforms back into the model, and returns a build shaped like a multi-body part build (`body` tagged per component, `bodies`, `bodyNames`, `components` with `dof`/`underDefined`, `mates` with residuals, `errors` keyed by component/mate id). Solver: fixed components never move; mates are added one at a time, a greedy step rotates only about the axis earlier mates left free and translates without breaking them, then Levenberg–Marquardt over all accepted mates (6 unknowns per floating component); a mate left above 1e-6 is rejected with "Mate cannot be satisfied (over-defined or conflicting)" and the previous positions are kept; DOF from the Jacobian rank, propagated through chains. Cylinders: connected facet patches whose normals are square to a common axis (neighbours turning < 40°), axis and radius by least squares; box sides and hex flats rejected. Also `refFromPick`, `massProps` (each part's density, mass-weighted centroid), `Builder` (`a.insert/mate/coincident/concentric/fix/float`), `runScript`, `isAssemblyScript`, `generateScript`.
- `sketch` (ui-core.js, ui-sketch.js): assemblies always rebuild synchronously (`Assembly.rebuild(App.model, App.asmCache)`; an in-flight worker part build is settled by it, a late worker reply is ignored); error status names the component or mate; `bodyName`/`bodyInfo` return component names; `loadModel` takes assemblies (nextId over components and mates); `UI.isAssembly`, `UI.componentIndex`, `UI.selectComponent`; hover `Component: Pin<1>`; sketch mode refused in an assembly.
- `commands` (ui-commands.js, ui-panels.js, ui-app.js, icons.js, style.css, index.html): `assembly.js` script tag; File ▸ New modal with New part / New assembly; Assembly CommandManager tab (Insert Components, Mate, Fix, Float, Hide/Show Components) + Evaluate, part tabs hidden, part-only commands refuse (sketch creation refused before a feature is added); Insert Component PM (Design Library parts, last open part, Browse… JSON, X/Y/Z); Mate PM (Mate Selections from face / origin-plane picks via `Assembly.refFromPick`, Standard Mates with Concentric default for two cylinders, Mate alignment, live preview, Cancel restores, double-click edits); tree `.ti.asm-root`, `.ti.component-row` "(f)" / "(-)", `.ti.mates-folder`, `.ti.mate-row` "Coincident1 (Plate<1>, Plate<2>)", context menus (Fix/Float/Hide/Show/Delete/Mass Properties; Edit/Suppress/Unsuppress/Delete); assembly Mass Properties per component + totals; script console and Design Library open assembly scripts; `window.anvil.newAssembly/newPart/isAssembly/insert/mate/components/mates/fix/removeComponent/removeMate`, `mass()/script()/run()/load()` for assemblies, `summary().type`; 7 new icons.
- `tests` (tests/, examples/, README.md): kernel `assembly` group ×26 + 3 example rows (383); example loops handle `kind: 'assembly'` (volume pin, `checks` positions, script round trip via `Assembly.generateScript`); bench skips assembly entries; smoke ×10 assembly steps via `window.anvil`; Design Library **Pinned plate assembly** (fixed Plate with bolt holes, two pins concentric with holes and flush with the plate bottom — one inserted rotated 90° — and a 30×20×5 cap seated on the plate top against two side faces; volume 100·60·8 − 4·A·8 + 2·A·20 + 30·20·5 with A = polyCircleArea(4), = 51391.868; Pin<1> [10,0,10], Pin<2> [90,0,10], Cap<1> [0,8,40]); README `## Assemblies`.

**Head fixes during integration.** `UI.loadModel` now clears `App.hiddenBodies` (a hidden component index leaked into the next document). Added 3 smoke steps that drive the Insert Component and Mate PropertyManagers (face picks through `App.command.onPick`, Coincident / Anti-Aligned default, ✓ stacks Plate<2> at y = 8, edit + Cancel leaves the model byte-identical) — smoke 63 → 66. README UI bullets corrected to the built UI.

**Verified by the head (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 383/383, ui-smoke 66/66, app page loads with no console errors; the Pinned plate assembly opens from the Design Library with 4 components, 7 mates all satisfied, volume 51391.868, the pins "(-)" with 1 DOF each, rendered correctly (screenshot checked). csg.js / kernel.js unchanged against origin/main.

**Merged with main after PR #2 (cloud-2026-09-25b, near-region CSG trees).** Conflicts in SESSION_LOG, QUEUE and kernel-tests.html resolved keeping both sides. The new `every example has the same volume with and without near-region trees` test built every Design Library entry as a part; it now goes through `buildExample`, so the Pinned plate assembly is built by `Assembly`. After the merge: kernel-tests 394/394, ui-smoke 66/66, app page loads with no console errors.

**Decisions.**
- Assemblies build on the main thread (the worker only loads csg.js + kernel.js); parts are cached per source, so only the solve reruns on a mate edit.
- Mates store component-local pick points and re-resolve them on every rebuild (like fillet/shell `at` points); the solved transforms are saved in the model so a reload starts from the solved state.
- Default alignment: Coincident face–face is Anti-Aligned (faces touch), anything with a plane is Aligned; Concentric is Aligned.
- A conflicting mate is rejected alone (its error in red), the rest of the assembly keeps its solved positions.

**Known gaps.** No Distance / Angle / Parallel mates, no mate offsets. No Move/Rotate Component drag in the viewport. Components are copies (an `{ example }` or `{ script }` source rebuilt from its text, an embedded model), not live links to part files. A `cylinder` ref on a point with no cylindrical face under it snaps to the nearest cylinder instead of erroring. No interference detection, no exploded view, no sub-assemblies. Browse… for a part file not exercised headlessly. The tree/HUD "▾" glyphs show as mojibake under `python -m http.server` because `index.html` has no `<meta charset>` (pre-existing; the artifact wrapper supplies one).

**Next up.**
1. Phase 5: 2D drawing view (orthographic projections with hidden lines as SVG, dimensions, PDF/DXF).
2. Assemblies: Distance / Angle / Parallel mates, Move Component drag, interference detection.
3. Phase 4: near-face-only BSP trees (owned by the other session).

## Run cloud-2026-09-25d — 2D drawings (Standard 3 View, exact hidden lines, SVG / DXF / PDF)

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents (the work sat in one new file plus small hooks). Task: ROADMAP Phase 5 "2D drawing view: orthographic projections with hidden lines as SVG, dimensions, export PDF/DXF". Another cloud session has an unmerged assembly-document PR on the ui-*.js files, `index.html` and `style.css`, so the new code lives in `drawing.js` / `drawing.css` and the edits to existing files are small and additive. `csg.js` and `kernel.js` untouched. Tests in headless Chromium 141 (Playwright 1.56) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` because cdnjs is blocked (harness only). Baseline on 6c44175: kernel 365/365, smoke 53/53.

**Done.**
- `drawing.js` engine (global `Drawing`, no UI needed): `orientation` (Front/Back/Left/Right/Top/Bottom/Isometric/Dimetric/Trimetric frames, u × v = e, the ortho ones equal to the sketch frames); `meshEdges` (face-outline segments grouped by supporting line and split at every collinear endpoint, so T-junctions between differently split faces pair up; faces meeting under 23° count as one smooth surface); `projectView` (sharp edges and silhouettes clipped against the front-facing triangles on a 2D grid: inside-the-triangle and in-front-of-its-plane are linear along the edge, so each triangle hides one exact interval; collinear merge, hidden-under-visible removal, circle recovery from closed equal-chord chains); `newDrawing` / `standard3View` / `layout` (A3 or ANSI B by units, largest standard scale that fits, third- or first-angle, isometric in the free corner with its own scale if needed) / `moveView` (projected views stay aligned and follow their parent); `autoDims` (overall width + height on Front, depth on Top, each distinct visible diameter once as `nX Ø…`, leaders out of the view); `compute` (paper-space primitives: border, title block with part name / scale / units / date / projection, views, dimensions); `toSVG` (real size, one `<g data-layer>` per view), `toDXF` (R12, one layer per view + DIMENSIONS + SHEET, HIDDEN linetype, CIRCLE / SOLID / TEXT), `toPDF` (1.4, one page, Helvetica WinAnsi, ASCII only).
- `drawing.js` UI: *File ▸ Make Drawing from Part*; a *Drawing* ribbon tab (Standard 3 View, Model View, Projected View greyed, Smart Dimension, Model Items, Sheet Properties, Save as SVG / DXF / PDF, Close Drawing) that replaces the part tabs while a drawing is open; the drawing FeatureManager (*Draw1*, *Annotations*, *Sheet1* ▸ *Sheet Format1*, *Drawing View1…n* with orientation badges and the part under each; double-click for properties, right-click menus); the sheet over the viewport with pan / wheel zoom / F to fit, view dragging, Smart Dimension on edges and circles, Model View PropertyManager then click-to-place, Sheet Properties modal (name, format, scale, projection), Delete, drawing menus and *Window* switching, part shortcuts swallowed. Drawings live on the model (`model.drawings`), re-render after every rebuild, and every edit is one undo step. `anvil.drawing.{make, open, close, isOpen, summary, standard3View, addView, setSheet, modelItems, svg, dxf, pdf, doc}`.
- Hooks in existing files (additive): `ui-panels.js` exports `UI.RIBBON` and consults `UI.ribbonTabsFor(names, extra)` and `UI.menuHook(menus)`; `index.html` loads `drawing.css` and `drawing.js`. Drawing icons are added by wrapping `Icons` in `drawing.js` (icons.js untouched).
- Tests: kernel 365 → 379 (group `drawings`, including the Design Library check on *Plate with bolt holes*: extents 100×8 / 100×60 / 60×8 and the isometric from its corners, Front and Right 4 visible + 4 hidden, Top 4 lines + 4 Ø8 circles, A3 at 2:1, dimensions `100, 8, 60, 4X Ø8`; and an independent Möller–Trumbore ray cast from 2100+ points on drawn pieces of four parts in four views). Smoke 53 → 61 (drawing steps). README *Drawings* section.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 379/379, ui-smoke 61/61, app page loads with no console errors. Outside the suite: the DXFs of the plate and bushing drawings pass `ezdxf.recover` with 0 errors / 0 fixes (layers FRONT / TOP / RIGHT / ISOMETRIC / DIMENSIONS / SHEET, HIDDEN linetype), and PyMuPDF opens the PDFs at 1190.55 × 841.89 pt with the title block and `4X Ø8` text extracted.

**Decisions.**
- Hidden-line removal works on the mesh edges, not on a rendered image; the tolerances are 1e-9 × the part diagonal for the in-front / inside tests (a split point lands within ~1e-7 of the analytic one).
- Smooth-edge threshold 23° (not the viewport's 22°): the kernel's 16-gon circles turn 22.5°, and at 22° every facet line of a Ø8 hole was drawn.
- Tangent edges are not drawn (SolidWorks "Tangent Edges Removed"); isometric views default to Hidden Lines Removed.
- Drawings are stored on the part JSON so save / autosave / undo carry them; a separate `.drawing` file type is left for the document work in the assembly PR.
- User-placed Smart Dimensions store view-space geometry, so they do not follow a later part change; model dimensions are recomputed every render.

**Known gaps.** No Projected, Section, Detail or Auxiliary views; no hole callouts with depth, no ordinate / baseline dimensioning, no dimension dragging. The Funnel's shell leaves sliver faces in the mesh, which show as extra lines in its drawing (a kernel issue, not the projector). Leaders can cross the part on busy views.

**Merge with PR #3 (assemblies, cloud-2026-09-25c).** Merged origin/main into `cloud/drawings`; conflicts in QUEUE, README, ROADMAP, SESSION_LOG, both test pages and `ui-panels.js` resolved keeping both sides: `refreshRibbon` takes the assembly/part tab set and extra tabs, then `UI.ribbonTabsFor` (drawing) may swap them; `MENUS()` still passes through `UI.menuHook`. Coexistence: `drawing.js` treats assemblies as undrawable (*Make Drawing from Part* disabled, `anvil.drawing.make()` returns null with a status message, `doc()` is null for an assembly model), the Window menu lists the open part or assembly (checked) plus the part's drawings, the drawing File menu keeps the part menu's New / New ▸ Part / New ▸ Assembly / Open / Save group (taken by position: labels with ▸ / … decode differently under a server without a charset), and loading an assembly closes an open drawing. The "every example draws" kernel test skips `kind: 'assembly'` examples; a new smoke step covers the assembly example. After the merge: kernel-tests 408/408, ui-smoke 75/75, app page with no console errors (headless Chromium, port 8765).

**Next up.**
1. Drawings of assemblies (a Make Drawing from Assembly using the solved component meshes).
2. Drawings: Projected View and Section View; dimensions that follow the part (reference model edges instead of view coordinates).
3. Configurations: per-configuration feature suppression and a design-table grid.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25e — Assembly follow-ups: Distance / Angle mates, Move / Rotate Component, Interference Detection

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. Task given by the user: the known gaps of run cloud-2026-09-25c. Another cloud session has an open PR adding 2D drawings that also edits `ui-*.js`, so most new code went into `assembly.js` and the `ui-*.js` edits are small and additive; `csg.js`, `kernel.js` and `drawing.js` untouched. One agent, no subagents. Baseline on 870ce97: kernel 394/394, smoke 66/66. Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` because cdnjs is blocked (runner only, no repo change).

**Done.**
- `assembly.js`: mate types `distance` (`distance` ≥ 0) and `angle` (`angle` 0–180°), Coincident `offset` (≥ 0), `flip` on all three (*Flip dimension*). Plane mates hold a signed gap target (B's plane `±d` along A's normal); the greedy step translates to the target. Angle residual = signed angle from A's direction to s·B's about a fixed angle axis k minus the target: k is the line normal to both directions when the mate is placed (sign made canonical, stored in A's local frame, so re-solving a solved assembly measures the same side and a flip moves it across and stays), or the hinge axis earlier mates leave the moving component when the directions are parallel. Greedy turns about the hinge (or k) and marks a hinged component fully oriented. Value errors (*Distance must be a number ≥ 0*, *Angle must be between 0° and 180°*, *Offset must be a number ≥ 0*, *Distance needs two planar faces or planes*). `setMateValues`, `MATE_TYPES`, Builder `a.distance(a, b, d, opts)` / `a.angle(a, b, deg, opts)`, `generateScript` writes `distance / angle / offset / flip`, `isAssemblyScript` knows the new calls. `moveComponent / rotateComponent / componentCenter` (refuse fixed components). `interference(model, build?, { minVolume })`: pairwise `CSG.intersect` of the world bodies with a bounding-box prefilter, tolerance 1e-9 · diagonal³, largest first, common solid returned; touching faces and failed parts are not interferences.
