- UI (small, additive): Mate PM *Standard Mates* gains Distance and Angle with value boxes (start at the measured distance / angle), Offset for Coincident, Flip dimension; tree icons for Distance / Angle. Move Component / Rotate Component ribbon buttons (were placeholders) open a PM (drag Move / Rotate, By Delta XYZ + Apply on the selected component). Viewport drag: `ui-sketch.js` asks `UI.componentDragHook` on a left press; in an assembly with no command (or Move/Rotate open) a press on a floating component drags it in the screen plane (or turns it about its centre), re-solving the mates each animation frame, one undo step on drop; a click still selects; a fixed component falls through to view rotation with a status message. Interference Detection button (Evaluate tab, was a placeholder) opens a PM listing *InterferenceN — volume / A · B*, highlights the interfering components, draws the common volumes in red on top (depthTest off), a click on a result shows that pair only, Calculate re-runs. `anvil.moveComponent / rotateComponent / interference`, `anvil.mates()` rows carry the values. Five icons (distance, angle, moveComponent, rotateComponent, interference), `.intf-*` styles.
- Under-defined components: the tree already showed `(-)` only while `underDefined` and nothing once fully defined; this run pins it with tests on the new fully defined example (and `(-) Pin<1>` on the Pinned plate).
- Examples: **Distance and angle mates** (base 100 × 10 × 60 fixed; block 20³ dropped in tilted, placed by Distance 15 + Coincident offset 30 + flipped Distance 10 at [30, 25, 50], identity; bar 40 × 4 × 10 on the top, Concentric with the base Y axis + Angle 30° at [0, 10, 0], Ry(−30°); all fully defined; volume 69600) and **Interference check** (block sunk 5 into a 60 × 10 × 40 base by a flipped Coincident offset, a Ø8 pin standing on the top, one sunk 8 into it; interferences 2000 and 8·A(4) = 391.868; `interference` field on the example entry, checked by the example loops).
- Tests: kernel group `assembly mates & interference` ×24 plus 5 via the example loops (394 → 423); smoke ×8 (66 → 74), including a real synthetic mouse drag in the Front view (x follows by 40 px · scale, y held by the mate, one undo step), a drag on the fixed base (view rotates, model unchanged, a click selects), both new ribbon buttons, the Interference PM rows / highlights / row selection, and the Mate PM Distance / Angle / Flip flow.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 423/423, ui-smoke 74/74, app page loads with no console errors; screenshots of the Interference PM (two red common volumes, pairs highlighted) and the Angle mate edit checked by eye.

**Merged with main after PR #4 (cloud-2026-09-25d, 2D drawings).** Conflicts in SESSION_LOG, ROADMAP, QUEUE and kernel-tests.html resolved keeping both sides (log entries in run order, both test groups). `UI.ribbonTabsFor` (drawing.js) only removes the Drawing tab outside a drawing, so the assembly Assembly / Evaluate tabs and the new Move / Rotate Component and Interference Detection buttons are unchanged (new smoke step pins it, and that Make Drawing refuses an assembly); the drawing tests skip every `kind: 'assembly'` example, the two new ones included. After the merge: kernel-tests 437/437, ui-smoke 84/84, app page loads with no console errors.

**Decisions.**
- Angle is a signed one-DOF constraint (about k), not an unsigned dot product: an unsigned residual cannot tell +30° from −30°, so Flip dimension would never move anything.
- Dragging re-solves live, like SolidWorks: the component only moves along the DOF its mates leave. Preview frames rebuild with `noSave`; the drop restores the pre-drag model and commits the final transform through the normal undo path.
- Interference ignores touching faces (SolidWorks' "treat coincidence as interference" off) via a volume tolerance, and checks the whole assembly (no component selection yet).

**Known gaps.** No Parallel / Perpendicular / Tangent / Width mates; Distance only between planes (not between cylinders). Rotate drag turns about the view axes only (no About Entity). Interference has no "selected components" subset, no "treat coincidence as interference" option, and runs on the main thread (fine for the examples: < 15 ms). Components are still copies; no exploded view or sub-assemblies. The `▸` glyph mojibake under `python -m http.server` (no `<meta charset>` in index.html) is still there.

**Next up.**
1. Drawings of assemblies (Make Drawing from Assembly from the solved component meshes).
2. Assemblies: Parallel / Perpendicular / Tangent mates, Distance between cylinders, Linear Component Pattern, Exploded View.
3. Interference Detection on selected components with a clearance option (Clearance Verification).

## Run cloud-2026-09-25f — Drawing views: Projected, Section, Detail; dimensions that follow the part

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: the known gaps of cloud-2026-09-25d — Projected View, Section View with hatching and A-A labels, Detail View with its own scale, Smart Dimensions that follow part changes. Another cloud session has an open assembly-mates PR on `assembly.js` and bits of `ui-*.js`, so all code is in `drawing.js` / `drawing.css`; `csg.js`, `kernel.js`, `assembly.js` and the `ui-*.js` files are untouched. Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` (cdnjs is blocked; harness only). Baseline on 26295fd: kernel 408/408 after a harness fix (below), smoke 75/75.

**Done (drawing.js engine).**
- View frames: a view is a named orientation or a stored frame `{ e, u, v }`; `projectedFrame(parent, 'up' | 'down' | 'left' | 'right', projection)` folds out a view the way third angle does (first angle mirrored), vertical neighbours share u, horizontal ones v, so they line up; a frame equal to a standard one takes its name (Front + down = Bottom), otherwise it is *Projected* (up from Top is the back upside down).
- `addProjectedView`, `addSectionView`, `addDetailView`, `deleteView` (removes the section / detail views cut from a view; projected children become free), `nextLabel` (A…Z without I, O, Q), `detailScale` (2x the parent, standard when possible). `moveView` / `layout` carry aligned children (and theirs) with the parent.
- `sectionOf(mesh, e, w)`: exact section by CSG — every body's polygons (from `mesh.faces[].polys`) intersected with a box that ends on the plane; cap faces = faces on the plane facing the viewer, one per body; cached on the mesh. Section views are centred on the whole part's extents in their frame so they stay aligned with the parent; hidden lines removed by default.
- ANSI31 hatch (`hatchLines`): 45° (135° on odd bodies), 2.5 mm paper spacing anchored to the sheet, even-odd over the cap loops (`faceOutline`), so holes stay open. Parent gets a thick chain line past the view, arrows along the line of sight, the letters; the view gets SECTION A-A.
- Detail views: the parent's content clipped to the circle (`clipProjection`: chords, circles cut into arcs), its caps re-hatched at the detail's scale inside the circle; circle and letter on the parent; DETAIL B / SCALE 4:1.
- `reattachDim`: user Smart Dimensions keep view geometry and are reattached every render — unchanged edge; else best of collinear-overlapping and parallel edges (within a quarter of the view, overlap ≥ half the union) by (1 − overlap/union) + offset/reach; circles by centre then by radius. No match → `dangling`, drawn olive (#a07c00, DXF 42, PDF RGB); undo reattaches. Every projection now also keeps `all` / `circlesAll` (visible + hidden, whole) for this.
- Writers: `arc` primitive (SVG path A, DXF ARC, PDF Béziers), `chain` style (SVG dash, DXF `PHANTOM` linetype, PDF dash), `hatch` style, per-primitive colour.

**Done (drawing.js UI).** Drawing ribbon: *Projected View* (was greyed), *Section View*, *Detail View* with icons; Insert ▸ Drawing View ▸ Projected / Section… / Detail; view context menu *Projected View*. Projected: parent = selected view or next click, preview snaps to the side the pointer is on, aligned. Section: PM (Cutting Line Horizontal / Vertical, Label), click places the line (snaps to circle centres within 8 px), click on a side places the view (viewer side in third angle). Detail: centre, radius, place, with circle / ghost previews. Tree rows with section / detail / projected icons and `A-A` / `B 4:1` badges; view properties hide Orientation for dependent views; `anvil.drawing.projectedView / sectionView / detailView / deleteView`; `summary()` has `kind`, `label`, `parent`, `hatch`, `sectionArea`, `dangling`.

**Tests.** kernel 408 → 418 (group `drawing views`): projected frames from every orientation; aligned Bottom / Back views that slide only along the projection, follow the parent and survive a re-layout; exact section areas (box 800; plate through its holes 6000 − 4·polyCircleArea(4); the dV/dc slope; bushing annulus as 48-gons and axial section = 2 × profile = 274; Two-body clamp one cap per body); Section A-A (alignment, 4 hole circles, every hatch line at 45° inside the plate and outside the holes, hatched length × spacing = paper area within 2 %, chain line, arrows, letters); an independent ray cast on a section view; first-angle placement; Detail View (4:1, chords 2√44, the arc's ends on the detail circle, detail of a section hatched inside the circle); labels and dependent deletion; dimensions following a wider / taller plate and a bigger / moved hole, dangling and reattaching, a moved edge beating a collinear blind-hole leftover; exports (SVG layers and hatch, DXF PHANTOM + ARC, PDF chain dash). Smoke 75 → 79: Projected View with the mouse (aligned, sideways drag refused), Section View PM + clicks (snapped cut through the Top view holes, area 672, hatch in SVG, chain line), Detail View clicks + undo / redo, Smart Dimensions following a thicker plate and dangling in olive when the holes are deleted.

**Harness fix.** `kernel-tests.html` worker test: its 8 s timeout started while the page was still running ~8 s of synchronous tests, so on this container it fired before the worker's queued reply (407/408 at baseline). The timer now starts once the page is idle.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 418/418, ui-smoke 79/79, app page loads with no console errors. Outside the suite: DXFs of plate, bushing and two-body clamp sheets with section, detail and projected views pass `ezdxf.recover` with 0 errors / 0 fixes (layers SECTION_A / DETAIL_B, PHANTOM linetype, ARC entities), and PyMuPDF opens the PDFs at 1190.55 × 841.89 pt with SECTION A-A / DETAIL B / SCALE text extracted; rendered sheets checked by eye.


**Merge with PR #5 (assembly mates, cloud-2026-09-25e).** Merged origin/main into `cloud/drawing-views`; conflicts only in QUEUE and SESSION_LOG, resolved keeping both entries. After the merge: kernel-tests 447/447, ui-smoke 88/88, app page with no console errors (headless Chromium, port 8765).

**Decisions.**
- Sections are cut with CSG from the mesh's own polygons (no kernel change, no second rebuild) and cached per plane on the mesh.
- The section view is placed on the viewer's side in third angle (arrows point away from it, like SolidWorks); first angle mirrors it.
- Detail views have no border (SolidWorks' ANSI default); the circle and letter are on the parent.
- Dimension reattachment works on the 2D projection (edges whole, visible or hidden), not on 3D topology: robust for grown / moved faces, and a dimension whose geometry is truly gone dangles rather than jumping to something unrelated.

**Known gaps.** Cutting-line snapping only snaps to circle centres (not to hole axes seen side-on). No aligned / offset / half sections, no auxiliary views, no broken-out sections. Detail views of detail views are refused. Section views of assemblies do not exist (drawings are parts only). Projected views keep the frame they were made with when the sheet's projection type changes.

**Next up.**
1. Drawings of assemblies (Make Drawing from Assembly from the solved component meshes; per-component hatch angles already alternate by body).
2. Drawings: auxiliary view, broken-out section, hole callouts with depth, ordinate dimensions.
3. Configurations: per-configuration feature suppression and a design-table grid.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25h — Drawings of assemblies: BOM, Auto Balloon, section hatch per component

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: drawings of assemblies (refused until now) — Make Drawing from Assembly, Standard 3 View / Model View with hidden lines across components, Section views with a hatch angle per component, a Bill of Materials anchored to the sheet, Auto Balloon linked to BOM item numbers, exports; plus a Phase 7 gap list in ROADMAP. Another cloud session is editing `kernel.js` / `csg.js` for mesh quality, so neither was touched; all new code is in `drawing.js` (engine + UI), with one additive line in `ui-panels.js` (`UI.ASM_RIBBON` export, so the Drawing tab exists in assembly documents). `assembly.js` and `drawing.css` needed no change. Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` (cdnjs blocked; runner only). Baseline on e068dde: kernel 447/447, smoke 88/88, app page 0 console errors.

**Done (drawing.js engine).**
- Assemblies are drawn from the solved assembly mesh (`Kernel.meshFromBody(Assembly.rebuild(m).body)`, one body per component), so every existing view type works on them. `meshEdges` now keys edge pieces per body: a pin standing in a hole no longer pairs its facet edges with the hole's (four faces made them "sharp" and drew every facet). Projected lines carry their body (`[u1, v1, u2, v2, body]`, circles `.body`) through `mergeCollinear` / `extractCircles` / `clipProjection`; a hidden piece under a visible piece of another body on the same line is dropped.
- `hatchStyle(body)`: 45°, 135°, 30°, 120°, 60°, 150°, then the same 1.6× wider apart; section and detail views hatch each cap with its body's style; hatch prims carry `body`; each sheet view has `hatches: [{ body, angle, spacing, lines }]`. Multi-body parts keep 45° / 135° for bodies 0 / 1.
- `bomRows(model, mesh?)`: one row per distinct component source, items in tree order, PART NUMBER = part name, DESCRIPTION = part `description` → Design Library entry name → local size `30 x 5 x 20` (first instance's world vertices taken back into the part frame). `bomBox(doc, units, n)`: 157 × 7·(n+1) mm table at a border corner (`top-right` default, `top-left`, `bottom-right` above the title block, `bottom-left`) or at a dragged `doc.bom.x / y`. `newDrawing` gives assemblies `bom: { show, anchor }` and `autoDims: false`. `layout` treats the table as a keep-out: views centred, else packed away from it, at the largest scale where no view box meets it; the isometric's box shrinks away from it.
- `autoBalloon(doc, model, viewId, { ignoreMultiple })` stores `{ key: source, component }` per item (or per instance); `balloonSpots` finds at render time, per balloon, the visible edge midpoint (or circle point) of that component farthest from the view centre, puts the balloon where the ray from the centre meets a rectangle 14 mm outside the view, and spreads neighbours along that rectangle. Item numbers come from the BOM at every render (components removed / added renumber / raise QTY).
- `compute` draws the BOM (lines + text on layer `BOM`, text truncated with "..." to its cell) and balloons (circle, leader, arrowhead, number on `BALLOONS`); sheet gets `bom: { rows, box }` and `balloons`. DXF: `BOM` / `BALLOONS` layer colours, `×` → `x` and other non-ASCII → `?` in TEXT; PDF: `×` as WinAnsi \327.

**Done (drawing.js UI).** *File ▸ Make Drawing from Assembly* (was a disabled *from Part*) makes Draw1 with Standard 3 View + BOM; Drawing tab gains *Auto Balloon* (PM: view, Ignore multiple instances) and *Bill of Materials* (PM: Table Position anchor, or where it was dragged); *Insert ▸ Annotations ▸ Auto Balloon*, *Insert ▸ Tables ▸ Bill of Materials…*, view menu *Auto Balloon*. Click a balloon or the table to select it (Delete removes; the BOM drags off its anchor, double-click opens its PM); tree rows *Bill of Materials1* (n items) and a *Balloons* row under each ballooned view; view rows show the assembly; the sheet tab / Window menu say *(assembly)*. `anvil.drawing.autoBalloon(viewId?, opts)`, `bom(anchor | { x, y } | null)`, `deleteBalloons(viewId)`; `summary()` adds `bom`, `balloons`, and per view `hatches` (with component names) and `balloons`.

**Tests.** kernel 447 → 455 (group `assembly drawings`): BOM rows of the three Design Library assemblies (Pinned plate: Plate 1 / Pin 2 / Cap 1; Distance and angle: Base / Block / Bar; Interference check: Base / Block / Pin 2) with descriptions; Standard 3 View extents = solved bbox (Pinned 100×20 / 100×60 / 60×20, Distance and angle 105×45 / 105×70 / 70×45 with the 30° bar reaching x = −5, Interference 60×30 / 60×40 / 40×30); BOM at the border corner with every view clear, for all four anchors on all three; hidden lines across components (hole under the cap a hidden circle, pin side hidden 0..13 and visible 13..20, no pin facet lines) plus 270 independent ray casts; Auto Balloon (3 per example, number = item, attach point on a visible edge of that component, outside the view, not overlapping; per-instance 1,2,2,3; Front view too); renumbering after removing the plate and QTY 3 after a third pin; Section through the pins: caps 672 + 160 + 160 (= 800 with the pins filling the holes), hatch 45 / 135 / 30 per component, every hatch line at its component's angle and inside its own cut face, hatched length × spacing = area; 12 distinct hatch styles; exports (16 BOM cells, 10 BOM lines, 3 balloon circles in DXF; SVG layers; PDF text). Smoke 88 → 91: the old "assembly refuses Make Drawing" step is replaced by three steps — Make Drawing from Assembly through the File menu (extents, BOM rows, anchor, tree row, ribbon buttons, Window), Auto Balloon through the ribbon PM + mouse-select / Delete / undo of a balloon, a third pin raising QTY, BOM PM Bottom Left, BOM mouse drag and Delete / undo, then Section View through the pins (hatch angles by component name) and SVG / DXF / PDF layers; the Interference-check step now makes its drawing (Base 1, Block 1, Pin 2).

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 455/455, ui-smoke 91/91, app page loads with no console errors. Outside the suite: DXFs of all three assembly sheets (Standard 3 View + Section A-A + balloons) pass `ezdxf.recover` with 0 errors / 0 fixes (layers BOM, BALLOONS, SECTION_A…; 16 BOM TEXTs, 3 balloon CIRCLE / TEXT / LINE / SOLID), PyMuPDF opens the PDFs at 1190.55 × 841.89 pt with ITEM NO. / PART NUMBER / DESCRIPTION / QTY. and the rows extracted; sheets and the app's drawing view checked by eye. origin/main had nothing new since e068dde, so the pre-PR merge was a no-op.

**Decisions.**
- BOM identity is the component source (same Design Library name / same script text / same embedded model), the analogue of SolidWorks' file + configuration; the BOM is computed at render, never stored, so it cannot go stale.
- Balloons attach to an edge with an arrow (SolidWorks' edge attachment) at the component's visible edge farthest from the view centre, which keeps leaders short and outward; balloons are not draggable yet.
- Make Drawing from Assembly inserts the BOM (SolidWorks asks); `bom: false` / Delete leaves it out. Assembly drawings start without model items.
- The BOM is a layout keep-out, so Standard 3 View may choose one scale smaller than it would without the table.

**Known gaps.** No manual Balloon, balloon dragging or stacked balloons; BOM columns are fixed (no custom properties, no indented / parts-only BOM); sections cut every component (no excluded components such as pins); no exploded views. The `▸` / `°` mojibake under `python -m http.server` (no `<meta charset>` in index.html and the test pages) is still there.

**Next up.**
1. Phase 7 (ROADMAP): sketch constraint solver; true curves in the kernel (coordinate with the mesh-quality session).
2. Drawings: balloon dragging / manual Balloon, excluded components in sections, exploded views.
3. Assemblies: Parallel / Perpendicular / Tangent mates, sub-assemblies, component patterns.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25i — Sketch constraint solver: relations, driving dimensions, Under / Fully / Over Defined

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: ROADMAP Phase 7 item 1, the sketch constraint solver. Another cloud session is editing `kernel.js` / `csg.js` for mesh quality, so neither was touched: the solver is a new file `anvil/sketch-solver.js` (global `SketchSolver`, no DOM) that hooks the Builder and `generateScript` from outside, and its UI is a new file `anvil/ui-relations.js`; `ui-sketch.js`, `ui-core.js`, `ui-commands.js`, `ui-panels.js` got focused hook edits. Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` (cdnjs blocked; runner only). Baseline on 56d30df: kernel 455/455, smoke 91/91, app page 0 console errors.

**Done (sketch-solver.js).**
- Sketch JSON: `rels: [{ type, refs, v?, off?, at? }]`. Refs: `"O"` origin, `"3"` whole entity, `"3.p1"` / `"3.p2"` / `"3.mid"`, `"3.c"` centre, `"3.v0"` corner, `"3.e0"` rectangle / polygon / polyline edge. Types: coincident, horizontal, vertical, parallel, perpendicular, tangent, concentric, equal, midpoint, collinear, fix, and the driving dimensions distance and angle. Old models have no `rels` and are untouched.
- Solver: every entity parameter is an unknown (arc angles in degrees); relations and the existing Smart Dimensions (`dims`, via `Kernel.dimValue`) are residual equations with sign / branch choices frozen from the starting geometry; Levenberg-Marquardt with minimum-norm steps `-Jᵀ(JJᵀ+λI)⁻¹r` (normal equations when equations outnumber unknowns), central-difference Jacobian per entity; round-off snapped (20.00000000000126 → 20) only on values that moved. Dimensions drive at their current value unless an edit gives a new one.
- Analysis: row-by-row reduction of J → rank, DOF, redundant relations (rows add no rank; yellow), conflicts (unsatisfied; red, geometry left as it was), per-entity fully / under defined from whether every unknown is pinned by the row space; sketch status Under / Fully / Over Defined.
- Tangent at a shared end point (a Coincident joins the ends) is written as "radius ⟂ line" (or "radii collinear") at that point: the distance form has a zero gradient there and would read as redundant with the Coincident.
- `setDim` (kernel `setDimValue` as the first guess, then a whole-sketch solve; refused and unchanged on conflict), `setRelValue`, `measure`, `applicable` (Add Relations list), `addRelation` (captures `v` / `at`), `deleteRelation`, `drag` (least-squares pull with a weak weight, then an exact minimum-norm projection onto the relations), `infer` / `inferPreview` (coincident / concentric / midpoint where a point lands exactly on another point or the origin, horizontal / vertical for level lines; an inferred relation that is not independent is skipped), `removeEntity` / `remap` / `clean` so relations follow deletes, trims and entity type changes.
- Script round trip without kernel.js: `rel(type, refs, value | { value, off } | { at })` and `solve()` are added to the SketchBuilder prototype; `Kernel.generateScript` is wrapped to insert `.rel(...)` lines before each sketch's `.id();`. A model without relations writes the same script as before.

**Done (UI).** `ui-relations.js`: relation icons (new green glyphs in `icons.js`) next to entities, one per coincident point, yellow / red when over-defining; click selects, Delete removes; View ▸ Sketch Relations toggles them. Yellow inference icons next to the entity being drawn. Add Relation (Sketch ribbon, Tools ▸ Relations ▸ Add…): pick entities / points (point within 7 px, else the entity or rectangle edge), Selected Entities, Existing Relations, a button per applicable relation. Display/Delete Relations (the ribbon placeholder, Tools ▸ Relations): every relation with Satisfied / Over Defining / Not Solved, filters All / Overdefining/Not Solved / Selected Entities, Delete and Delete All. Smart Dimension: a second entity or point makes a distance (angle for two non-parallel lines) dimension, placed with a third click, drawn and edited in the same Modify box. Every dimension edit re-solves the whole sketch (`SketchSolver.setDim`); a conflicting value is refused with the reason. Select-tool drag on an entity or point drags it through the solver (`UI.sketchDragHook`, before the assembly component hook); fully defined geometry does not move. Entity PM edits re-solve with the edited entity weighted. `ui-core.js`: active-sketch entities coloured by state (under blue, fully black / light grey in the dark theme, over yellow, conflict red), `App.sketchState` refreshed on every sketch redraw; `ui-panels.js`: Under Defined / Fully Defined / Over Defined in the status bar with the DOF as tooltip. Inference on every entity drawn (`UI.addEntity`), relations restored by Cancel sketch, shifted on entity delete, remapped after Trim and Mirror (not copy). `anvil.relations()`, `anvil.addRelation()`, `anvil.deleteRelation()`, `anvil.sketchStatus()`, `UI.editRelDim()`. Example *Constrained L profile* (six lines drawn by eye, 13 relations + 4 dimensions, Fully Defined, 12750).

**Tests.** kernel 455 → 471: group `sketch solver` (13): DOF counts per entity type; the constrained rectangle from four sloppy lines (DOF 2 → 0, exact 40 × 20); a dimension edit re-solving it (55); tangent arc-line with shared end (distance = r, radius ⟂ line, DOF 6) and external circle tangency; equal circles driven by one diameter (r = 10, 10, DOF 4); redundant Parallel (yellow), conflicting distance (red, geometry unchanged), refused dimension edit; perpendicular / collinear / midpoint / concentric / vertical points / fix + equal / angle 30 → 45; distance dimensions; dragging; inference; relations after deletion and `clean`; `.rel()` script round trip (fixed point, no `.rel` for plain models, inapplicable relation throws); solving every Design Library sketch moves nothing. Plus the new example and its round trip. Smoke 91 → 97: six steps driving real mouse events (see README *Tests*). Harness fix: `kernel-tests.html` now arms the 10 s async timeout once the page is idle (the new synchronous tests pushed the worker test's reply past a timer armed at declaration).

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 471/471, ui-smoke 97/97, app page loads with no console errors; the README relation example run in Node (over-defined distance flagged as conflict, geometry 60 × 20, volume 12000); screenshot of a sketch with inferred relations, a redundant Parallel (yellow lines and icons, Over Defined in the status bar) and a yellow inference icon checked by eye. origin/main had nothing new since 56d30df, so the pre-PR merge was a no-op.

**Decisions.**
- Relations live next to `dims` in the sketch feature; `dims` keep their kernel meaning and become driving through the solver, so drawings and old code that read `dims` are unaffected. Two-entity dimensions are relations with a value (`distance`, `angle`) because `Kernel.dimGeometry` only knows single entities and kernel.js was off limits.
- Numeric Jacobian (central differences) instead of hand derivatives: sketches are small, and every relation stays one residual function.
- An over-defining relation is kept and flagged, as SolidWorks shows it; a conflicting dimension *edit* is refused, so the sketch never jumps to a least-squares compromise.
- Inside a sketch, undo still restores the state before the sketch session (one undo step per sketch edit, as before).

**Known gaps.** No relations on slot sides or ellipse / spline (not in the kernel); no Symmetric, Pierce, Merge or Intersection relations; no driven (reference) dimensions or the "make driven?" prompt; Smart Dimension on a point pair starts from an entity click (clicking a point first dimensions its entity if the second click is not another entity); inference only on exact snaps (no tangent or parallel inference while drawing); the `▸` / `°` / `⌀` mojibake under `python -m http.server` (no `<meta charset>`) is still there.

**Next up.**
1. Phase 7: true curves in the kernel (coordinate with the mesh-quality session).
2. Sketch: Symmetric relation, driven dimensions, tangent / perpendicular inference while drawing an arc or line from an end point, construction toggle.
3. Drawings: balloon dragging / manual Balloon, excluded components in sections, exploded views.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25j — Materials, custom properties, property-linked title block and BOM columns

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: ROADMAP Phase 7 "Custom properties and materials" plus the editable BOM item. Another session is editing `kernel.js` / `csg.js`, so neither was touched: the library, mass and properties live in a new `anvil/materials.js` (global `Materials`, no DOM) that hooks the part / assembly Builders and `generateScript` from outside, the UI in a new `anvil/ui-materials.js`; `drawing.js` got the title block and BOM changes; `assembly.js`, `ui-core.js`, `ui-panels.js`, `ui-app.js` got small hook edits.

**Done (materials.js).**
- Library in six classes (Steel, Stainless Steel, Aluminium Alloys, Copper Alloys, Titanium Alloys, Plastics), 23 materials: Plain Carbon Steel, AISI 1020 / 1045 / 4130, Galvanized, AISI 304 / 316, 17-4 PH, 1060, 5052-H32, 6061-T6 (SS), 7075-T6 (SN), Brass, Copper, Aluminium Bronze, CP Titanium, Ti-6Al-4V, ABS, PLA, Nylon 6/6, Acetal (POM), Polycarbonate, Acrylic (PMMA); density, E, Poisson, tensile, yield, colour. `find` takes aliases ("6061", "delrin", the old task-pane names).
- `model.material` (applying sets `density` and `color`; a typed density that differs unlinks it), `model.bodyMaterials = { featureId: name }` for per-body materials; `massProps(model, build?, mesh?)` = Kernel.massProps fields + `massKg`, `bodies[]` (per-body volume / centroid integrated from the combined mesh's body-tagged faces, mass-weighted centroid); identical to Kernel.massProps without body materials. `bodyColors` for the viewport.
- `model.properties = { custom: [...], config: { cfg: [...] } }`; `evaluate` replaces `"SW-Material"`, `"SW-Mass"` (g / lb), `"SW-Density"`, `"SW-Volume"`, `"SW-Surface Area"`, `"SW-File Name"`, `"SW-Configuration Name"` (an `@file` suffix is ignored); Configuration Specific rows override Custom ones; `sheetProperty` falls back to the material / mass for Material / Mass.
- Script: `p.material`, `p.bodyMaterial(fN, name)`, `p.property(name, value, { type, config })`, `a.property`; `generateScript` writes them (and `p.density` / `p.color` when not the defaults or the material's). Design Library scripts are unchanged.
- `Assembly.massProps` (small edit in assembly.js) weighs a component with its part's body materials and reports `material` per component.

**Done (drawings).** Title block regridded into 11 fields (TITLE / PART NO. / REV, DWG. NO. / MATERIAL / SCALE / UNITS, DATE / MASS / PROJECTION / SHEET), each a text with `$PRPSHEET:"…"` (the model's properties) and `$PRP:"SW-…"` (the drawing's) links, shrink-to-fit; `doc.titleBlock` overrides, edited in Sheet Properties (one undo step with the sheet change). BOM: `doc.bom.columns` (item / partNumber / description / qty / property, with title, width, hidden), `doc.bom.type` 'top' | 'parts'; PART NUMBER / DESCRIPTION use the Part Number / Description properties; Parts only lists a multi-body part's bodies (own material and mass, identical bodies grouped); BOMs on part drawings; the PM has BOM Type, Columns checkboxes and Add Column; balloons fall back to the first row of their part. `compute(doc, mesh, model, { build })` takes the build for masses.

**Done (UI).** Edit Material dialog (class tree, Properties table, Appearance, Apply / Remove Material / Close) from the Material tree row (double-click; right-click favourites), a body row's *Material ▸* entries and the task pane; per-body vertex colours in ui-core (assemblies: each component's part colour); Mass Properties shows materials per body / component; *File ▸ Properties…* dialog (Summary / Custom / Configuration Specific, preset names, link list, live Evaluated Value, default rows for a document without properties). `anvil.material / bodyMaterial / materials / properties / property / editMaterial / fileProperties`, `anvil.drawing.bomColumns / bomType / titleBlock`; summary gains `material`, `massKg`. Added `<meta charset="utf-8">` to index.html and the two test pages: the `▸` / `—` mojibake under `python -m http.server` is gone.

**Tests.** kernel 471 → 486 (group `materials & properties`, 15): library; box masses 135 / 425 / 51 / 390 g; bolt-hole plate (analytic 16-gon volume 46432.529 mm³) in 6061-T6 125.37 g, AISI 304 371.46 g, Nylon 6/6 52.93 g; inch part 0.098 lb; steel + ABS bodies 8.82 g with mass-weighted centroid; assembly 279.82 g with per-component and body materials; properties / configuration override / links; script round trips; unchanged example scripts; title block values and their text in SVG / DXF / PDF; BOM columns; Top-level vs Parts only on part and assembly drawings. ui-smoke 97 → 103.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765, three.js r128 from npm three@0.128.0).** kernel-tests 486/486, ui-smoke 103/103, app page loads with no console errors. Screenshots of the two-material part, Edit Material, File Properties and the drawing (Parts only BOM with MATERIAL / MASS / VENDOR, title block BRACKET / 6061-T6 (SS) / 203.00 g) checked by eye. origin/main had nothing new since d90cfa6, so the pre-PR merge was a no-op.

**Decisions.**
- Material names are stored, density and colour are copied onto the model: every existing density consumer keeps working untouched (kernel.js was off limits).
- Body materials are keyed by the id of the feature that created the body, not the body index, which shifts when bodies merge or vanish.
- Parts only, with no sub-assemblies yet, lists the bodies of a multi-body part (the cut-list view); Top-level only lists parts. When sub-assemblies arrive, Parts only should also flatten them.
- Title block and BOM property columns fall back to the material / mass (and TITLE / PART NO. to the name) so a part without File Properties still fills them; SolidWorks would leave them blank.
- SW-Mass shows grams (MMGS) or pounds (IPS), matching SolidWorks' document units.

**Known gaps.** No custom material library editing (user-defined materials) or appearance overrides separate from the material; no component-level material override in assemblies (a component's material is its part's); no indented BOM, revision table or general tables; drawings have no `$PRP` properties of their own beyond `doc.properties`; Date / Number / Yes or no types are stored but not validated.

**Next up.**
1. Phase 7: true curves in the kernel (coordinate with the mesh-quality session).
2. Assemblies: sub-assemblies (then a real Parts only / Indented BOM), component patterns.
3. Drawings: revision table, multiple sheets and editable sheet formats, balloon dragging / manual Balloon.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25k — Neutral formats: STEP AP214, 3MF, OBJ export; STEP / STL / OBJ / 3MF import; the Imported feature

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: ROADMAP Phase 7 "STEP / IGES / Parasolid import and export; 3MF / OBJ export". Other cloud sessions were editing `kernel.js` / `csg.js` (mesh quality), `materials.js` / `drawing.js` (materials) and `assembly.js` (assemblies), so none of those were touched: everything is in a new file `anvil/exchange.js` (global `Exchange`, no DOM), which hooks the kernel from outside; the UI got focused edits in `ui-app.js`, `ui-panels.js`, `ui-commands.js`, `icons.js`, `style.css`, `index.html` and `worker.js`.

**Done (exchange.js).**
- `bodyMesh`: every body as a watertight triangle mesh (weld on a 1e-6 grid, split the T-junctions CSG leaves, centroid fans for polygons with collinear points), with planar face groups. All six parts checked have 0 open edges and the kernel's volume.
- Export: `exportOBJ` (+ `exportMTL`), `export3MF` (stored zip written in JS with CRC32; objects per body, basematerials colour, units mm / inch; assemblies as a components object per part and a build item per component with transform and partnumber), `exportSTEP` (AP214 `AUTOMOTIVE_DESIGN`; one `MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP` per body over a `CLOSED_SHELL` of planar `ADVANCED_FACE`s with shared straight `EDGE_CURVE`s, split points shared by only two faces dropped; product named after the part; colour `STYLED_ITEM`; inch parts as `CONVERSION_BASED_UNIT('INCH')`; assemblies as a root product with one NAUO + `ITEM_DEFINED_TRANSFORMATION` per component, parts shared by source).
- Import: a Part 21 parser; `importSTEP` (MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP / FACETED_BREP / BREP_WITH_VOIDS / CLOSED_SHELL; planar ADVANCED_FACE / FACE_SURFACE / POLY_LOOP faces with holes by ear clipping + visibility-checked hole bridges; SURFACE_CURVE-wrapped lines; the context's length unit; every assembly placement path through REPRESENTATION_RELATIONSHIP_WITH_TRANSFORMATION and MAPPED_ITEM, instances named `Product<n>`; curved faces refused with a message naming them), `importSTL` (ASCII / binary, split into shells, voids kept with their host), `importOBJ` (n-gons, negative indices, `o` / `g` groups), `import3MF` (stored packages, components, transforms, units); all go through the same weld / T-junction repair, are turned outward and report open edges.
- The Imported feature: `{ type: 'import', name: 'Imported1', file, format, units, merge?, solids: [{ name, vertices, triangles }] }` with base64 Float64 / Uint32 arrays. **Hook** (kernel.js unchanged): `Kernel.rebuild` is wrapped — for each import feature it rebuilds the prefix up to it with the cache, puts the solids into that step's bodies, and passes the patched steps on as the cache, so the final pass builds every later feature against the imported bodies with the kernel's own code (patched steps are flagged so a reused cache never adds them twice; the caller's cache tail is reused when nothing before the last import changed). `Kernel.cacheFromBuild` flags worker-build caches; `Kernel.generateScript` writes `var fN = p.importMesh({…});` in the feature's place and `Builder.prototype.importMesh` reads it. `worker.js` loads exchange.js, so worker rebuilds build imports too.

**Done (UI).** *File ▸ Open* is now a dialog with *Files of type* (all / Anvil / STEP / STL / OBJ / 3MF) and a file input, plus the paste box (JSON, script, or STEP / ASCII STL / OBJ text); a neutral file opens as a new part named after it with Imported1. *File ▸ Save As* has *File name* and *Save as type* (Part or Assembly `.anvil.json`, script, STEP AP214, 3MF, OBJ + MTL, STL) and downloads; *File ▸ Export STEP AP214… / 3MF… / OBJ…* open it preset; *Export model as JSON…* keeps the copyable text dialog. *Insert ▸ Features ▸ Imported…* appends an Imported feature to the open part (one undo step). Tree icon `imported`, tooltip "Imported STEP from x.step, 1 solid", a PropertyManager (file, format, solids, triangles, *Merge result*, name). `anvil.exportStep / export3mf / exportObj / exportMtl / importFile / saveAs`.

**Tests.** kernel 471 → 484 (group `exchange: STEP / 3MF / OBJ / STL`, 13 tests; two OpenCascade-written STEP fixtures in `tests/fixtures/`), smoke 97 → 103 (6 steps). Details in README *Tests*.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765, three.js r128 from npm three@0.128.0 routed in place of the CDN).** kernel-tests 484/484, ui-smoke 103/103, app page loads with no console errors; the imported part, tree row and dialogs checked by eye in screenshots. Outside the suite (Python): OpenCascade 8 (OCP, FreeCAD's kernel) opens the STEP of Plate with bolt holes, L-bracket, Bushing, Two-body clamp (2 solids), Hex nut, Mug, an inch block, and the Pinned plate / Distance and angle assemblies as valid solids (`BRepCheck_Analyzer`) with the same volume (1e-6) and bbox; XCAF reads the assemblies as one assembly with components `Plate<1>, Pin<1>, Pin<2>, Cap<1>` / `Base<1>, Block<1>, Bar<1>`; lib3mf reads every 3MF as manifold and oriented with the right unit (inch for the inch block) and 4 build items for the Pinned plate, with the solved volume; trimesh reads every OBJ (watertight after merging by position, colour from the .mtl) and 3MF with the same volume. OpenCascade-written STEP (a planar block it modelled, 27875; its own re-write of both assemblies) imports with exact volumes and instance names; OCC's cylinder is refused with the curved-face message.

**Decisions.**
- STEP export is faceted-exact planar B-rep (ADVANCED_FACE on PLANE), not FACETED_BREP: CAD systems treat it as a normal solid with real edges; round faces go out as their facets until the kernel has true curves.
- An imported file is one feature with one body per solid (SolidWorks' Imported1); `merge` is opt-in. STEP assemblies import flattened into placed solids in a part (not an assembly document), named per occurrence.
- Imported meshes live in the feature as base64 arrays, so the model JSON, the undo stack and the rebuild keys stay cheap; plain arrays are accepted by the script API.
- The rebuild hook reuses the kernel's own prefix cache rather than reimplementing any feature; it can move into a kernel feature registry unchanged (`patchImportStep`) if one appears.
- New parts from a file open in mm unless the STEP / 3MF says inches (`units: 'auto'`); insertions scale to the open part's units.

**Known gaps.** No IGES / Parasolid; no curved STEP faces (import refuses, export facets); compressed 3MF not readable (writes are stored); a STEP assembly imports as a multi-body part; no re-import / "update from file" on the Imported feature; colours are not read on import; the `▸` / `°` mojibake under `python -m http.server` (no `<meta charset>`) is still there.

**Next up.**
1. Phase 7: true curves in the kernel (then exact cylinders in STEP both ways).
2. Exchange: open a STEP assembly as an assembly document (components + fixed placements); read colours; deflate for 3MF (DecompressionStream).
3. Drawings: balloon dragging / manual Balloon, excluded components in sections, exploded views.

**Merge with PR #9 (materials, cloud-2026-09-25j).** Merged origin/main (0c8ae61) into `cloud/exchange-formats`; conflicts in QUEUE, README, SESSION_LOG, kernel-tests.html and ui-smoke.html were both-sides appends, resolved keeping both (log entries and QUEUE in run order, both test groups, both README sections, the materials.js and exchange.js file rows). Exports now carry material: each body's material from `Materials.bodyMaterial` (body material → part material → part colour) feeds OBJ `usemtl` / MTL entries named after the material, 3MF `basematerials` (one base per material, `pindex` per body object) and a STEP `STYLED_ITEM` colour per solid; STEP product names unchanged; assembly components carry their parts' materials. New kernel test (materials in exports) and a smoke check. Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765): kernel-tests 500/500, ui-smoke 109/109, app page with no console errors; a clamp in 6061-T6 with a Brass body read back with both colours by trimesh (OBJ + MTL), lib3mf (two base materials, pindex 1 / 2, manifold) and OpenCascade XCAF (sRGB colours per solid); the OCC / lib3mf / trimesh validation of every earlier file still passes.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25l — Advanced assembly mates, Component Patterns / Mirror Components, Exploded View

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task given by the user: three ROADMAP Phase 7 assembly items (the newer mates, component patterns and Mirror Components, Exploded View). Other cloud sessions were running at the same time, so `kernel.js` / `csg.js` (mesh quality), `materials.js` / `drawing.js` (materials / BOM) and `exchange.js` (export formats) were not touched; the new maths is in `assembly.js`, the new UI in a new file `ui-assembly.js`, and the `ui-*.js` edits are small hooks (plus the Mate PM, which lives in `ui-commands.js`). Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` because cdnjs is blocked (runner only, no repo change). Baseline on d90cfa6: kernel 471/471, smoke 97/97, app page 0 console errors.

**Done (assembly.js).**
- Mates: **Parallel** (two planes / axes: directions equal, 2 rotations; plane + axis: axis in the plane's direction set, 1), **Perpendicular** (the converse), **Tangent** (cylinder–plane: axis parallel to the plane and one radius above it, flip = behind; cylinder–cylinder: axes parallel, rA + rB apart, flip = internal |rA − rB|; the radius is the least-squares fit of the facets, i.e. the distance to the flats), **Width** (`a`/`a2` width faces, `b`/`b2` tab faces or `b` a cylindrical tab: the tab's mid-plane / axis on the width mid-plane), **Distance between cylinders** (a Distance with two axes: parallel, centre distance), **Limit Distance / Limit Angle** (`min`/`max`; residual = how far the value lies outside the range, so inside it the component moves freely and the limit removes no DOF — limits are left out of the DOF Jacobian), **Gear** (`ratio: [a, b]`, B turns −θ·a/b; each turn measured about the ref's own axis from a `phase` stored the first time it is solved; `flip` = Reverse). New mates default to **closest** alignment (the sign the directions have when the mate is solved). A generic greedy step (turn so the directions satisfy the mate — align, or make perpendicular about the hinge earlier mates left — then slide along the one measured direction) precedes the existing LM refinement. Errors: *Tangent needs a cylindrical face and a planar or cylindrical face*, *Width needs two width faces and two tab faces (or one cylindrical tab)*, *Gear ratio must be two numbers > 0*, *Limit Distance needs 0 ≤ minimum ≤ maximum*, …; a cylinder–plane Distance is still an error (existing test).
- `MATE_GROUPS` (standard / advanced / mechanical), `MATE_NAMES`; `setMateValues` knows `min`, `max`, `ratio`; Builder `parallel / perpendicular / tangent / width / gear / limitDistance / limitAngle`; `generateScript` writes `min / max / ratio / phase / a2 / b2`.
- **Component patterns**: `model.patterns` (`linear` direction/spacing/count/reverse + optional direction 2, `circular` axis/angle/count/equal/reverse, `mirror` plane/oppositeHand) make instance components `{ pattern, seed, instance }` in `model.components`, synced on every rebuild (`syncPatterns`) and placed from the seed's solved transform (`placeInstances`) before and after the solve; the solver treats instances as fixed; mates on an instance trigger a second solve. Mirror copy = proper rotation (the seed's local axis nearest the plane normal flipped, bounding-box centre reflected); opposite hand = det −1 transform, body polygons reversed (`worldBody`). Deleting a seed deletes its instances; a lower count deletes the extra instances and their mates; `moveComponent` / `rotateComponent` refuse instances. `linearPattern / circularPattern / mirrorComponents` in scripts, `generateScript` writes them at their first instance's position with `instanceNames` so names and order survive the round trip.
- **Exploded View**: `model.explodedViews` (steps with components, unit world direction, distance), `model.exploded` (the view shown); `rebuild(model, cache, { explodeT })` adds the offsets to the world bodies only (transforms and mates stay collapsed) and returns `build.explode { view, name, t, offsets, lines }`; explode lines one segment per step and component through its centre; `refFromPick` subtracts the offset; `explodedBodies(model, build, view?, t?)` is the **drawing hook** (drawing.js was off limits: drawings are made from the current mesh, so a drawing made while exploded is exploded; a per-view "Show in exploded state" option should call this hook).

**Done (UI).** Mate PM (`ui-commands.js`): *Standard Mates* keeps the `select[data-key=type]` (Coincident, Parallel, Perpendicular, Tangent, Concentric, Distance, Angle; "—" when an Advanced / Mechanical type is chosen), *Advanced Mates* (Width, Limit Distance with Maximum / Minimum starting at the measured value, Limit Angle) and *Mechanical Mates* (Gear, Ratio A / Ratio B, Reverse); Width shows *Width selections* / *Tab selections* and fills four slots in pick order; alignment adds *Closest*; hidden for Gear. `ui-assembly.js` (new, loaded after `ui-relations.js`): Linear Component Pattern (the ribbon placeholder) with Circular Component Pattern and Mirror Components stacked beside it, one PM (selected component = seed, *Selecting* seeds / direction / axis / plane, previews, one undo step, edit by double-click); FeatureManager folders `LocalLPattern1` / `LocalCirPattern1` / `MirrorComponent1` after the Mates with the instance rows inside (`UI.asmTreeExtras`, a hook in `refreshAssemblyTree` which now builds rows through `componentRow`); Exploded View (the ribbon placeholder): Explode PM with components, X / Y / Z or *Along selected face*, Reverse, Distance, *Done* for the next step, and dragging a component along the direction sets the distance (wraps `UI.componentDragHook`); the assembly ConfigurationManager (`UI.asmConfigPanel`, a hook in `refreshConfigPanel`): *Default [ Assem1 ]* ▸ *ExplView1* ▸ steps, New Exploded View…, Explode / Collapse, Animate explode / collapse (`UI.asmBuildOpts = { explodeT }` read by `ui-core.js` rebuildSync), double-click and context menus; explode lines drawn dashed orange (redrawn from the `UI.refreshTree` wrapper, which also refreshes the ConfigurationManager — the assembly tree path never did). `anvil.pattern / patterns / removePattern / explode / exploded / animateExplode / explodedViews`; `anvil.mates()` rows carry `min / max / ratio`. `UI.renderPM` exported. Twelve icons (parallel, perpendicular, tangent, width, gear, limitDistance, limitAngle, lpatternComp, cpatternComp, mirrorComp, explode, explodeStep).
- Example **Gear train and patterns**: base plate with two shafts and two rails; Gear A (Ø24) and Gear B (Ø48) with Ø6 bores on the shafts, Gear mate 12 : 24 with phase [0, 0] and Gear A inserted at 45° → Gear B −22.5° (45° keeps the 16-gon bores on the shaft facets, so Interference Detection finds nothing); three dot pins on Gear B (Circular Component Pattern about its axis — they turn with it); a pin row of four (Linear Component Pattern) and a mirrored pin; a slider centred in the slot by Width and clamped from x = 70 to 40 by Limit Distance 10..60; a roller lying on the plate (Tangent + Perpendicular + Coincident); a three-step exploded view. 13 components, 17 mates, 3 patterns, volume 222139.696, builds in ~150 ms.

**Tests.** kernel 471 → 499: group `assembly: advanced mates, patterns, exploded view` (25: Parallel closest / aligned, Perpendicular face–face and cylinder–face, Parallel cylinder–face, Tangent cylinder–plane (±, fitted radius) and cylinder–cylinder (external / internal, error), Width planar and cylindrical tab + errors, Distance between cylinders (and edited), Gear 1 : 2 kinematics over two turns, ratio 3 : 1 and Reverse, bad ratio, Limit Distance clamps both ends and free inside, Limit Angle, setMateValues / MATE_GROUPS, script round trip of every new mate (fixed point), linear 3 × 2 grid positions and seed-driven update, circular equal / unequal, mirror copy and opposite hand (det −1, volumes, centroid), mates on an instance and a lowered count, delete seed / removePattern / pattern errors, pattern script round trip, explode offsets / lines / collapsed transforms / explodedBodies, animation fractions and exploded picks, explode editing and round trip, the example's BOM), plus the example's three per-example rows (volume with 13 position checks and no interference, script round trip, same volume as before the CSG early-out). Two older tests adjusted: the MATE_TYPES check looks at the first four types, and the assembly drawing tests are pinned to the three assemblies they have expectations for (they enumerated every assembly example). Smoke 97 → 106: Mate PM groups; Parallel + Tangent by picks; Width by four picks + edit/cancel + Limit Distance; Gear by the PM then rotating gear A; Linear Component Pattern from the ribbon (folder, seed-driven, instance refused, undo / redo); Circular + Mirror from the ribbon stack, edit count, delete; Explode PM with Done, ConfigurationManager rows, explode lines, Collapse / double-click / animation, undo; a real mouse drag with the Explode PM open (distance = 40 px / px-per-mm); the Design Library example with its folders, rows and animated explode. Bug found by the smoke run: `asmMove` / `asmRotate` pushed an undo step before refusing — they now refuse pattern instances up front.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 499/499, ui-smoke 106/106, app page loads with no console errors — before and after merging origin/main (03bda1c, dashboard only, no conflicts). Screenshots of the Gear train example collapsed, exploded (explode lines, ConfigurationManager with ExplView1 and three steps) and of the Mate PM with the Advanced group checked by eye. `kernel.js`, `csg.js`, `drawing.js` unchanged against origin/main (`materials.js` / `exchange.js` do not exist on main yet).

**Merged with main after PR #9 (cloud-2026-09-25j, materials / custom properties / BOM columns).** Conflicts in QUEUE, SESSION_LOG, index.html (both `ui-assembly.js` and `ui-materials.js` script tags) and ui-panels.js (the component row keeps the material in its tooltip and takes the `depth` of an instance row inside a pattern folder) resolved keeping both sides. Materials belong to the part source, and a pattern instance copies its seed's source, so instances (copies and opposite-hand mirrors alike) share the seed's part build, material, density, colour and BOM row; new kernel test: a Brass block patterned ×3 and mirrored twice gives five Brass components of 68.00 g in `Assembly.massProps`, one BOM row *Block · Brass · 68.00 g · Acme · 5* with the Material / Mass / Vendor columns, and the same BOM, mass and centroid when exploded (the explode moves the bodies, not the transforms; only the bbox follows the exploded bodies). After the merge: kernel-tests 515/515, ui-smoke 112/112, app page loads with no console errors.

**Merged with main after PR #10 (cloud-2026-09-25k, exchange formats).** Conflicts only in QUEUE, ROADMAP and SESSION_LOG (kept both, in run order j, k, l; the ROADMAP keeps #10's STEP lines and this run's ticked mates line). Checked the combination: STEP / 3MF / OBJ export an assembly's pattern and mirror instances as ordinary components (one NEXT_ASSEMBLY_USAGE_OCCURRENCE / build item / group each, at the solved transform), but an **opposite-hand** instance (det R < 0) came back from STEP misplaced by 10 mm — an AXIS2_PLACEMENT_3D is always right-handed — and relied on a negative-determinant matrix in 3MF. Fixed in `exchange.js` `docParts`: such an instance is exported as its own part "Block (opposite hand)" whose mesh is the source reflected in its local YZ plane, placed by the proper rotation R·diag(−1, 1, 1). New kernel test (exchange group): base + asymmetric block, linear ×3, circular ×2, mirror copy and opposite hand, shown exploded; STEP has 7 NAUOs over 3 B-reps, 3MF 7 items all with det +1, and STEP / 3MF / OBJ re-import every instance with the collapsed body's volume, bbox and centroid (1e-6); STEP and 3MF text are identical to the collapsed assembly's. The test fails on main's exchange.js and passes with the fix. After the merge: kernel-tests 530/530, ui-smoke 118/118, app page loads with no console errors.

**Decisions.**
- Pattern instances are real components (not a derived list) so body indices, picking, hide/show, BOM counts, mass properties and interference work unchanged; their transforms are output, like solved transforms.
- The explode is display state (no undo step for Explode / Collapse, offsets not in the transforms), as in SolidWorks where mates stay collapsed; adding / editing / deleting steps are undo steps.
- Gear phases are stored on the mate (not recomputed each solve), so the relation survives reloads; the driven component is the second one (the greedy moves B when both are held by their shafts).
- Limit mates are left out of the DOF count (inside the range they fix nothing); at a bound the solver still holds the component.
- Tangent uses the facet-fitted radius: the cylinder rests on a flat, which is what the faceted solids really do (true curves are a kernel item).

**Known gaps.** No SmartMates, collision detection while dragging, or the other Advanced / Mechanical mates (Symmetric, Profile Center, Path, Linear Coupler, Cam, Slot, Hinge, Rack Pinion, Screw); Width has only the Centred constraint; no Tangent between a cylinder and a Distance-type plane offset; explode steps translate only (no rotation, no auto-spacing, no radial explode); one exploded view is edited by the PM (the model holds several); exploded views are not per configuration; drawings have no per-view exploded option (hook: `Assembly.explodedBodies`); a Gear with a non-integer ratio slips if gear A turns more than ±180° from its phase in one step; the `…` / `▸` mojibake under `python -m http.server` (no `<meta charset>` in index.html) is still there (the new file escapes its strings).

**Next up.**
1. Drawings: a "Show in exploded state" view option using `Assembly.explodedBodies`, explode lines in drawings (drawing.js session).
2. Assemblies: SmartMates by dragging, collision detection while moving, Symmetric / Slot / Hinge / Rack Pinion mates, rotation in explode steps.
3. Assemblies: sub-assemblies and live links to part files.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25g — Kernel mesh quality: hull lofts, Kernel.meshQuality, the Funnel's slivers and crack

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: the 2D-drawings run (cloud-2026-09-25d) found sliver faces in the Funnel's shell that show as extra lines in its drawing; the Evaluate-tools session (PR #12) then reported the Funnel's body open (3 open edges, a ~1e-5 mm crack where the cone meets the tube). Scope narrowed by the user mid-run: keep the loft fix, prove the new Funnel volume analytically, add and assert `Kernel.meshQuality`, needle cleanup optional. Edited `kernel.js`, `tests/kernel-tests.html`, `tests/BENCHMARKS.md`, `README.md`; `csg.js`, `drawing.js` and `ui-*.js` untouched. Merged origin/main (PRs #3–#11) before the tests below; the kernel changes are internal to `buildLoft` plus a new export, so the `exchange.js` / `sketch-solver.js` wrappers of `rebuild` / `cacheFromBuild` / `generateScript` are unaffected (the exchange and sketch-solver groups pass). Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` (runner only).

**Where the slivers came from.** Not the CSG and not the projector. The Funnel's loft resampled its Ø14 21-gon onto 51 points to match the Ø80 51-gon; the quads between them were not planar, so they split into needle triangles (tips ~5°) with slightly concave (saddle) diagonals up to 3.9°. Shell grows each face's slab by T·tan(φ/2) per edge and clamps a corner at 5× that distance; at the needle tips the corners were clamped short of the neighbouring slabs, which left V-grooves 0.1–0.8 mm² in the inner wall (faces meeting at 90° and 174°), drawn as ~30 radial lines in the Top view and dozens of hidden lines in Front / Right. The same mismatch left a 1e-5 mm crack at the tube (3 open edges).

**Done.**
- `buildLoft`: when every section is convex and all sketch planes are parallel, the side is the convex hull of consecutive sections (`hullLoftLoops`: a rotating-calipers merge of the two loops' edge directions; each edge of one section joins the other's support vertex, parallel edges make a quad). Every side face is planar, every edge convex, and each section keeps its own polygon, so the neck still matches the tube made from the same circle. Other lofts (non-convex or non-parallel sections) keep the resampled path unchanged.
- Funnel volume 12268.395 → 12160.516. Proved without the kernel in the new `mesh quality` group: the hull's side planes come from brute force over (edge, vertex) pairs of the two n-gons (69 planes: 51 + 21 minus 3 parallel pairs), and the wall is integrated slice by slice as |B| − |B ∩ (C ∪ S)| (B the body section, C the hull section with every side plane moved in by 1.5, S the tube 21-gon moved in by 1.5; convex clipping, Simpson with 20 000 intervals): 12160.5160926 vs kernel 12160.5160943 (1.4e-10). The Cone alone equals the prismatoid formula h/6 (A1 + A2 + 4 Am) with Am the Minkowski mean section to 1e-12. The old value was the shell of a different, wrong surface: the resampled cone lies 70 mm³ inside the hull (80480.5 vs 80550.5) with saddle diagonals, and its shell had the grooves and the crack above.
- `Kernel.meshQuality(mesh, opts)`: vertices welded at 1e-9 × diagonal; every directed edge must cancel exactly or, for T-junctions, against antiparallel collinear edges by parameter range; what is left is an open edge (a 1e-5 mm crack is found: tested by shifting one face of the Funnel), an edge used twice in one direction or by more than two triangles is non-manifold; slivers = collinear triangles (height under tol), needles = corners under 1°, min angle. Asserted on every Design Library example (assembly components one by one, since touching components share edges): 0 open, 0 non-manifold, 0 slivers, min angle > 0.001°. With the old loft the check reports the Funnel's 3 open edges; now 0.
- Funnel drawing: Front 7 visible / 17 hidden lines (was 7 / 469), Top 72 / 0 (was 394 / 333) — the rims only; checked by eye on a rendered sheet, pinned by a test.
- No needle-triangle cleanup in `meshFromBody`: a face-level retriangulation costs more than the rebuild on the small parts (see BENCHMARKS.md), over the 10 % budget. The remaining needles (fan triangles of convex CSG polygons, ≥ 0.0016°) do not open the mesh and do not show in drawings.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765, after merging origin/main).** kernel-tests 562/562, ui-smoke 118/118, app page loads with no console errors. Every other example's volume is bit-identical to main (max relative difference 0) and the three flat patterns (U-bracket, Tray, Normal Cut Bracket) hash identically (flat JSON + DXF). Bench, best median of four runs each: sum 1023 → 537 ms, Funnel 762 → 276 ms, every other part within ±0.1–0.3 ms of main.

**Decisions.**
- Fix at the source (loft), not by extending shell slab corners: extending the corners only helps at convex vertices, and the resampled loft's vertices were saddles, where a longer slab over-cuts.
- The hull is used only for convex sections on parallel planes, where it is the natural ruled frustum; a square → circle loft now lands on the hull too (its test bounds hold).
- meshQuality's weld tolerance is 1e-9 × the diagonal, far below the drawing's 1e-3 edge-pairing grid, so hairline cracks are reported rather than hidden.

**Next up.**
1. Shell on non-convex resampled lofts (star → circle): saddle vertices still clamp slab corners; a vertex-aware corner rule.
2. Optional needle cleanup that stays within budget: re-fan only polygons with collinear vertices or a corner under 1°.
3. Show `Kernel.meshQuality` in the Evaluate tab's Check (PR #12's session owns that UI).
## Run cloud-2026-09-25n — Evaluate tools: Check, Zebra Stripes, Section Properties, Draft Analysis, Clearance Verification, Interference Detection on selected components

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: ROADMAP Phase 7 "Evaluate" line. Other cloud sessions were editing `kernel.js` / `csg.js` (mesh quality), `drawing.js` / `drawing.css` (drawing annotations) and `assembly.js` / `ui-assembly.js` (assemblies), so none of those were touched. The logic is a new `anvil/evaluate.js` (global `Evaluate`, no DOM) and the UI a new `anvil/ui-evaluate.js`; existing files got small additive edits: `ui-commands.js` (exposes `UI.renderPM` / `pmSpec` / `pmTitle`), `ui-panels.js` (ribbon buttons, tooltips, *Tools ▸ Evaluate ▸* rows), `icons.js` (three icons), `style.css`, `index.html` (two script tags).

**Done (evaluate.js).**
- `checkMesh` / `checkBody` / `check`: weld on a 1e-6 grid (CSG bodies through `Exchange.bodyMesh`, which also splits T-junctions), then open edges, non-manifold edges (≥ 3 triangles, or two running the edge the same way), zero-area faces (area ≤ 1e-14·diag² or ≤ 1e-10·longest edge²; CSG polygons checked too) and self-intersections (strict edge-through-interior test for triangles sharing no vertex, x-sweep, capped at 2e6 pairs / 200 findings). `check` flattens every problem into drawable items.
- `smoothNormals`: per-corner average of the adjacent face normals within 22° (one vote per face), for the zebra shader.
- `sectionFromLoops` / `sectionOfFace` / `sectionOfSketch`: exact Green's-theorem integrals (A, first moments, ∫x², ∫y², ∫xy), centroidal Ixx / Iyy / Ixy, polar J, principal I1 ≥ I2 and angle (Mohr), world axes, origin moments, perimeter.
- `draftAnalysis`: asin(n·pull) per face, classes positive / requires / negative, surfaces joined by smooth `bodyEdges`, straddle when a surface has both signs.
- `minDistance` (triangle–triangle distance: Ericson closest point on triangle and segment–segment, Möller–Trumbore crossings, BVH pruned by the best distance), `clearance`, `interference` (same CSG.intersect test and tolerance as `Assembly.interference`) over `pairsFor(selected)`.

**Done (UI).** Evaluate ribbon (part): Section Properties, Check, Zebra Stripes (toggle, pressed state), Draft Analysis, Clearance Verification replace the placeholders; assembly Evaluate: Section Properties, Interference Detection, Clearance Verification, Check, Zebra Stripes, Draft Analysis. PMs: Check (counts, problem list, colour per kind, click a row to show it alone), Zebra Stripes (number, direction, sharpness; OK keeps it on; the overlay follows rebuilds and hidden bodies; section-view clipping), Section Properties (follows the selection: face, sketch row, active sketch; centroid and principal axes drawn), Draft Analysis (direction of pull from a plane or a clicked face, reverse, angle, straddle; legend with counts; face colours overlay; hover shows a face's draft), Clearance Verification (selection box edited by clicking bodies, *Selected items and the rest*, minimum clearance, results with closest points and line), Interference Detection (replaces the ui-commands.js version with the same rows / message / highlights plus *Selected Components*, click-to-toggle, *Entire assembly*). `UI.evaluate`, `anvil.check / sectionProperties / draftAnalysis / clearance / interferenceOf / zebra`.

**Tests.** kernel 500 → 516 (group `evaluate`, 16 tests: rectangle, L-section, ring and holed-face section properties against closed forms; tapered-extrude and cross-cylinder draft; box and cylinder clearances; selected interference vs `Assembly.interference`; Check on broken meshes and on every example; zebra normals). ui-smoke 109 → 116 (7 steps). Details in README *Tests*.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765 via `python -m http.server`, three.js r128 from npm three@0.128.0 routed in place of the CDN).** kernel-tests 516/516, ui-smoke 116/116, app page loads with no console errors. Screenshots of Zebra Stripes on the Mug, Draft Analysis on a tapered block with a cross cylinder (straddle blue, cap yellow), Section Properties of an L face, Clearance between offset boxes and Check on the Funnel looked right.

**Finding for the mesh-quality session.** Check reports the *Funnel* example's body as not closed: 3 open edges (a crack about 1e-5 mm wide around a sliver at the cone / tube junction, wider than the 1e-6 weld) and 2–3 collinear triangles. Every other example is clean. The kernel test reports but does not assert the Funnel, so a fix there needs no test change.

**Decisions.**
- Check reads the welded, T-junction-split mesh (`Exchange.bodyMesh`), not the raw CSG polygons: T-junctions are how CSG shares edges, not defects. Thin but non-degenerate CSG slivers are not "zero-area".
- Zebra stripes are view-fixed reflections (like SolidWorks) on smoothed normals; flat faces show a uniform tone, faceted curved faces continuous stripes.
- Draft counts are per surface (tangent-joined facets), so a faceted cylinder is one face, as a SolidWorks user would count it.
- Interference Detection lives in ui-evaluate.js (the ui-commands.js version was deleted at the merge with PR #11); `anvil.interference()` still checks the whole assembly, `anvil.interferenceOf` takes a selection.

**Known gaps.** Sensors, Undercut / Thickness Analysis, curvature display still placeholders; coplanar disjoint faces are one "face" for Section Properties (the kernel mesh groups by plane); self-intersection checks skip coplanar overlaps (duplicate faces show up as non-manifold edges instead).

**Next up.**
1. Phase 7: true curves in the kernel (then exact section properties / draft on round faces).
2. Evaluate: Thickness Analysis (ray casts along −n) and Undercut Analysis (reuse `draftAnalysis` with occlusion rays); Sensors on mass / clearance values.
3. Assemblies: Collision Detection while moving (reuse `Evaluate.minDistance`).

**Merge with PR #11 (advanced assemblies, cloud-2026-09-25l).** Merged origin/main (614eb34) into `cloud/evaluate-tools`; conflicts in QUEUE, SESSION_LOG, index.html and ui-commands.js resolved keeping both sides in run order (25l before 25n; `ui-assembly.js` loads before `evaluate.js` / `ui-evaluate.js`; `UI.renderPM` kept as a guarded wrapper both files use, plus `pmSpec` / `pmTitle`). The old Interference Detection in ui-commands.js (and its `UI.interference` / `interferenceSession` exports) is deleted; ui-evaluate.js now defines `UI.interference` (whole assembly, collapsed) for `anvil.interference()`. Interference Detection and Clearance Verification analyse the assembly collapsed: pattern and mirror instances are ordinary components (opposite-hand bodies already turned outward), and in the exploded state the bodies come from `Assembly.explodedBodies(…, 0)`, with results drawn shifted by each component's explode offset and a "Computed with the assembly collapsed." note. Found while testing: Clearance's `interfering` flag (an edge through the other's interior) missed a block overlapping its mirror image (coplanar sides) and nested solids; for CSG bodies it is now the `CSG.intersect` volume (1e-9 · diag³), a nested solid reporting distance 0. New tests: kernel 2 (pattern / mirror / opposite-hand instances, exploded vs collapsed, nested), smoke 1 (exploded assembly with a pattern and an opposite-hand mirror). Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765, three.js r128 from npm): kernel-tests 548/548, ui-smoke 126/126, app page with no console errors.
## Run cloud-2026-09-25m — Drawing annotations: centre marks, centrelines, hole callouts, ordinate / baseline / chamfer dimensions, dimension editing and driving, balloons

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: ROADMAP Phase 7 drawing annotations (the "centre marks … dimension dragging and editing" and "manual Balloon … QTY" lines). Other cloud sessions own `kernel.js` / `csg.js` (mesh quality), `exchange.js` (export formats) and `assembly.js` / `ui-assembly.js`, so none of those were touched: everything is in `drawing.js` and `drawing.css`, plus tests and docs.

**Done (engine, `Drawing`).**
- `circlesAlong(mesh, F)`: every closed circular edge loop perpendicular to a view direction, with its depth (edges grouped by depth before `mergeCollinear` / `extractCircles`, so a hole's top and bottom stay apart). `cylAxes(mesh, F)`: cylinder axes lying in the view plane from the circles seen along the view's u and v (coaxial / overlapping axes joined).
- Centre marks (`addCenterMark`: single / linear with connection lines / circular with a Kåsa-fitted pitch circle, radial lines and a centre mark; 2.5 mm cross, 1 mm gap, extension lines 2.5 mm past the circle), centrelines (`addCenterline`, 3 mm past both ends), `autoCenterMarks` / `autoCenterlines` / `autoInsert` driven by `doc.options` (Document Properties); `standard3View` inserts when the options are on. New `center` style: SVG dash 6-1.5-1.5-1.5, PDF dash, DXF `CENTER` linetype (the LTYPE table only grows when a drawing uses it, so older DXF output is byte-identical), layer `ANNOTATIONS`.
- Hole callouts (`addHoleCallout`, `holeInfo`, `holeText`): read from the solid. The first version probed points with ray parity and relied on a planar junction circle, which a countersink cut by a revolve does not have (its cone and the drill have different facet counts); the final version cuts lines parallel to the axis (`lineIntervals`: exact Möller-Trumbore, sorted, paired) just outside the drill, just outside each larger circle and halfway between. THRU vs blind and the entry side come from the empty gap on the axis; counterbore vs countersink from where the halfway line's wall ends; countersink angles snap to the standard ones within 1.5°. Count "4X" = visible holes of the view that read the same, one per centre (a counterbore's two circles once).
- Ordinate / baseline / chamfer dimensions in `view.dims` (`addOrdinate`, `addBaseline`, `addChamferDim`), references `{ kind: 'dia', c, r }` (centre) or an edge, all reattached per render (`reattachUserDim`). Ordinate texts share a line 10 mm out, crowded ones jog; baselines stack 8 mm apart; chamfer = setback along the lead-in edge × cos θ and θ.
- Dimension properties (`at`, `tx`, `lead`, `off`, `flip`, `tol`, `prec`, `label`): `dimLabel` formats bilateral (stacked beside the value at 0.7 height), limit (upper over lower), symmetric (±), precision and `<DIM>` text; model items keep theirs in `doc.dimProps[view:kind:n]` so they survive size changes. `dimPrims` was restructured around one `linear()` helper and a `label()` that emits the stacked texts; with no properties the primitives are exactly the old ones.
- Driving: `dimSource(model, build, view, dim)` traces a model item to the sketch dimension (circle Ø, rectangle w / h, line / slot length, arc R) or blind extrude depth with the same value whose 3D direction the dimension measures, nearest on the sheet; `driveDim` writes it (through `SketchSolver.setDim` when the sketch has relations) and refuses equation-driven values and non-positive ones.
- Balloons: `addBalloon` (attach point, placement relative to the view, stack with direction, QTY and its side); part drawings without a BOM number their balloons as item 1; dragged balloons keep out of Auto Balloon's perimeter spreading.
- Writers: `plainText` turns ↧ / ⌴ / ⌵ into `X 6.8 DEEP` / `CBORE` / `CSK` for DXF and PDF; DXF `%%p` / `%%d`, PDF WinAnsi `\261` / `\260`.
- Reattachment fix found by the new ordinate test: an edge on the view's outline remembers which side of the bounds it was (`d.bound`) and prefers the new outline edge on that side, so widening the plate no longer hands the right-edge reference to the hole silhouette at x = 94.

**Done (UI).** Annotation ribbon tab (Smart Dimension with Ordinate / Baseline / Chamfer Dimension stacked beside it, Model Items, Balloon, Auto Balloon, Stacked Balloon, Hole Callout, Center Mark, Centerline, Document Properties) plus Insert ▸ Annotations / Dimensions and Tools ▸ Options ▸ Document Properties. Center Mark PM (style, extended lines, mark size; picked holes highlighted), Centerline tool (axis within the cylinder radius, or two parallel edges), Hole Callout tool (refuses a circle that is not a hole), Ordinate / Baseline PM (direction; base then features; one undo step), Chamfer Dimension tool, Balloon / Stacked Balloon PMs (quantity, placement, stack direction; a rubber-band preview while placing). Dragging a dimension (line and text), a callout leader or a balloon; double-click opens the Dimension PM (Primary Value with "Drives Sketch1 (rect width)", Tolerance/Precision, Dimension Text, Leaders), the Balloon PM or the Hole Callout PM. A driven value is validated on a copy, then one undo step changes the part and rebuilds. Tree rows Center Marks / Centerlines / Hole Callouts per view (Delete from their menu); Delete removes a selected annotation. New drawings made in the app start with Auto insert on (centre marks, centrelines). `anvil.drawing.options / centerMark / autoCenterMarks / centerline / autoCenterlines / holeCallout / deleteAnnotation / ordinate / baseline / chamferDim / dimProps / dimSource / driveDim / balloon / balloonProps`; `summary()` gains `annotations`, `dimDetails`, `options` and balloon `index / stack / qty / manual`.
- Two interaction bugs found while checking by eye with real mouse events: the selection overlay (redrawn on every mousedown, with a fill) swallowed the second click of a double-click, so no `dblclick` fired on a selected dimension (`#dw-overlay { pointer-events: none }`); and the sheet's mouse events bubbled into the part viewport's handlers, so a double-click also opened the feature under the pointer in the hidden 3D view (Boss-Extrude1 replaced the Dimension PM) — `stopPropagation` in the sheet's mousedown / dblclick / wheel / contextmenu. Both also affected double-clicking views before this run.

**Tests.** kernel 486 → 496 before the merge, 510 after it (group `drawing annotations`, 10): auto-inserted marks at the analytic Top-view positions of the four plate holes (cross and extension-line segments) and the Front / Right centrelines; nothing when the option is off; linear connection line; circular mark on six pattern holes (pitch circle r = 20 × scale at the analytic centre); hole callouts for THRU, blind 10, counterbore through / blind 15, countersink Ø12 × 90°, far-side blind, a boss (null), `4X Ø8 THRU` on the plate; ordinate texts and positions, following a width change to 120; baseline stacking (11 / 19 / 27 mm) and `2 X 45°`; tolerance formatting (bilateral, limit, symmetric, zero lower, `<DIM> TYP`, Ø limit, inches, precision) and model-item properties surviving a part change; dimension sources and a driven rebuild (120 × 60 × 6 with four Ø10 holes against the analytic 16-gon volume; `=T` refused); `at` / `flip` / `lead` geometry; part and stacked assembly balloons with QTY; CENTER linetype, symbol words, SVG dash and PDF WinAnsi. ui-smoke 103 → 108, 114 after the merge (five steps: auto insert + Annotation tab + Document Properties; Center Mark PM linear, Hole Callout drag, Centerline tool, Delete / Undo; Ordinate and Chamfer Dimension tools; dimension drag, Dimension PM tolerance and driving 100 → 120, Undo; Balloon tool, drag, Balloon PM, Stacked Balloon tool on the assembly, Delete / Undo).
