**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765 via `python -m http.server`, three.js r128 from npm three@0.128.0 routed in place of the CDN URL in the harness only).** kernel-tests 496/496, ui-smoke 108/108, app page loads with no console errors. Exports of three drawings (plate with every annotation kind, a counterbore + countersink block, the pinned-plate assembly with a stacked balloon) through ezdxf (audit: 0 errors, 0 fixes; CENTER linetype present only when used; `CBORE %%c11 X 6.8 DEEP`, `CSK %%c12 X 90%%d`, `60.0 %%p0.2`), PyMuPDF (one A3 page; text has THRU, CBORE, CSK, DEEP, ±, °, 1X) and an XML parse of the SVG. After merging origin/main (PR #10, exchange formats; the test pages were rebuilt as main's version plus this run's blocks, because git aligned the two appended blocks mid-test): kernel-tests 510/510, ui-smoke 114/114, app loads with no console errors. Screenshots of the sheets, the Annotation tab and the Dimension PM checked by eye (they caught the "2X" count on a counterbore and colliding leaders: callouts now leave 35° up from the diameter leaders, concentric diameter leaders fan upward).

**Decisions.**
- Hole callouts are read from the geometry, not from feature data: there is no Hole Wizard feature yet and kernel.js was off limits. When a Hole Wizard lands, its feature can hand `holeText` the same `{ dia, thru, depth, cbore, csk }` directly.
- Only model items drive the part; Smart Dimensions stay reference dimensions (SolidWorks' behaviour). The source is matched by value and direction, so two identical parameters in the same direction are told apart only by where they project; ambiguous cases pick the nearest.
- Auto insert is off in the engine (`Drawing.newDrawing` without `options`) so existing drawings and tests are unchanged, and on for drawings made in the app, like SolidWorks' template.
- Centre marks are drawn axis-aligned even in a circular pattern (SolidWorks rotates them radially).

**Known gaps.** No magnetic lines for balloons, notes, surface-finish / datum / GD&T symbols; no radial / diameter dimension style options (leader only), no dual units; ordinate dimensions do not auto-jog around other annotations; hole callouts need the drill's end circles to be clean loops (holes cut through curved faces are read as plain diameters); a stacked balloon's QTY is its first item's.

**Next up.**
1. Hole Wizard feature (counterbore / countersink / tapped with cosmetic threads) — kernel side, coordinate with the mesh-quality session; the drawing callout already reads the geometry.
2. Drawings: notes and GD&T (feature control frames, datums), magnetic lines, multiple sheets.
3. Phase 7: true curves in the kernel.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25o — Sub-assemblies (rigid / Flexible) and live links to saved documents

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish, one agent and no subagents. Task given by the user: ROADMAP Phase 7 "Assemblies: sub-assemblies, and components that are live links to part files instead of embedded copies". Other cloud sessions were running at the same time, so `kernel.js` / `csg.js` (mesh quality), `drawing.js` / `drawing.css` (drawing annotations) and `evaluate.js` / `ui-evaluate.js` (evaluate tools) were not touched; the logic is in `assembly.js` and `ui-assembly.js`, with small additive hooks in `exchange.js`, `ui-panels.js`, `ui-commands.js`, `ui-app.js`, `ui-core.js` and `style.css`. Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.56, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` because cdnjs is blocked (runner only, no repo change). Baseline on 614eb34: kernel 530/530, smoke 118/118, app page 0 console errors.

**Done (assembly.js).**
- **Sources.** `resolveSource(source)` → `{ kind: 'part' | 'assembly', model, file? }` for `{ example }` (Design Library parts and now assemblies), `{ script }` (part or assembly script), `{ model }` (embedded part or assembly) and the new `{ file: name }`; `partModel` returns an assembly model for a sub-assembly source instead of refusing it.
- **Sub-assemblies.** `buildPart` builds a sub-assembly with `rebuild` in turn (collapsed; the build carries `model`, so every level can be walked); the top-level body is the sub-assembly's combined body. A document that contains itself is *Circular reference: X contains itself*; nesting deeper than 16 is refused.
- **Path refs.** `{ component, path: [inner id | name, …], face | cylinder | plane | axis }`: `localRef` resolves the ref in the inner part's frame and carries it out through the inner transforms; `refFromPick` on a sub-assembly face returns the path ref of the part under the pick (`pickInner`: nearest patch, picked normal preferred).
- **Flexible.** `flexible: true` / `setFlexible`: `virtualModel` rewrites the solve — the sub-assembly's fixed parts become a rigid base (id S), each floating part a virtual component `'S/k'`, its own mates join the parent's with rewritten refs, parent refs into S by path go to `'S/k'`; `finishVirtual` stores the in-context inner transforms as `flexState` (relative to S), builds a per-instance composed sub-assembly body (`composeSub`), and maps statuses, mate results (inner mates under the sub build, their failures as a warning on S) and Gear phases back. Solve as Rigid drops `flexState`.
- **Live links.** `Assembly.Documents`: a backing store (in memory by default; the app installs the browser's saved files) plus a dirty layer for edits not saved yet; `get / has / put(name, model, { dirty }) / save / revert / remove / list / isDirty / dirty / use`. The part-build cache is keyed as before (so `build.cache[JSON.stringify(source)]` lookups elsewhere keep working) and an entry carries a `stamp` (a hash of the linked document and of every document it links to in turn), so a changed document rebuilds and an unchanged one is reused. `references(model)`, `saveReferences(model)`.
- **Broken references** are suppressed with a warning, not errors: the virtual model holds the component fixed and leaves its mates out; `build.components[i]` `{ suppressed, broken, file, warning }`, `build.mates[id]` `{ ok, suppressed, warning }`, empty body; BOM and mass leave it out; the document reappearing brings it back.
- **Across levels.** `massProps` recurses (mass-weighted centroid, sub-assembly rows with world-centroid `components`); `interference` checks every part at every level (`leafBodies`, names `Hinge<2>/Arm<1>`, `aPath` / `bPath`), `{ subAssembliesAsComponents: true }` checks each sub-assembly as the union of its parts (touching shells side by side are not a solid the BSP intersect handles); `bom(model, build?, { type: 'top' | 'indented' | 'parts' })`.
- `packAndGo(model)` → `{ model, embedded, missing }`; `generateScript` writes `{ file }`, `path`, `flexible` and `flexState`; `a.flexible(c, on)`; pattern instances are always rigid.

**Done (exchange.js, additive).** `docParts` walks the levels: `instances` stays the flat list of placed parts (OBJ and every existing consumer), plus `top` / `subs`; STEP writes each sub-assembly as its own product with NAUOs under it (children first; a flexible instance is its own product *Hinge (Hinge<2>, flexible)*), 3MF a components object per sub-assembly. A mirrored (det < 0) sub-assembly is written as its parts.

**Done (UI).** `ui-assembly.js`: the document store backed by `localStorage` `anvil.files` (the File ▸ Save store, parse cached by raw string); FeatureManager sub-assembly rows (`UI.asmRowDecor`, a hook in `componentRow`) that expand into their components every level down and a Mates folder, linked rows with ↗ and *linked to "X"* (*edited, not saved*), broken rows struck through *(suppressed)* with the warning and greyed mates; component menu (`UI.asmComponentMenuExtras`, a hook in `componentMenu`): Open Part / Open Assembly, Edit Part (linked or embedded), Solve as Flexible / Rigid, Make Linked Document…; a document stack with a banner and *Return to Assem1* (the assembly keeps its undo stack; a changed linked part stays in memory as dirty, a changed embedded part replaces its copies as one undo step; compared against the document as opened, so an untouched part is not left edited); File ▸ Save of an assembly saves its edited references (`UI.afterSaveHook` in `fileSave`, which now defaults to the document's saved name, `App.fileName`, reset by `loadModel`); File ▸ Pack and Go…; the Assembly tab's *Bill of Materials* placeholder opens a BOM dialog (Top-level only / Indented / Parts only); Insert Components lists Design Library assemblies and saved documents as links (`UI.insertSourcesExtra`), Browse… takes assembly JSON; the Mate PM names picks inside a sub-assembly *Face@Arm<1>@Hinge<1>* (`UI.innerRefName`). `anvil.documents / saveDocument / removeDocument / flexible / openPart / editPart / returnToAssembly / docStack / bom / packAndGo / saveReferences`.
- Examples **Hinge sub-assembly** (a fixed leaf, an arm hinged on its top edge with 1 mm knuckle clearance, 1 DOF) and **Hinged cover (sub-assemblies)** (a base and two hinges mated by faces of their leaves; Hinge<2> Flexible with an Angle mate opening its arm to 30°; 88000 mm³, no interference, `checks` include the in-context `flexState`).

**Tests.** kernel 530 → 545 before the merge: the two examples (3 rows each) and group `assembly: sub-assemblies and live links` (9): nested transforms two levels down (centroid, bbox, path ref and pick), BOM over three levels (Top-level / Indented with items 3.2.1 / Parts only ×7 pins), Hinged cover mass properties against the analytic centroid, Flexible vs Rigid (+ `flexState` script round trip), a part edit propagating to two assemblies (one through a linked sub-assembly, dirty edits, `saveReferences`, cache reuse while unchanged), broken and circular references, interference inside / across levels / as components, STEP NAUO tree + STEP / 3MF / OBJ re-import of every part at its world place + Pack and Go with an empty store (STEP text equal to 1e-9), and the script round trip with links. `checkAsmExample` also checks `flexState` keyed by inner name. Smoke 118 → 121: the Hinged cover in the FeatureManager / BOM dialog / Mass Properties; Solve as Rigid / Flexible from the menu with undo, a pick inside a sub-assembly, Edit Part of an embedded part with undo, a Mate PM pick inside Hinge<1>; live links end to end (save, Insert Components, Open Part / Return unchanged, Edit Part / change / Return, File ▸ Save saves both, a second assembly updates, break / restore, Pack and Go).

**Merged with main (d6f7316: PRs #12 evaluate tools, #13 drawing annotations, #14 mesh quality).** Conflicts in QUEUE, README (file rows, Tests paragraphs, Known limitations) and the two test pages were both-sides appends, resolved keeping both. Main's new *mesh quality* and *Check* tests require every example body to be closed and manifold; the first hinge had the arm touching the leaf along the hinge line (an edge of four faces in the sub-assembly's combined body), so the arm now starts 1 mm off the hinge line (volumes unchanged; centroid and pick expectations updated). After the merge: **kernel-tests 607/607, ui-smoke 134/134, app page loads with no console errors**. `kernel.js`, `csg.js`, `drawing.js`, `drawing.css`, `evaluate.js`, `ui-evaluate.js` identical to origin/main.

**Decisions.**
- A sub-assembly is one component with one body (the combined body of its parts) so body indices, picking, hide/show, Move / Rotate and every existing per-component path keep working; the levels below are reached through the build (`partBuilds[id].model`), not by flattening the model.
- Flexible is solved by a virtual flat model handed to the unchanged solver, not by a hierarchical solver; its result is stored in the parent (`flexState`), never in the sub-assembly document, as in SolidWorks.
- The document store is the existing save mechanism (browser saved files), so Open / Save / the Files task pane and links share one namespace; dirty edits are an overlay, so assemblies show an edit before it is saved and File ▸ Save of the assembly saves it.
- Broken references are warnings and suppression, never errors, so an assembly with a missing file still opens, solves and exports.
- Cache keys stayed `JSON.stringify(source)` (drawing.js, exchange.js and massProps look entries up by it); freshness of links is a stamp on the entry.

**Known gaps.** Flexible applies one level (a flexible sub-assembly's own sub-assemblies and its pattern instances move rigidly with its base); no configurations of sub-assemblies; Edit Part opens the part on its own (no transparent assembly around it, no in-context references to assembly geometry); the drawing BOM (`drawing.js`, off limits this run) lists a sub-assembly as a top-level row only — its Indented / Parts only across levels needs one call to `Assembly.bom` there; the Evaluate tab's Interference Detection (`evaluate.js`) intersects top-level bodies, so a sub-assembly with touching parts is one non-unioned body there (Assembly.interference handles it); inserting a document that does not exist is refused (only an existing link can break); the document store is per browser (no files on disk).

**Next up.**
1. Drawings: Indented / Parts only BOM across sub-assemblies via `Assembly.bom` (drawing.js session); "Show in exploded state".
2. Assemblies: configurations in assemblies (and of sub-assemblies), SmartMates, collision detection while dragging.
3. Desktop shell / real files on disk so links can point at files outside the browser.
## Run cloud-2026-09-25q — Selection Filter, box selection, selection sync and Instant3D

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish. One agent, no subagents. Task: ROADMAP Phase 7 "Selection filters and box selection in the viewport; Instant3D drag handles on features". Other cloud sessions were editing `kernel.js` / `csg.js` / `drawing.js` / `exchange.js` (true curves) and `assembly.js` / `ui-assembly.js` (sub-assemblies), so none of those were touched; geometry is read only through `App.mesh` / `App.edges` / `K.dimGeometry` / `K.resolvePlane`. The code is a new `anvil/ui-select.js` (plus the pure `BoxSelect` geometry object in the same file); other files got small hooks: `ui-core.js` (2 lines: `UI.drawSelectionExtras` at the end of `updateHighlights`, `UI.entitySelected` for sketch colours), `ui-sketch.js` (box hook on mousedown, `UI.pickHover`, `UI.selectClick`, `UI.sketchSelectClick`, vertex / body hover text, `UI.autoDims` export), `ui-panels.js` (Instant3D ribbon button live, *View ▸ Selection Filter (F5)*, `data-fid` on feature rows), `index.html` (script tag), `style.css` (appended block).

**Done.**
- Multi-selection: `App.multi` holds the whole list while `App.multi[0] === App.sel` (and the mesh / mode it was made on), so every existing `App.sel = …` still works and simply starts a new selection; `App.sel2` is the second item (Measure unchanged). Ctrl / Shift + click toggles. Items 3… are highlighted by the hook; vertices are drawn as points. Delete removes several selected sketch entities in one undo step.
- Box selection: a left press on empty space starts the box (a press on the model still rotates, so every existing drag keeps its meaning); left → right window (blue solid, fully inside), right → left crossing (green dashed, touching); Ctrl adds, Shift toggles; Esc cancels. Parts select sharp edges, assemblies components, sketches entities; with a filter the filtered kinds (faces by their triangles, bodies over all faces, vertices, visible sketches). A box in Fillet / Chamfer / flange PMs feeds their edge box.
- Selection Filter toolbar (F5, floating at the viewport's top left): Toggle (F6, suspends), Clear All, Select All, Faces, Edges, Vertices, Solid Bodies, Components, Sketch Entities; respected by clicks, hover, box selection and every `App.command.onPick` selection box; switching a filter on drops disallowed items. Vertex picking (8 px, occlusion like edges) exists only under Filter Vertices.
- FeatureManager sync: selected features / bodies / component rows marked, the owner feature of a selected face or edge gets an *owner* mark, Ctrl / Shift + click on a row toggles it into the selection; Esc clears both.
- Instant3D (on by default, *Features ▸ Instant3D* toggles): a selected end face of a blind / mid-plane Boss-Extrude or Cut-Extrude, or the cap of a Revolve under 360° (or the feature row) shows a drag arrow; dragging snaps to a ruler (1-2-5 steps ≥ 8 px; 1° / 5° / 15° on the arc) and rebuilds live (coalesced; worker or not), one undo step, the end face is reselected, and a value box opens on release (Enter applies within the same undo step). The feature's sketch dimensions are drawn in the viewport with drag dots (placed + driven dims), dragging re-solves via `SketchSolver.setDim`; clicking a value opens the box. In a sketch the dots sit on the dimension arrows. Revolve direction is found from the cap face itself (angle of its centroid from the profile side), so the kernel's rotation sign is not assumed.
- `anvil.selection / selectionFilter / boxSelect / clearSelection / instant3D`; `UI.selection / setSelection / selectionFilter / boxItems / applyBox / instant3DHandles / instant3DEdit / BoxSelect`.

**Tests.** kernel 590 → 596 (group `box selection geometry`: rect mode, segments, triangles, polylines, combine, ruler steps / snap). ui-smoke 131 → 138 (7 steps, real synthetic mouse drags: window 4 / crossing 8 edges on a 40 × 20 × 10 block with box colours, Ctrl / Shift; Faces 1 / 5, Vertices 4, Bodies 1 / 0; F5 toolbar, clicks under each filter, Fillet box under Filter Faces, F6; Instant3D depth 10 → 30 = 24000 one undo step, typed 25 = 20000, Undo 8000; cut depth 4 → 6, revolve 90° → 135° (× 1.5) → typed 180°, ribbon toggle; sketch dim 40 → 50 in the part (10000) and 20 → 25 in the sketch; sketch box / Delete / undo, assembly crossing selects Pin<2>, tree marks, Ctrl-click row, Esc).

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765 via `python -m http.server`, three.js r128 from npm three@0.128.0 routed in place of the CDN URL in the harness only).** kernel-tests 596/596, ui-smoke 138/138, app page loads with no console errors. Then origin/main moved to 636cbf8 (PR #15, sub-assemblies, cloud-2026-09-25o) and was merged into this branch: conflicts only in SESSION_LOG.md, style.css (both appended blocks kept, 25o first) and README's *Tests* paragraphs (main's text plus this run's sentences). After the merge: kernel-tests 613/613, ui-smoke 141/141, app page loads with no console errors. Screenshots of the depth arrow in the Front view, the sketch dimensions drawn in 3D with the value box, and the sketch-mode dots looked right.

**Decisions.**
- Left-drag on the model keeps rotating (Anvil's long-standing mouse map; tests and users rely on it); boxes start on empty space. SolidWorks rotates only with the middle button.
- Default box contents follow SolidWorks: edges in parts, components in assemblies, entities in sketches; faces / vertices / bodies need the filter.
- No occlusion test in box selection (hidden-behind items are included), like SolidWorks' default for wireframe-style box selection; the ROADMAP keeps an option for later.
- The typed-value box closes (without applying) on blur, so a box left open can never write into a model restored by Undo.

**Known gaps.** No lasso; Instant3D only on extrude / revolve and sketch dimensions (no fillet radius, shell thickness, pattern spacing, no direct face dragging); no Instant3D for through-all / second-direction extrudes; the vertex filter's points come from sharp edges only (no silhouette vertices on round faces).

**Next up.**
1. Instant3D on more features (fillet / chamfer / shell / pattern values) and Move Face (Direct Editing).
2. Lasso selection and a "box select visible items only" option (ray tests per candidate).
3. Selection filter in drawings (edges / dimensions / annotations) through drawing.js's own picking.

## Run cloud-2026-09-25p — True curves: exact circles, arcs, cylinders, cones, spheres and tori

**Context.** Cloud session (no HEAD.lock, board or republish). Board request: "make the arcs radii & circles true not segmented lines". Head did kernel.js / csg.js / tests; three subagents did drawing.js, exchange.js and the viewport (ui-core / ui-app / ui-panels / ui-commands, ui-smoke.html). assembly.js / ui-assembly.js untouched.

**Done.**
- Profiles: `profile.curves` + `loop.seg` mark which segments are chords of which circle / arc.
- Solids: `polygon.surf = { s: { t: cyl | cone | sph | tor, o, d, r, … }, P?, Q? }` on every facet of a curved face (extrude, drafted extrude → cone, holes, revolves, fillets between planar faces), carried by CSG splits, clones, transforms and pack / unpack.
- `massProps` exact: sliver integration per fragment (Gauss-Legendre on fan triangles, closed-form columns; two-level for spheres / tori; side walls for areas). Cylinders, holes, counterbores, bosses, cones, frusta, spheres, tori, partial revolves, drafted cylinders and fillets match their formulas to ≤ 1e-11; 14 Design Library parts are tessellation-independent to 1e-11.
- `Kernel.curveEdges` / `curveSurfaces`; chord-tolerance tessellation (0.05 mm, n multiple of 8, 16..128), `setTessellation`, `model.tessellation` (cache key, worker), `legacy: true` reproduces old meshes bit for bit.
- Drawings: exact circles / arcs / ellipses (SVG, DXF, PDF), visible / hidden arcs, exact silhouettes, R dimensions. STEP: CYLINDRICAL / CONICAL / SPHERICAL / TOROIDAL faces with CIRCLE edges and seams; import of those; OpenCascade validity + volumes to 1e-15 (checked independently by the head on cylinder, bolt plate, counterbore, torus, fillet). Viewport: analytic normals, no seams, 128-segment curve edges, Tools ▸ Options ▸ Image Quality.
- Examples: Gear train's Gear A turned 90° and the roller 7.5° so the 24-gon bores / flats line up (Tangent and interference in assembly.js still use facets).

**Tests.** Old polygonal pins run under `withLegacy` (unchanged numbers); analytic pins moved to πr² formulas with comments; new groups `true curves: kernel / drawings / STEP`. Verified on the branch before the attempted main merge: kernel-tests 640/640, ui-smoke 135/135, app with no console errors. After merging origin/main (PRs #15, #16) kernel was 665/665 and the app clean but ui-smoke did not finish within 15 min; the merge was aborted and is left to the foreman.

**Bench** (sum of medians, idle): 422.1 ms before → 775.4 ms with the default 0.05 mm tolerance; 434.5 ms with `?legacy` (the records cost ~3 %; the rest is the finer mesh, mostly the Funnel shell).

**Next up.** 1. Tangent mate / interference with analytic radii (assembly session). 2. Surface records for sweeps, lofts between coaxial circles, shells of round faces, fillets along curved edges. 3. Faster shells on fine meshes. 4. Tighten curveEdges on trimmed cones (Hex nut chords 0.03 off the circle).

## Run cloud-2026-09-26r — Analytic mates, exact interference / clearance, drawing BOMs across levels

**Context.** Cloud session, small budget, no helpers (no HEAD.lock, board or republish). Follow-ups to cloud-2026-09-25p (true curves) and -25o (sub-assemblies).

**Done.**
- `assembly.js`: `analyticCylinder` reads a patch's `polygon.surf` cylinder record (axis point, canonical direction, radius); `findCylinder`, `refFromPick` and `pickInner` try it before the facet fit (`cylinderFromPatch`, kept for record-free meshes). Tangent (cylinder–plane, cylinder–cylinder), Concentric and Distance between cylinders therefore use the true axis and radius: a lying Ø8 pin sits with its axis exactly 4 above the face at any turn about its axis. `findCylinder` results carry `exact: true`.
- Interference: `Assembly.interference` and `Evaluate.interference` (and `minDistance`'s interfering test) take the common volume from `Kernel.massProps` (curve correction) when the common body has curved facets (`exactVolume`): the Interference check example's Base ∩ Pin<2> is now π·4²·8 (was the 16-gon's area × 8).
- Clearance: `Evaluate.exactClearance` refines the facet result when the closest points lie on exact cylinders: parallel cylinders outside each other (D − rA − rB), one inside the other's hole (rB − rA − D), cylinder and a plane parallel to its axis (|n·c − w| − r); the facet value only picks the configuration (within 5 % of the radii) and the gap must run square to the axes. Other cases keep the facet value.
- Gear train example: the roller's 7.5° insert turn is gone (Tangent now rests it at exactly 15); its check pins y = 15. Gear A keeps its 90° turn (it sets the Gear mate's phase), comment updated: the bores no longer need to line up facet for facet.
- `drawing.js`: BOM Type *Indented* (new, `BOM_TYPES` has three entries, `Drawing.bomType`) and *Parts only* across sub-assembly levels come from `Assembly.bom` (items 1.1 / 3.2.2, `row.level`, quantities multiplied through levels); multi-body parts in Parts only still split into bodies; top-level rows keep their body indices for balloons. PM select, status text and `anvil.drawing.bomType` accept 'indented'.

**Tests.** kernel 665 → 671: Tangent tests pinned to the exact radius (1e-9, were the apothem); new group `analytic mates, interference and clearance` (Tangent at 0° / 7° / 11.25° turns → axis height 14 to 1e-9, pin–peg 6 / 2; Concentric of a 7°-turned gear bore on its shaft to 1e-9, record radius 3, no interference; Distance 20 between turned pins; interference π·4²·8 to 1e-9 in Assembly and Evaluate; clearance 9 / 2 / 3 to 1e-9 for pin–peg, peg in hole, pin over a block; stripped records fall back to the fit); `Drawing BOM across levels` in the sub-assemblies group. ui-smoke 145/145 (the Interference PM step pinned to π·4²·8, the Tangent mate step to 10 + 4).

**Verified (headless Chromium via Playwright, `python -m http.server 8765` in anvil/, three.js r128 from npm three@0.128.0 routed in place of the CDN URL in the harness only).** Before: kernel 665/665, ui-smoke 145/145. After: kernel-tests 671/671, ui-smoke 145/145, app page loads with no console errors.

**Known gaps.** Exact clearance only for parallel cylinders and cylinder–plane (no skew cylinders, cones, spheres or tori); Concentric on cones / spheres still unsupported; interference is exact only as far as the kernel's curve correction (`Kernel.massProps`) is for the common body.

**Next up.** 1. Concentric / Tangent on cones and spheres from their records. 2. Exact clearance for spheres and skew cylinders. 3. Surface records for sweeps, lofts and shells (from 25p).


## Run cloud-2026-09-26s — Sketch solver: Symmetric, driven (reference) dimensions, construction geometry

**Context.** Cloud session (not the scheduled local run): no HEAD.lock, no Foreman board, no artifact republish, no subagents. Task: the sketch-solver gaps listed in cloud-2026-09-25i. Branch `cloud/sketch-symmetric`, PR to main (the foreman integrates). kernel.js was not edited: the kernel already had `construction: true` (skipped by `sketchProfile`, kept by offset / trim), so no profile change was needed. Tests in headless Chromium (Playwright, /opt/pw-browsers) against `python3 -m http.server 8765`, three.js r128 routed from npm `three@0.128.0` (cdnjs blocked; runner only). Baseline on c926ab1: kernel 671/671, smoke 145/145.

**Done (sketch-solver.js).**
- `symmetric` relation, refs `[a, b, axis]`: two points, two lines or two circles / arcs and the line they mirror about. Each mirrored pair gives two equations (midpoint on the axis, chord ⟂ axis); two lines pair their ends the way they mirror when the relation is made (4 equations); circles add equal radii (5). Add Relations offers it for three picks (like, like, line) in any order, preferring a construction line as the axis.
- Inference: a line drawn with its ends mirrored about a centerline gets Symmetric (its Horizontal is then redundant and skipped), a point / line / circle drawn as the mirror of an existing one gets Symmetric with it.
- Driven dimensions: `driven: true` on a distance / angle relation or a Smart Dimension removes its equation; `analyze` reports `'driven'`; `updateDriven` (run after every solve) rewrites the measured `v` of driven relations; `setDim` / `setRelValue` refuse driven ones; `overDefines(sketch, kind, i)` and `setDriven(sketch, kind, i, on)`.
- Over-definition is judged by counts, not only by the added rows: the row reduction flags whichever equation comes last, so a new relation that repeats an older Smart Dimension used to read "ok" while the dimension turned yellow. `addRelation` and `infer` now compare redundant / conflict counts before and after; `overDefines` compares the DOF with and without the item.
- Script round trip without kernel.js: `.construction([i…])`, `.driven([i…])`, `.rel("distance", refs, { driven: true })`, written by the `generateScript` wrapper before `.id();`. (Construction flags did not round-trip through scripts before this.)

**Done (UI).** `ui-relations.js`: the *Make Dimension Driven?* page modal (`#drv-driven` *Make this dimension driven* / `#drv-driving` *Leave this dimension driving*) when a new single-entity or two-entity dimension would over-define the sketch; clicking a driven dimension offers *Make driving*; *Construction Geometry* toggles the selected entities; *Centerline* = the Line tool drawing construction lines; `anvil.construction()`, `anvil.driven()`, `UI.setDimDriven`. `ui-sketch.js` hooks: Centerline flag in `addEntity` (reset by `setTool`), the question after a Smart Dimension is placed, driven labels in parentheses, a driven dimension's click goes to the modal. `ui-core.js`: construction entities drawn with a dashed twin of their state material. `ui-panels.js`: the two ribbon buttons in the Add Relation stack. `icons.js`: `relSymmetric`, `construction`, `centerline`. `style.css`: `.dim.driven` grey.

**Tests.** kernel 671 → 676: Symmetric points (free axis 8 → 6 DOF, fixed axis 4 → 2, exact mirror, axis ordering), lines (8 → 4) / circles (6 → 3, equal radii) / one line's ends (4 → 2, a Horizontal on top is redundant), inference (line across a centerline, mirrored circle, preview), driven dimensions (distance repeating the height: over-defines, driven → Fully Defined, reads 20 then 30 after the height edit, edits refused; driven Smart Dimension follows a width edit; driving again over-defines), construction (1 loop, extrude volume of the rectangle alone, `.construction` / `.driven` / driven `.rel` round trip and fixed point). Smoke 145 → 146: Centerline from the ribbon (dashed material), a line across it infers Symmetric, the second dimension on it opens the modal, *Make this dimension driven* → grey "(16)", a 16 → 30 edit keeps the symmetry and the driven label reads "(30)", Construction Geometry on / off.

**Verified (headless Chromium, port 8765).** kernel-tests 676/676, ui-smoke 146/146, app page loads with 0 console errors.

**Decisions.**
- Symmetric between two arcs constrains centres and radii only (not the end angles).
- The dash length of construction lines is set from the zoom when the sketch is redrawn (not on every zoom step).
- "Leave this dimension driving" keeps the over-defining dimension (yellow), as SolidWorks does.

**Known gaps.** No Symmetric from Mirror Entities yet (SolidWorks adds it to mirrored copies); no tangent / perpendicular inference while drawing; Pierce / Merge / Intersection relations; construction lines are not offered as revolve axes by name.

**Next up.**
1. Mirror Entities about a centerline adds Symmetric relations to the copies.
2. Tangent / perpendicular inference from an end point while drawing a line or arc.
3. Concentric / Tangent mates on cones and spheres (from 26r).

## Run cloud-2026-09-26t — Hole Wizard: Counterbore, Countersink, Hole, Straight Tap (ISO M2–M20)

**Context.** Last cloud session on the credit, small budget: no HEAD.lock, board, subagents or republish. ROADMAP Phase 7 "Hole Wizard types". Branch `cloud/hole-wizard`, PR to main (the foreman integrates). Baseline on f6fae6f: kernel 676/676, smoke 146/146.

**Done.**
- `holewizard.js` (new, no DOM, loaded by the app, kernel tests and the rebuild worker): ISO coarse table M2–M20 (ISO 273 clearance Close / Normal / Loose, ISO 4762 counterbore Ø / depth, ISO 10642 countersink Ø at 90°, tap drill), the `hole` feature (`holeType`, `size`, `fit`, `end` blind / through, `depth`, `threadDepth`, `sketch` = a points sketch on the face). Each hole is a `revolve` tool of its half section on a plane through the axis, built through `Kernel.buildTool`, so the walls carry true-curve records; several points union into one tool. `p.holeWizard({ sketch | face + offset + points, type, size, fit, depth | end: 'through', threadDepth })`, the generateScript line, `HoleWizard.spec / threads / holeName`. Names follow SolidWorks: *CBORE for M6 Socket Head Cap Screw1*, *M8x1.25 Tapped Hole1*.
- `kernel.js` (minimal, as agreed): `registerFeature(type, { build, cut, script })` and three one-line hooks (buildTool `default`, `isCut`, generateScript `default`). No other kernel change.
- `ui-holewizard.js` (new): Hole Wizard PM with Type / Positions tab buttons, Hole Type, Hole Specifications (Standard ISO, Size, Fit, a read-out of the sizes), End Condition (Blind depth / Through All, thread depth for taps), Hole Position (first face click places the first hole, more clicks on the same face add holes, Remove last / Clear), live preview, OK = points sketch + hole feature in one undo step; edit rewrites both. Cosmetic threads: dashed circle at the major diameter on the face (`UI.updateCosmeticThreads`, called from `applyBuild` in ui-core.js).
- Wiring: ribbon / menu *Hole Wizard* → `UI.cmdHoleWizard` (the sheet-metal *Simple Hole* and `UI.cmdHole` stay), `editFeature` case, tree icon `holeWizard` and tooltip, index.html / worker.js script tags.
- `drawing.js`: dashed cosmetic-thread circles in orthographic views along the thread axis (not section / detail views); the hole callout of a tapped hole adds `M8x1.25 ↧ 12` (or `THRU`) under the tap drill line.

**Tests.** kernel 676 → 683 (group *Hole Wizard*): ISO table sanity; volume removed for hole / cbore / csk / tap against πR²D, the counterbore and frustum formulas to 1e-9; three through-all counterbores; counterbore wall 6.5 high on the cylinder records and countersink included angle 90° from the cone records; script round trip (identical second generation); error messages; drawing callout `Ø6.8 ↧ 16 M8x1.25 ↧ 12`, the Ø8 dashed thread circle at the analytic sheet position, and a counterbore callout `Ø6.6 ↧ 16 ⌴ Ø11 ↧ 6.5` with no thread. Smoke 146 → 147: two face clicks, Type tab → Counterbore M6 blind 16, volume, tree name and icon, one undo step, edit to Straight Tap M8 → 2 cosmetic threads, script round trip.

**Verified (headless Chromium via Playwright, `python3 -m http.server 8765` in anvil/, three.js r128 routed from npm three@0.128.0 in the runner only).** kernel-tests 683/683, ui-smoke 147/147, app page loads with 0 console errors.

**Decisions.** Flat-bottom blind holes (no 118° drill point), so drawing callouts read the depth to the bottom and volumes stay simple; the thread is cosmetic only (the solid is the tap drill); a hole feature's positions live in an ordinary sketch of points (editable in sketch mode too).

**Known gaps.** No hole series, no drill-point angle option, no ANSI inch / pipe tap sizes, no Near side / Far side countersinks or Up To Surface; patterned holes (LPattern of a hole) are cut but carry no cosmetic thread; points are placed by clicking (no dimensions to them from the PM, though the sketch can be dimensioned).

**Next up.** 1. Hole series and Up To Next / Up To Surface end conditions. 2. Drill point option with callouts measured to the shoulder. 3. Cosmetic threads on patterned / mirrored holes and in section views.

## Run local-2026-09-26a — Whole curved faces for picking and selection

**Context.** Local session (Opus 5.5, normal plan usage), at the user's request with a screenshot: hovering or selecting the side of a boss highlighted one thin facet strip at a time. Holes and bosses must pick as full surfaces, as in SolidWorks.

**Done.**
- `ui-core.js`: `faceGroups(mesh)` / `UI.faceGroup(i)`. Mesh faces on the same analytic surface (key from `Kernel.curveSurfaces`), in the same body and connected by shared vertices form one selectable face; a planar face is its own group; cached per mesh. `selectionRanges` highlights the whole group for face hover and selection, and hovering another facet of the selected cylinder no longer draws a hover on top of it.
- `ui-select.js`: box selection tests each whole curved face (window: all of it inside; crossing: any part) and lists it once; `selKey` maps any facet to its group, so Ctrl / Shift-click on another facet of a selected cylinder toggles that same face.
- `tests/ui-smoke.html`: new step on the Bushing (147 mesh faces → 6 selectable: 3 cylinders + 3 planar), the Ø30 group holds all its facets, bore / body / flange stay separate, real Front-view clicks (click selects, Ctrl-click on a neighbouring facet toggles it off), crossing box over the part lists 6 faces.

**Decision.** The kernel keeps one mesh face per facet: an attempt to merge facets in `meshFromBody` broke 39 kernel tests (shell offsets each face by its plane, fillet / chamfer edge resolution, drawing silhouettes and hidden lines, mesh quality and exports all rely on planar mesh faces). The grouping lives in the UI layer where picking happens.

**Verified (port 8812, pane visible).** kernel-tests 683/683, ui-smoke 148/148, app page with no console errors.

**Next up.** Commands that take a picked face (Concentric / Tangent mates, Fillet face selection, Hole Wizard refusal on curved faces) could read `UI.faceGroup` to show the whole face in their PropertyManager selection boxes.

## Run local-2026-09-26h — OpenCascade experiment

**Context.** The user asked whether SolidWorks-level modelling is possible; the proposed route is a real B-rep kernel (OpenCascade) under Anvil's UI. This run tests it in the browser, offline.

**Done.** `experiments/occt-lab.html` builds Plate with bolt holes, Bushing, Rounded handle, Mug and Funnel with both kernels (time, volume, faces, triangles, a three.js view with one colour per B-rep face) plus OCCT-only checks (fillet every edge, fillet the Mug handle joints, exact STEP). `experiments/get-occt.ps1` fetches opencascade.js 2.0.0-beta.b5ff984 (50.3 MB wasm, sha256 pinned; not committed, `.gitignore`). `serve.ps1` serves `.wasm` as application/wasm. Findings and the recommendation in `experiments/OCCT.md`.

**Findings.** Loads in 0.9-1.9 s from localhost. Volumes agree (Plate / Bushing exact in both, Rounded handle 0.002 %, Funnel 0.05 %, Mug 0.7 % from Anvil's faceted sweep). True faces: 10 / 6 / 16 / 8 / 6 against 102 / 147 / 332 / 851 / 258 mesh faces. Total time about equal (OCCT slower on booleans and sweeps, 5-10x faster on loft + shell). ## Run local-2026-09-26k — OpenCascade kernel, stage 2 (fillet / chamfer, shell, draft, loft, sweep, Hole Wizard)

**Done (`occt-kernel.js`).**
- Fillet / chamfer: the body nearest the first pick (Kernel's pickBody), faces unified first, then the edges nearest each picked point within max(0.5, size); OpenCascade carries them along tangent edges (SolidWorks' tangent propagation).
- Shell: faces nearest the picked points removed, `MakeThickSolidByJoin` with Intersection joins; no faces → closed hollow (body − inward `MakeOffsetShape`).
- Drafted extrude: ruled `ThruSections` between the section offset (`MakeOffset`, Intersection join) at each end, split at the sketch plane when the range crosses it; holes grow where the material shrinks, as Kernel.buildExtrude.
- Loft: smooth `ThruSections` through the sections' outer loops (SolidWorks' loft; Anvil's is ruled).
- Sweep: `MakePipeShell` (corrected Frenet, right-corner transitions) along the path sketch's lines and arcs built exactly and chained from the end nearest the profile, or a 3D polyline; the profile turned about the path start to face the first segment, per loop, holes subtracted.
- Hole Wizard: `HoleWizard.section` revolved about each position (shared `revolveSketch`).
- Result `tolerance`: 1e-4 relative, 2e-2 when the part has a sweep, a loft or an edge pick with chain: false (Anvil facets those / stops at the picked edge). Used by ui-occt.js and occt-tests.html.

**Tests.** occt-tests 34/34 (every part example except the three sheet-metal ones, plus Hole Wizard types, closed hollow shell, drafts, a line-arc-line sweep with its exact volume π·9·(20 + 7.5π + 25), a fillet on a hole rim); examples with a known `volume` must now match it to 1e-6 in OpenCascade. ui-smoke 151/151 (OpenCascade step: Mug 8 faces, U-bracket "needs sheet metal"). kernel-tests 683/683.

**Findings.** OpenCascade's volumes are the exact ones where Anvil approximates: the line-arc-line sweep 1938.54 vs Anvil 1915.80 (1.2 % low, faceted arc), the Mug 85025.2 vs 85618.3.

**Next up.** Stage 3: display OpenCascade's mesh when the switch is on (true faces for every pick, including coplanar faces Anvil merges), drawings via HLRBRep, STEP / IGES through OpenCascade; sheet metal last.

## Run local-2026-10-06a — Structure: headless Node runner, kernel.js and drawing.js split

**Context.** Interactive session (not the scheduled task): the user asked for the two "large improvements" that change what Anvil can do rather than how it looks — a headless runner so Claude can model without a browser, and the two monoliths broken up so more lanes can work at once. Three lanes in parallel: `kernel` and `drawing` in their own git worktrees (served on 8814 / 8813), `headless` in the main checkout. Node.js 24 LTS installed on this machine for the runner.

**Done.**
- **Headless** (`anvil/headless/anvil.js` 434 lines, `load.js`, `occt.js`, `xmldom.js`, root `package.json`; README *Headless (Node)*): `node anvil/headless/anvil.js build | drawing | example | examples | test`. `load.js` loads the DOM-free engine set that `tests/kernel-tests.html` lists (parsed from its `<script src>` tags, so file splits are picked up automatically) with `vm.runInThisContext`; `occt.js` loads OpenCascade in Node after the same SHA-256 checks as `occt-worker.js` (the ESM takes emscripten's Node path, so it is run as a CommonJS-style wrapper). `build` prints a JSON summary (volume, area, centroid, bbox, massKg via `Materials.massProps`, faces, errors) and writes STL / STEP (exact with `--occt`) / flat-pattern DXF / face DXF / model JSON / script; `drawing` writes SVG / DXF / PDF of the standard views through the `Drawing` engine half; `example --all` checks every example volume; `test` runs the kernel test page under a small DOM shim with `Worker` on `worker_threads`, `DOMParser` from `xmldom.js` and a synchronous `XMLHttpRequest` for fixtures. `npm test` / `npm run examples`.
- **Kernel split** (`kernel.js` 336 lines root + `kernel-sketch.js`, `kernel-curves.js`, `kernel-equations.js`, `kernel-mesh.js`, `kernel-rebuild.js`, `kernel-features.js`, `kernel-sheetmetal.js`, `kernel-script.js`; 4,776 lines total): `Kernel` is one object created in `kernel.js` and extended by each file's IIFE `(function (K, _) { … })(Kernel, Kernel._)`; 77 shared privates and state objects on the non-enumerable `Kernel._` (so `Object.keys(Kernel)` is unchanged: 105 keys, identical before / after); 142 top-of-file aliases from earlier files, 17 call sites changed to `_.name` for later files; every other moved line byte-identical. Load lists updated in index.html, worker.js, drawing-worker.js, occt-worker.js, the three test pages, experiments/occt-lab.html, README.
- **Drawing split** (`drawing.js` 597 lines root + engine `drawing-curves.js`, `drawing-hlr.js`, `drawing-views.js`, `drawing-sheet.js`, `drawing-annotations.js`, `drawing-render.js`; UI `drawing-ui.js`, `drawing-ui-tools.js`, `drawing-ui-panels.js`): same mechanism, 61 privates on `Drawing._`, the UI half's shared closure state on `UI.drawingInternal` (103 members); `Drawing`, `UI.drawing` and `anvil.drawing` key sets identical before / after; SVG hash and DXF / PDF lengths of the same drawing identical to the monolith. drawing-worker.js imports the engine files only.
- **Standard toolbar** (`index.html`, `style.css`, `icons.js`, `ui-panels.js` renderStdToolbar / syncStdToolbar, README): Undo and Redo buttons right after the menus, where SolidWorks' Standard toolbar has them; greyed when their stack is empty (synced from refreshTree, which every rebuild / undo / redo / rename reaches), two-line tooltips with Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y and a "Nothing to undo" note; ui-smoke step added (302 steps now).
- **CLAUDE.md**: lane table (`kernel`, `drawing`, new `headless`), file table, publish list now says to read index.html's script list, step 4.2 runs `node anvil/headless/anvil.js test` first, conventions line fixed (the head pushes).

**Decisions.** Splits are pure moves behind an internal namespace rather than ES modules, to keep the no-build, script-tag loading. The headless loader derives its file list from the kernel test page so there is one list to maintain for the DOM-free set. The published app's file map must now include the eight `kernel-*.js` and nine `drawing-*.js` files.

**Verified (merged tree on port 8812).** Node: `example --all` 29/29, `test` 811/811 (43.9 s). Browser: kernel-tests 811/811, occt-tests 56/56, ui-smoke 297/301 and 296/301 (two runs) — the failing steps differ between runs and are all pointer-drag steps (Instant3D arrow drag, click-drag Line, Trim trail, Move Component delta); the **unchanged main** (bee686b in a worktree on 8814, same pane, 972×599) scores 296/301 with its own set of five drag failures, so the misses come from the browser pane this session (it was resized during the runs), not from the split. Resolved the same session: `anvil/headless/ui-smoke.js` (`npm run ui-smoke`) runs the page in headless Chrome over the DevTools protocol (1600 × 1000 window, page visible, nothing throttled) — **301/301 in 111 s** on the merged tree. Use it instead of the pane for ui-smoke from now on..

## Run local-2026-10-06c — Sketch selection, tangent snapping, snapping integrity audit; true circles on screen

**Context.** Interactive session. User: "circles are STILL not true circles" (a screenshot of a faceted sketch circle); then "when doing something like linear sketch pattern the sketch circle or line gets de-selected as the cursor operation, add tangent snapping when clicking on a circle when having a line selected, and do a thorough comb of snapping integrity" — with the head (Fable 5.1) reviewing every line from two Opus 5.5 lanes (`sketch`: tangent snapping; `tests`: the audit, in worktrees on 8813 / 8814).

**Done.**
- **True circles on screen** (head, `ui-core.js` screenPolylines, `ui-sketch.js` hover): sketch circles, arcs and slots tessellated for the zoom (0.15 px sagitta, 32–2048 segments per turn, redrawn when the zoom moves 30 %); the kernel's 0.05 mm chord tolerance is swapped only for the display call; model-side profiles unchanged (a Ø10 circle stays 24-sided for the solid).
- **Sketch commands consume their selection** (head, `ui-commands.js` closePM): a PropertyManager that closes in a sketch (✓ or ✗) deselects the entities it was started with, as SolidWorks does (`spec.keepSelection` opts out).
- **Tangent snapping** (`sketch` lane, `ui-sketch.js` lineEndTangent / curveTangentDirs, `sketch-solver.js` smoothCandidates): with the Line tool the end locks onto the exact tangent point of a circle / arc from the first point (7 px of the curve, 6 px of the tangent point, Tangent glyph), a line started on a circle / arc locks onto its tangent there; the click adds Tangent after the end's Coincident (pair `ok`, not redundant); the end-tangent lock runs before the start's direction lock, so the end wins when both could apply. Kernel tests 811 → 814.
- **Snapping audit** (`tests` lane, 11 defects, 3 fixed there, 8 applied by the head): drop-merge measured analytically on every other entity (the old pickEntity found the dragged entity itself: the snap blinked and a slow drop 2 px off a line merged with nothing); drop on a midpoint gives Midpoint and the glyph names the relation; an over-defining merge says so and the point stays; box selection and `pickEntity` test the curve as drawn (a click between model facets at deep zoom picked nothing); rectangle / polygon / polyline edges are on-curve snap targets; woken points are forgotten with their entity (Undo, Delete); a tool's point cannot land on one it already placed (a Centerpoint Arc on its own centre made a 360° arc, a slot a 0.01-wide slot); each typed-value Enter is its own undo step; grid rounding / alignment that lands on a curve shows the snap ring; the crossing of two lines gets Coincident with both; Polygon / Center Rectangle centres are sketch points (Coincident, picks, drops) while Corner Rectangle / slot centres are no snap at all; the centre glyph says Concentric only when a centre is being placed; `inferCandidates`' tolerance no longer falls through on a 0 coordinate. Checked sound: pixel tolerances at three zooms, snap order and ties, one relation per snapped end, Tangent never with Perpendicular, typed values, Smart Dimension placement at any zoom, pre-highlight = click.
- README *Sketching* rewritten for the above; the Line tool's Esc wording fixed.

**Verified (merged tree, 8812).** kernel-tests 818/818 (Node), ui-smoke 317/317 headless (the audit's six former `(known: …)` steps and two kernel tests flipped to assert the fixed behaviour).

**Next up.** `sharedEnd` follows only a direct end-to-curve Coincident, so a chained line leaving a point on a circle cannot get Tangent (the start lock is off there); `curveSnap` runs before the start-tangent lock, so near the start the tangent line still snaps to the circle itself (the arc-end lock has the same limit); Round-14 follow-ups in QUEUE.md.

## Run local-2026-10-06d — Tangent line from a circle (Mastercam-style), SolidWorks double-click, smoke driver fix

**Context.** The user sent a Mastercam screen recording ("MAKE it do this"): Line Endpoints with *Tangent* on — the first click lands on a circle and the line's start then slides round the circle so the line stays tangent wherever the cursor goes, the second click fixes the free end. Then: "make double clicks work like they do in SolidWorks i.e. double clicks delete last line". Head only (small change); frames of the clip were rendered with WPF's MediaPlayer (no ffmpeg on this machine).

**Done.**
- **Tangent-sliding start** (`ui-sketch.js` tangentStart, placePoint, inferPoint, toolEntities; README *Line tool*): a Line whose first point lands on a circle / arc by the on-curve snap (not one of its points) gets `st.tanFrom`; from then on the start is the exact tangent point of that curve for the cursor (the one nearer the previous start, so it sweeps round continuously; an arc's only inside its sweep; inside the circle the line starts at the clicked point), the Tangent glyph rides the cursor, the click adds Tangent after the start's Coincident, and the chain goes on from the free end. **T** toggles drawing from the clicked point instead. While sliding, the direction locks and alignments with the clicked point are skipped; the free end still snaps to points and curves. The earlier 6 px start-direction lock (r15/tangent15) is superseded on circles / arcs; its test now accepts the slid start.
- **Double-click** (`ui-sketch.js` placePoint / dblclick): the chain ends without the stub its second click made — a line placed within 500 ms of the previous one and shorter than 6 px on screen is taken back with its relations, dimensions and undo snapshot (the two clicks of a double-click are a pixel or two apart, which made a tiny segment).
- **Headless smoke driver** (`headless/ui-smoke.js`): the pre-flight request is a HEAD. It was a GET whose 640 KB body was never read, and `anvil/serve.ps1` is single-threaded and blocks on writing a response the client does not drain, so the browser's own request for the page starved for the driver's lifetime (every run timed out with an empty summary). The page tab is the one opened at launch (no `/json/new`), the Chrome / Edge path is found automatically, and the port can be changed (`--port`).

**Verified (8812).** kernel-tests 818/818 (Node); ui-smoke 319/319 headless (two new steps: the tangent slide with Tangent + Coincident and the T toggle; the double-click stub removal).

- **Scale bar** (user request with a Mastercam crop; `index.html` #scale-bar, `style.css`, `ui-core.js` updateScaleBar / `UI.scaleBar()`, README *Viewport*): a 100 px bar at the bottom right of the viewport labelled with its length in model units at the current zoom (2 decimals in mm, 4 in inches), updated per frame when the text changes, hidden under a drawing document. ui-smoke step added (320).

- **Trim between tangent lines** (user screenshot: Power trim across the small arc of a circle between two lines tangent to it deleted the whole circle; `kernel-sketch.js` primIntersections, `sketch-solver.js` tPrimHits, kernel test): a tangent line touches a circle at one point and its discriminant came out within rounding of zero, often slightly negative, so no crossing was found, nothing split the circle and "an entity nothing crosses is deleted" applied. A line within 1e-7 of touching now counts as touching, once; the trim span highlight (tCuts) and the Trim to closest / Power trim paths share the fix.

- **Inches by default** (user request "MAKE anvil default to inches"; `ui-core.js` defaultUnits / setDefaultUnits / lenDefault / imagePresets, `ui-app.js` newPart / newAssembly / run, `ui-panels.js` Tools menu, `ui-commands.js` PropertyManager defaults, `kernel-rebuild.js`, `ui-sketch.js` fmtDim, README *Units*): new parts and assemblies start in inches (IPS); *Tools ▸ Default units for new documents* switches to millimeters (localStorage `anvil.units`); a script's own `.units()` wins (the examples are metric), saved documents keep theirs, the headless runner and `Kernel.newModel` stay mm. An inch document with no Image Quality of its own is built at 0.002 in (the 0.05 mm default scaled; it was 0.05 in, a Ø1 in circle 16-sided); the Image Quality presets come in inches; PropertyManager defaults come in shop fractions (extrude 0.375 in, fillet / chamfer / shell / offset 0.0625 in, patterns 0.75 in, simple hole Ø0.25 in); inch dimensions read three decimals. The smoke suite presets `anvil.units = mm` before boot (its geometry is metric) and one step checks the inch default end to end; a kernel test pins the inch tessellation (40 facets on a Ø1 in cylinder vs 16).

**Next up.** A typed length while the start slides is measured from the clicked point, not the sliding tangent point; the sliding start has no Mastercam-style "which tangent" flip key (the nearer tangent point wins); serve.ps1 could get a write timeout so a slow client cannot stall it.

## Fix 2026-10-07 — corrupt flat DXF on the OpenCascade kernel

**Context.** User: "fix the corrupt flat DXF". An inch bracket (Ø.281 holes at .5 from the edges) saved through File ▸ Save As DXF on the default OpenCascade kernel had two holes joined by a ~5 in slit across the blank, a stray sliver loop, and only 2 of 4 holes as circles; the same part unfolded from Anvil's own mesh was clean.

**Cause.** `Kernel.faceOutline` (kernel-mesh.js) rebuilt each edge's far end as `a + u·t` and welded vertices with `key3` (rounded to 1e-4). A Ø.281 hole at .5 has points at x = .57025, exactly on a rounding midpoint, so the recomputed end rounded to the neighbouring key; the interior triangle edge lost its twin, counted as boundary, and the loop chained from one hole into the next. OpenCascade's triangulation (long triangles between holes) exposed it; Anvil's 24-gon holes never landed on a midpoint.

**Fix.** The split pieces keep the real vertex coordinates (edge ends and the splitting vertex), and vertices are welded once by a 1e-4 neighbour-cell search (`.k`), so two copies of a point that straddle a midpoint share a key; a vertex welded to an edge's own end never splits that edge.

**Verified.** kernel-tests 863/863 (new `flat-weld` test fails on the old code); occt-tests 57/57 (new row: the inch bracket's flat from OpenCascade's mesh has the outline, 4 holes and the slot, and its DXF has 4 CIRCLEs); examples 29/29 on both kernels; ui-smoke 335/335; in the app the flat DXF is 4 outline LINEs, 4 CIRCLEs, slot 2 LINEs + 2 ARCs.

**Next up (DXF / DWG).** Views DXF is drawn at the sheet scale (a 60 × 40 plate came out 1:10) with the title block and always R12; no preview or native save dialog; edge-flange length / position defaults differ from SolidWorks (a 6 in base with 1.5 in flanges is 6.24 × 1.62 outside); DWG only via LibreDWG.

- flat: coplanar walls of two flanges on one edge line no longer add a stand-in rectangle (stray vertex, hidden wall holes); `mergeFlatRegions` 2D merge replaces the CSG region merge (`K.flatOptions.merge = 'csg'` keeps the old); `K.cleanFlatLoop`; `flat.etch` + ETCH layer in `exportDXFFlat`.
- inch flange: `meshFromBody` planeKey rounding split a sheet's top face on offsets like 0.075 in; the pick fell to the bottom edge under the mm-sized `max(0.5, T)` tolerance and the flange folded down. `coplanarBody` before meshing, picks within 0.75·T; OpenCascade `shapeToCSG` gives a planar face one plane and a 0.05 mm chord in model units. Inch STEP row in occt-tests.

## 2026-10-14e (interactive head, 1 reused Opus agent + 1 Fable 5.1 agent): one Esc ends any command; trim at T-junctions

- Esc (lane CG, the sketch-docs agent's second task): every command's Esc handler calls `UI.escapeCommand()` (ui-commands.js): open value boxes close unapplied (`UI.escapeBoxes`), the sketch tool goes down (placed segments kept), the PM is ✗ (`pmCancel`), drawing tools end (`UI.escapeTools`). Menus, flyouts, the view cube, the Level popup, modals and plain editors keep their own Esc. Parallel Line: one Esc removes the floating line and closes the PM and the distance box. Esc in the Modify box now also puts Smart Dimension down; in a drawing Esc in a Note box ends the Note tool. Head: Dynamic Mirror registers an escapeTools hook (one Esc: tool down and mirror off). Tests: rfCG; r3-sketch3, r9-sketch9, rfD, sketch-tools2, rfA (its tool loop uses cancelTool).
- trim (lane CH, Fable): root cause — `Kernel.trimEntity` and the solver's `tCuts` counted only crossings (eps 1e-9 on the other entity's parameter) and never Coincident point-on relations; each trim's `tRound` (1e-4) then moved an unrelated junction ~1e-4 off, so the next trim found no cut and deleted the whole line. New `Kernel.trimCuts` / `Kernel.entityEnds`: crossings + other entities' ends lying on the entity within 1e-9 × sketch size + ends a Coincident holds on it; own ends excluded. Used by trimAt, trimSpan (hover), Power Trim, trim inside / outside; `afterTrim` ties each junction to its kept piece (Coincident); `extendTarget` meets such ends. An end 0.001 short is not a junction (whole line deleted, as before — user). Split Entities (`crossings`, ui-sketch-construct.js) still has its own 1e-9 test: noted in sketch.md. Tests: kernel rfCH (10), smoke rfCH (2).
- agents: the sketch-docs agent retired at 422 K; the trim agent (Fable, 260 K, first fresh agent started from a card: 68 steps, 28 min) stays for trim / sketch follow-ups.

## 2026-10-14f (interactive head, the drawings-ui agent's second task): the r11/occtswap11 flake

- tests (lane CI): the step compared `App.holdFrameUntil` with the test page's `performance.now()`, which runs 1–5 s ahead of the app frame's clock (the page reads the group files before it loads the frame), and its tooth-count edit was estimated at 1.43–1.53 s against `HOLD_MAX` 1.5 s (ui-occt.js: above it Anvil's part shows at once, by design). Now it reads `W.performance.now()` and edits the keyway (estimate ~0.7 s); the held assertion is unchanged. The same clock fix in r12-occtui12 (a "not held" check passed by accident). App code unchanged. Three full headless runs in a row: 717/717.

## 2026-10-14g (interactive head, user: "scan recent changes for mistakes, let the agents fix on the fly"): scan-and-fix workflow

- Workflow `anvil-risky-changes-scan-fix` (an all-Fable first attempt was stopped by the user for cost): five Opus finders, one per risky chunk of the 2026-10-13/14 work (sheet metal, Trim, cornerTool, Enter / Esc / Undo, line double-click and unit boxes), read-only, about 5 minutes each; a Fable skeptic per chunk tried to refute each finding; a Fable fixer per chunk fixed the confirmed ones in its own worktree with a regression group that fails on the old code. 18 findings, 17 confirmed, 1 refuted (a closed loop re-opening after a double-click on its start point: not reproducible).
- sheetmetal (rfCJ, kernel): a cut that splits the base face (a window across a channel bottom into both bends) kept only the anchor piece in the flat: every component of the base face is now laid with the base map, flanges and all. `bendSurfaceNotches` treated any gap at a bend end as a corner: now only when a relief or a neighbouring flange whose bend still has metal there owns that end (`cornerAt` / `bendMetal`), so a corner notch cut after the flange leaves no stray bend-strip tab. The Sketched Bend anchor pass used the old 1e-6 side tie-break: now the 10 % rule like the base pass (a pocket on the sketch side flipped UP to DOWN). Known, left: a hole cut from the top face that bites into a bend below the top leaves the bend's inside surface whole, so the strip is not notched there (the test bounds that excess), and a hole reaching a bend line shows the strip's 1e-3 weld overlap (window 119.998 for 120).
- trim (rfCK, kernel + smoke): a slot's / arc slot's `p1` / `p2` (cap-arc centres) were treated as junction ends (cuts at the centre, Coincidents the user never made): skipped via `isSlotCentre`. `trimCuts` dropped crossings at the entity's own ends, so Trim away inside / outside refused an entity ending on a bound: `opts.ends`. A foot found by both the crossing and the end-foot test gave two cuts (a sliver of circle): deduped along the primitive at its tolerance.
- corner tool (rfCL, smoke): specs no longer hold entity indices that a replay shifts: polyline / rect picks store the vertex point, pairs store the pick point + tolerance (`entityAt`), so deleting an Entities to Fillet row re-makes later picks on the right geometry; a refused Radius / Distance blocks the next pick (no mixed sizes at ✓); the first entity of a two-entity pick survives a value change.
- enter-esc-undo (rfCM, smoke): the browser's own text undo in a value box (`historyUndo` / `historyRedo`) no longer leaves the box pending; Edit ▸ Undo / the toolbar undo always cancel an open command (only a keyboard Ctrl+Z in a typed box goes to the text); Circle / Arc / Polygon / Slot tool PMs are sketch tools for undo (Ctrl+Z undoes the last entity, as Line does); an arrow-key spin and the `=` / shop-text paths arm the box (Enter is ✓, Ctrl+Z cancels).
- sketch-input (rfCN, smoke): the line double-click popped the wrong undo snapshots once the 60-step stack was full (the discarded segment came back): `pushed` now follows the dirty flag; the Circle unit box arms after its preview (no third Enter); Ctrl+Z in a unit box with nothing typed cancels the command; re-picking the Parallel Line reference clears the placed preview and the distance box.
- Process: messaging the running workflow fixers to wrap up started a second copy of each in the same worktree (and one copy reverted the enter-esc files once; both re-verified). TaskStop by agent id stops both copies. Next time: put the wrap-up rule in the fixer prompt, or stop the workflow and finish from the worktrees. The head committed each worktree, squash-merged, and finished the sheet-metal lane from its last state.
Tests: kernel 1175/1175, examples 29/29 (both kernels), ui-smoke 731/731, occt 87/87.

All but perforated and shell-fuzz finished inside it (those two ran 1-2 minutes over).

- shell-fuzz (rfGN): `edgeKey` on inch fractions, `ringSolid` retry; stacks 202, 565, 601, 606 close. step-size (rfGO): spline lofts export as B-spline faces, 15-17 MB to 15-37 KB, OpenCascade reads them valid; import added to exchange.js. massprops (rfGP): tori, cones, tees and cross holes bounded at trims, to 3e-9 of independent integrals; the SolidWorks gaps on R12, R14, R15 turned out to be the fillet's shape. Its first version moved the Funnel, rfFP and WHEEL: the head bisected to it and sent it back; the Funnel and rfFP pass, and the WHEEL reference was wrong (41.8282847 against the closed form and OpenCascade 41.828282).

## local-2026-11-02d (local, interactive head; 1 Opus agent): kernel tests in parallel

Context: the user, "make the tests run in parallel from now on". The agent's trial at 12 workers pegged all 16 threads of the user's machine without warning; the head killed that run and capped it. The default is 8 workers, half the threads, so the PC stays usable.
- parallel-tests (anvil/headless/test-run.js, test-timings.json): `node anvil/headless/anvil.js test` splits group files across child processes balanced by recorded timings; 1012 s serial, 216 s on 8 workers, 14 workers no faster (217 s, floored by rfGI / rfGH at about 200 s each). `--only`, `--serial`, `--jobs N`, `--timings`. Peak 5.0 GB. Serial against two parallel runs: 0 differences across 4376 lines. No group pinned serial, none flaky. ui-smoke stays one Chrome.
- Head: the head's claim that the suite's slowdown came from sharing the machine was wrong: it ran 977 s alone. The agent found the cause, and it was a real regression in the published app: lanes perforated and massprops were each green, but on merged code `boundCorrection` scanned every hole rim for every hole (59 s at 800 holes; 3200 holes did not finish in 170 s, where the round had reported 4.2 s). `boundCorrection` now finds neighbours through a sparse grid: 3200 holes 7.4 s from a cold start, exact volume, every suite green; rfGM has a speed row.
- Decisions: a speed claim is measured on merged code before it is reported. Using up the usage window is not a goal.
- Next: QUEUE.md leftovers.
Tests: kernel 2311/2311 in 216 s on 8 workers, plus the new rfGM speed row (rfGM 7/7), examples 29/29 (both kernels), ui-smoke 896/896, occt 176/176, app console not rechecked after the one-function fix.
